==================================================================================================
nest genome :: category syntheses
20 distillations: state of the art + concrete nest recommendations
==================================================================================================

##################################################################################################
# compression-general  -  general-purpose byte compression and entropy coding
##################################################################################################

summary:     nest already vendors zstd but runs its weakest config: every non-embedding section is compressed
             cold at a hardcoded level 19 (crates/nest-format/src/encoding/zstd_codec.rs
             DEFAULT_ZSTD_LEVEL=19, zstd::encode_all with no dictionary, no LDM, no params
             struct), and the encoding choice is preset-driven not measured
             (writer/payload.rs::maybe_zstd is a binary raw-vs-zstd switch). The collective state
             of the art across these 12 repos points to three concrete, high-leverage,
             fully-sovereign-compatible moves for nest's actual payload shape (thousands of short,
             mutually-similar PT-BR chunks plus small structured metadata): (1) TRAINED SHARED
             DICTIONARIES (zstd ZDICT/COVER, echoed by brotli's static dictionary, zlib
             deflateSetDictionary, klauspost, c-blosc2 superchunk dict) -- the single biggest
             unrealized ratio win, attacking the per-section entropy-table + cold-start redundancy
             that level-19-no-dict pays on every blob; deterministic because ZDICT is a pure
             function of sorted samples and content_hash is over DECODED bytes so it stays stable.
             (2) BYTE-SHUFFLE/BYTEDELTA PRE-CODEC (c-blosc2) -- a near-memcpy SIMD transpose in
             front of zstd that turns the near-constant high bytes of nest's u64 span offsets
             (sections/spans.rs) and the contiguous f32 int8-scale array (encoding/int8.rs) into
             long runs, a pure ratio multiplier (Blosc shows 3.9x->5.9x on float arrays) with zero
             new dependency and no format break. (3) COST-DRIVEN ENCODING SELECTION (libdeflate's
             measure-bits-out, klauspost block-type-by-cost) -- trial-encode each section and keep
             the smallest, replacing nest's preset-only choice. The entropy-coder family
             (FSE/tANS, Huff0 from FiniteStateEntropy, klauspost separable primitives, libdeflate
             two-level Huffman table) is the longer-horizon path to a sovereign in-house entropy
             floor below generic zstd, especially over delta-coded BM25 postings (0x08) and HNSW
             adjacency (0x07). snappy/lz4/zstdmt contribute FORMAT discipline rather than ratio:
             skippable/unskippable extension bits and self-describing skippable-frame chunk
             headers that make nest's reserved 0x09+ sections forward-compatible and large
             sections seekable/parallel-decodable for citation reopen. 500xCompressor is a pure
             reference (lossy neural, 8B-LLM at decode time) and the canonical reason semantic
             compression stays OUT of the frozen offline v1. Every recommendation respects the
             hard rules: embeddings stay raw/f16/int8 scored from mmap (shuffle and dict NEVER
             touch the hot float path), encoding ids 4-255 and section ids 0x09+ are additive
             within v1, no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump, no panics (typed thiserror), repr(C)/LE,
             <=300 loc per file.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The field has converged on a clean separation of concerns that maps directly onto nest. (1)
DECORRELATION/MODELING is split from ENTROPY CODING: c-blosc2 proves the cheapest ratio gains come
from a SIMD byte-transform stage (shuffle/bitshuffle/bytedelta) that merely presents
better-conditioned bytes to an unchanged downstream coder; klauspost and zstd factor entropy into
reusable primitives (FSE/tANS + Huff0). (2) tANS (FSE) is the modern entropy floor --
near-arithmetic accuracy at single-LUT-lookup speed, with Huff0 (4 interleaved bitstreams for ILP)
for literals; this is the zstd/klauspost back-end and the libdeflate two-level packed-table +
branchless-refill decoder is its fast deterministic decode form. (3) TRAINED SHARED DICTIONARIES
are the decisive lever for the many-small-similar-records regime that nest IS: zstd ZDICT/COVER,
brotli's 13.5k-word static dictionary + 121 transforms, zlib deflateSetDictionary, and c-blosc2's
superchunk dict all attack cold-start and inter-record redundancy that per-blob compression cannot
touch. (4) FORMAT DISCIPLINE is standardized: self-describing frames with skippable-magic ranges
(zstd 0x184D2A5x, snappy reserved skippable/unskippable partitioning, zstdmt 12-16 byte chunk
headers) give additive forward-compatibility and per-chunk seekability/parallelism, and
resolve-once functable dispatch (zlib-ng) decouples algorithm from microarchitecture. (5) ENCODING
CHOICE is measured, not heuristic: libdeflate iterates LZ parsing on real post-entropy bit cost to
a fixpoint and selects block types by measured size -- 'compress once, serve many' fits nest's
deterministic offline build exactly. At the extreme, 500xCompressor's neural KV-cache compression
marks the boundary nest will NOT cross: lossy, LLM-at-decode, non-deterministic, the antithesis of
offline/single-file/byte-exact. nest sits in the sweet spot to exploit (1)+(3)+(5) immediately on
its non-embedding sections with zero hot-path risk, and to grow (2) into a sovereign in-house
entropy stage over its postings/adjacency residuals.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Trained shared zstd dictionary (ZDICT/COVER greedy frequent-segment selection + pre-baked
   FSE/Huffman tables + seed repeat-offsets): build ONE deterministic dictionary at build time
   over nest's many short similar chunks so each tiny blob compresses against shared cross-record
   statistics instead of re-transmitting its own entropy tables and re-discovering shared strings
   cold. Highest-leverage zstd feature for nest's exact workload; confirmed by brotli static dict,
   zlib deflateSetDictionary, klauspost, c-blosc2.
2. Byte-shuffle + bytedelta pre-codec (c-blosc2): SIMD strided transpose grouping byte-position k
   of every fixed-size element into contiguous planes so near-constant high-order bytes of numeric
   arrays form long runs zstd crushes, optionally deltaed per plane. Cheapest known ratio
   multiplier on top of an existing entropy coder (3.86x->5.86x on Blosc's float32/ERA5),
   near-memcpy speed, build-time only.
3. Cost-driven encoding selection (libdeflate iterate-on-measured-bits; klauspost/zlib
   block-type-by-cost): pick the section encoding by actual measured output size, not a static
   preset rule. One-pass analog: trial-encode each non-embedding section under candidate
   codecs/params and keep the smallest; the chosen encoding id is just recorded per-section in the
   manifest, fully additive.
4. FSE/tANS table-based ANS entropy coder (Cyan4973/FiniteStateEntropy, klauspost fse, zstd
   back-end): single state-transition LUT lookup per symbol hitting within a fraction of a percent
   of Shannon entropy on skewed streams where Huffman loses up to ~1 bit/symbol. The sovereign
   in-house entropy floor for delta-coded BM25 postings, HNSW neighbor lists, and term
   dictionaries, dependency-free and deterministic (table build is a pure function of the
   histogram).
5. Huff0 + libdeflate two-level Huffman decode table with packed-u32 entries and branchless 64-bit
   bit-buffer refill: a tiny, fast, deterministic, no-C-dependency entropy decoder that runs
   straight off mmap (one table read + shift per symbol, 4 interleaved bitstreams for ILP). The
   reference design for any future nest-native entropy section decoder under the no-panic / LE /
   mmap constraints.
6. LZ77 repeat-offset cache + LDM (zstd): 3-most-recent-offset cache so periodic/structured data
   spends ~1 offset code, and long-distance matching over a large window (up to 2GB) catching
   far-apart duplicates -- the engine extension that scales nest toward the north-star whole-repo
   / whole-database / parquet payloads where cross-file duplication dominates.
7. Skippable/unskippable extension discipline + self-describing skippable-frame chunk header
   (snappy reserved-id partitioning, zstd 0x184D2A5x magics, zstdmt 12-16 byte per-chunk header
   with compressed+uncompressed size hints): the canonical way an additive format lets old readers
   skip unknown OPTIONAL extensions while erroring cleanly on unknown REQUIRED ones, and the
   blueprint for seekable per-chunk random-access decode (citation reopen) + exact output-buffer
   pre-allocation without inflating a whole section.
8. Pure-Rust byte-aligned LZ77 fallback codec (snappy tag-byte stream / lz4 token+LSIC varint): an
   entropy-coder-free, allocation-light, GB/s-class block format whose decoder is table-free and
   branch-light. Two uses for nest: a zero-C-dependency sovereign fallback when everything is
   vendored into one binary, and the only realistic path to compressing surfaces zstd is banned
   from (per-citation text materialize, a future just-in-time lz4 block over int8 embeddings
   decoded into scratch before the SIMD kernel).
9. Functable dispatch pattern (zlib-ng resolve-once struct of fn pointers; CRC32-C via carryless
   multiply): generalize nest's existing SimdBackend OnceLock in nest-runtime/src/simd/mod.rs into
   a small functable so new kernels (f16 convert, i8 widen, shuffle/un-delta, future bitpacked
   decoder) register one entry each instead of growing match arms, keeping each simd/*.rs under
   the 300-line cap; CRC32-C as a fast non-crypto hash for dedup/identity probes that precede
   compression.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add a trained shared-dictionary zstd codec. New encoding/zstd_dict.rs exposing
  zstd_encode_with_dict / zstd_decode_with_dict (zstd crate's dictionary API), a new
  SECTION_ENCODING_ZSTD_DICT id (next free in reserved 4-255, additive within v1), and a new
  additive section id 0x09 (SECTION_DICTIONARY) that stores the single dictionary blob. Train ONE
  deterministic dictionary in nest-python at build time over chunks_canonical (and optionally
  spans/provenance/bm25 terms), feeding samples in canonical/alphabetical order so ZDICT is a pure
  function (deterministic + reproducible). Compress every text/json section against it.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/zstd_dict.rs (new, <300 loc) + register id in layout/mod.rs
            (SECTION_ENCODING_ZSTD_DICT, SECTION_DICTIONARY=0x09 in OPTIONAL_SECTIONS) + wire
            decode arm in encoding/mod.rs::decode_payload + emit in writer/payload.rs::maybe_zstd
            / encoding_choice.rs; training entry on the python build_fn side
    impact: Highest single ratio win for nest's actual payload shape (thousands of short,
            vocabulary-overlapping PT-BR chunks). Should push the 'tiny' preset's
            non-embedding/text bytes meaningfully below the current 0.283 overall ratio.
            content_hash stays stable (it hashes DECODED canonical bytes; dict affects physical
            encoding only), section checksum still hashes physical bytes, embeddings untouched.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/facebook/zstd, https://github.com/google/brotli,
            https://github.com/madler/zlib, https://github.com/klauspost/compress,
            https://github.com/Blosc/c-blosc2

* Insert a byte-shuffle (+ optional bytedelta) pre-codec in front of zstd as a new additive
  encoding id (shuffle+zstd). Strided transpose of section bytes by a typesize recorded in the
  manifest, applied ONLY to cold numeric sections, reversed in decode_payload before zstd_decode.
  Targets: chunks_original_spans (u64 byte_start/byte_end whose high bytes are near-constant ->
  long runs) and the int8 per-vector scale array (n contiguous f32 LE scales, the classic shuffle
  case).
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/shuffle.rs + bytedelta.rs (new, pure deterministic, no-panic, each
            <300 loc) + new SECTION_ENCODING_SHUF_ZSTD id in layout/mod.rs + decode arm in
            encoding/mod.rs + gate strictly to non-hot-path sections in writer/encoding_choice.rs;
            typesize stored in manifest per section
    impact: Pure ratio multiplier on structured numeric payloads with zero new dependency and no format break
            (Blosc shows 3.9x->5.9x on float arrays). CRITICAL: must NOT touch the embeddings body
            (shuffle destroys the row-contiguous layout the AVX2/NEON dot-product reads from mmap)
            -- gate by section id. Build-time + cold-decode cost only.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/Blosc/c-blosc2

* Make encoding selection cost-driven instead of preset-driven. For each non-embedding section,
  trial-encode under the candidate codecs (raw / zstd / zstd+dict / shuffle+zstd) and keep the
  smallest measured output; record the chosen encoding id per-section in the manifest. One-pass
  analog of libdeflate's block-type-by-measured-cost.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/writer/encoding_choice.rs (turn the SectionEncoding enum from a fixed
            preset into a chooser) + writer/payload.rs::maybe_zstd generalized to try-all-pick-min
    impact: Guarantees each section gets its best available encoding without hand-tuning per preset; never
            regresses (raw is always a candidate). Fully additive, deterministic (smallest size is
            a total order, ties broken by lowest encoding id). Modest absolute bytes but compounds
            with the dict and shuffle wins.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/ebiggers/libdeflate, https://github.com/klauspost/compress,
            https://github.com/madler/zlib

* Add ZstdParams knobs (level + enable_long_distance_matching + window_log) replacing the
  hardcoded level-19. Set per preset: tiny -> level 22 + LDM for large whole-database/whole-repo
  payloads; keep current behavior for small sections. LDM catches far-apart cross-file duplication
  the normal match-finder misses.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/zstd_codec.rs (ZstdParams struct, replace encode_all(bytes,19)
            with the param path via the zstd crate's compressor) + threaded through
            writer/encoding_choice.rs presets
    impact: Directly serves the north-star whole-repo/whole-database/parquet payloads where LDM + max level
            dominate; cheap one-time build cost, decode speed roughly level-independent. No format
            change (still SECTION_ENCODING_ZSTD bytes).
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/facebook/zstd

* Vendor a pure-Rust FSE/tANS + Huff0 entropy codec for skewed integer/token streams, registered
  as new additive encoding ids, applied to delta-coded BM25 postings (0x08) and HNSW neighbor
  lists (0x07) and term dictionaries -- never to embeddings. Share the
  spread/normalize/table-build core between encode and decode; FSEU16 variant for 16-bit tokenized
  terms. Use libdeflate's two-level decode table + packed-u32 entries + branchless 64-bit refill
  for a tiny mmap-friendly decoder.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/fse.rs + huff0.rs (new, <300 loc each, shared table core) + new
            SECTION_ENCODING_FSE id in layout/mod.rs + decode arm in encoding/mod.rs; encode-side
            delta + entropy in nest-runtime/src/bm25/codec.rs and ann/codec.rs
    impact: A sovereign in-house entropy floor below generic zstd on skewed residuals (tANS within a fraction
            of a percent of Shannon entropy where Huffman loses up to ~1 bit/symbol). Table build
            is a pure function of the histogram -> byte-identical/deterministic, content_hash and
            reproducible builds preserved. Larger surface, so lower priority than dict+shuffle.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/Cyan4973/FiniteStateEntropy, https://github.com/klauspost/compress,
            https://github.com/ebiggers/libdeflate, https://github.com/facebook/zstd

* Formalize a skippable-optional vs unskippable-required bit on section entries so a future v1
  file with an unknown OPTIONAL section (e.g. a new graph index, audio/video payload) still
  validates and serves on an older runtime, while unknown REQUIRED sections error cleanly via
  NestError -- extending the additive guarantee without a v2 bump. Mirror zstd's 0x184D2A5x
  skippable-magic pattern and snappy's reserved-range partitioning.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/layout/section_entry.rs (a flags field on SectionEntry) + reader/parse.rs +
            reader/validate.rs (skip-or-error logic) + manifest capabilities
    impact: Protects the additive-only guarantee as the format grows toward repos/audio/video/parquet; an
            older binary keeps working against newer optional extensions. Pure format-hardening,
            no ratio impact, but load-bearing for the north-star expansion of payload types.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/google/snappy, https://github.com/facebook/zstd,
            https://github.com/mcmilk/zstdmt

* Chunk large compressed sections into independently-decodable sub-blocks with a self-describing
  12-16 byte header (skippable-frame style: magic + compressed-size + uncompressed-size hint), so
  a single chunk decodes for nest://content_hash/chunk_id citation reopen without inflating the
  whole section, materialize() pre-allocates exactly (no realloc), and large text sections can
  decode in parallel via rayon. Pair with the shared dictionary so small-chunk random access does
  not cost ratio.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ (sub-block framing inside the existing zstd/dict encoding id, no
            new section id, no version bump) + nest-runtime/src/materialize.rs (per-chunk decode +
            parallel path)
    impact: Unlocks O(1) citation reopen and parallel materialize at scale without breaking
            single-file/offline/deterministic (fixed chunk size + fixed params -> deterministic
            byte layout -> stable per-section physical SHA-256). Slight ratio cost per chunk
            window-reset, recovered by the shared dict. Use rayon/crossbeam, NOT zstdmt's
            two-mutex pool.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/mcmilk/zstdmt, https://github.com/google/snappy,
            https://github.com/klauspost/compress

* Add a GB/s-class byte-aligned LZ77 codec (lz4/snappy block format: token + LSIC/varint length
  extension + 2-byte LE offset, wildcopy decode) as a new additive encoding id, as the first
  compressor nest can legally apply to hot-path-adjacent surfaces zstd is banned from:
  per-citation text materialize (always-compressed text without per-query latency) and, longer
  term, an lz4-framed transparent layer over int8 embeddings decoded into a small scratch buffer
  just-in-time before the SIMD dot product. Also reuse the LSIC add-255 varint for nest's own
  section-length/manifest varints.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/lz4_block.rs (new, <300 loc, panic-free) + new
            SECTION_ENCODING_LZ4 id in layout/mod.rs + decode in encoding/mod.rs; just-in-time
            embeddings decode path would sit in nest-runtime/src/search.rs scratch buffer before
            simd/mod.rs::score_int8_section
    impact: Does NOT beat zstd on cold ratio (lz4 loses), so not the headline lever -- its value is widening
            the SET of compressible bytes to surfaces with a tight decode budget, and a
            zero-C-dependency sovereign fallback for one-binary vendoring. Embeddings-over-lz4 is
            speculative and must prove it does not regress the AVX2/NEON kernel latency.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/lz4/lz4, https://github.com/google/snappy

* Generalize the SimdBackend OnceLock into a functable-style struct of fn pointers (resolve-once,
  indirect-call-forever) so new kernels register one entry each, and add CRC32-C via carryless
  multiply (PCLMULQDQ on x86_64, PMULL on aarch64) behind it as a fast NON-crypto hash for dedup
  keys / section identity probes that precede compression. The four SHA-256 surfaces stay
  cryptographic and unchanged.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/mod.rs (functable struct replacing the BACKEND OnceLock + per-fn
            match arms) + a new simd/crc32c.rs kernel; keep each simd/*.rs under 300 loc as
            kernels multiply (shuffle/un-delta, f16 convert, i8 widen all become entries)
    impact: Engineering scaffolding only (no ratio win): keeps the SIMD layer under the file-size cap as
            shuffle/un-delta/entropy kernels arrive, and speeds dedup/identity work before
            compression. CRC-as-hash must never be confused with the SHA-256 integrity contract.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/zlib-ng/zlib-ng, https://github.com/facebook/zstd

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Trained shared zstd dictionary over chunks_canonical (and spans/provenance/bm25 terms) -- the
    single biggest unrealized win for nest's thousands-of-short-similar-chunks shape; kills
    per-section entropy-table + cold-start redundancy that level-19-no-dict pays on every blob;
    expected to push the tiny preset's text bytes below today's 0.283 ratio while keeping
    content_hash stable.
  - Byte-shuffle + bytedelta in front of zstd on chunks_original_spans (near-constant high bytes
    of u64 offsets -> long runs) and the int8 scale array (contiguous f32 LE) -- pure multiplier
    on structured numeric sections, zero new dependency, Blosc-measured 3.9x->5.9x headroom on
    float arrays.
  - Cost-driven per-section encoding selection (trial-encode, keep smallest measured output) --
    guarantees each section's best available codec/params without hand-tuning, never regresses
    since raw is always a candidate, deterministic by smallest-size total order.
  - zstd level 22 + long-distance-matching for the north-star large payloads (whole-repo /
    whole-database / parquet) -- LDM catches far-apart cross-file duplication the 64KB-window
    match-finder misses, at one-time build cost with level-independent decode speed.
  - In-house FSE/tANS over delta-coded BM25 postings (0x08), HNSW adjacency (0x07), and term
    dictionaries -- within a fraction of a percent of Shannon entropy on skewed residuals where
    Huffman loses up to ~1 bit/symbol and where zstd's generic stage carries unnecessary
    container/table overhead; a sovereign entropy floor below generic zstd.
  - Repeat-offset cache awareness for periodic/structured metadata (chunk_ids, spans deltas) --
    structured data spends ~1 offset code instead of a full offset, compounding with shuffle on
    the same arrays.
  - Shared-dictionary + per-chunk framing together -- recover the small-chunk ratio loss from
    seekable chunking so citation-reopen random access costs no ratio.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - NEVER apply shuffle/bytedelta/dictionary/zstd to the embeddings BODY scored from mmap on the
    hot path. Shuffle destroys the row-contiguous layout AVX2/NEON dot-products read; any
    entropy/dict stage breaks 'scored directly from mmap'. Gate every transform strictly by
    section id in writer/encoding_choice.rs. Embeddings stay raw/f16/int8 forever in v1. (Only the
    int8 SCALE array -- not the i8 bodies -- is a safe shuffle target.)
  - Dictionary training must be a pure deterministic function: feed ZDICT samples in
    canonical/alphabetical order and seed any randomized step, or reproducible builds (same chunks
    + same model fingerprint => byte-identical file) break. Same for FSE/tANS: table build must be
    a pure function of the histogram. Verify byte-identical output across two builds before
    shipping.
  - content_hash is over DECODED canonical bytes and section checksums are over PHYSICAL bytes --
    confirm a dict-compressed or shuffled section yields the SAME content_hash as raw (it does
    only if decode is exact and lossless). bytedelta/shuffle must round-trip bit-exactly;
    trunc_prec (lossy mantissa zeroing) is explicitly OUT of any required/content-hashed section.
  - All new codecs are additive within v1: encoding ids in reserved 4-255, section ids in reserved
    0x09+, and do NOT bump NEST_FORMAT_VERSION (that is only for a true v2 where an existing field
    changes meaning). Adding the dictionary section must not enter content_hash (keep it OPTIONAL,
    hash over the canonical six only) so citations survive.
  - Do NOT add a DEFLATE/zlib/zlib-ng codec or a Snappy/lz4 codec to beat zstd on cold ratio --
    they all LOSE to zstd-19 on text. Their value is structural (fallback, hot-path-adjacent
    surfaces, format discipline), not headline ratio. lz4-over-int8-embeddings is speculative and
    must prove no AVX2/NEON latency regression before it ships.
  - 500xCompressor and any neural/semantic compression is incompatible with the frozen offline v1:
    it needs an 8B-LLM forward pass at decode time, breaks determinism (float GPU kernels),
    offline/single-file/small-core, and cannot losslessly reconstruct bytes for
    nest://content_hash/chunk_id reopen. Keep it OUT; cite it only as the reason semantic
    compression is excluded.
  - No panics in library code -- every new codec (shuffle, bytedelta, fse, huff0, lz4_block,
    zstd_dict) returns typed thiserror NestError on malformed/oversized input; reject unknown
    encoding ids cleanly (decode_payload already does for unknown ids). lz4 wildcopy needs output
    over-allocation slack -- handle bounds without UB.
  - File hygiene: <=300 lines per rust source file (333 hard cap), single-responsibility. Split
    shuffle/bytedelta/fse/huff0/lz4 into separate modules and share table/spread cores between
    encode and decode to stay under the cap as the encoding/ directory grows from 4 files to many.
  - zstdmt's two-mutex worker pool is the WRONG concurrency model for Rust -- steal only the
    on-disk skippable-frame chunk format, implement parallel decode with rayon/crossbeam. CRC32-C
    as a hash is non-cryptographic and must never be conflated with the four SHA-256 integrity
    surfaces.
  - Per-chunk framing and multithreaded chunking slightly HURT ratio (each chunk resets the codec
    window/dictionary). Only chunk LARGE sections, and always pair chunking with the shared
    trained dictionary so small-chunk random access stops costing ratio; keep small metadata
    sections as single frames.
  - Runtime is offline by construction -- none of these codecs may open a socket or fetch
    anything; dictionaries live INSIDE the container, trained at build time in python, decoded
    from mmap in rust. All zstd/dict/shuffle cost is amortized one-time at build/cold-decode and
    must stay off the SIMD scoring hot path.


##################################################################################################
# compression-integer  -  integer and posting-list compression (bitpacking, varint, elias-fano)
##################################################################################################

summary:     The category collectively defines the modern integer-compression playbook nest is missing
             entirely: transform the data (delta/d1 for sorted, frame-of-reference subtract-min,
             zigzag for signed) then pack residuals at a per-block minimal bit-width, with two
             escape hatches for the realistic non-uniform cases - patched exceptions
             (PFOR/FastPFor/TurboPFor) for a few large outliers, and locally re-based partitions
             (partitioned Elias-Fano) for clustered/bursty sequences. The decisive property for
             nest is that several of these (FOR/libfor select(), VByte select()/lower_bound,
             Elias-Fano broadword rank/select) support O(1) random access and nextGEQ DIRECTLY on
             the compressed bytes, which zstd structurally cannot - so they compress AND keep
             nest's mmap-and-score-off-disk discipline, unlike zstd which forces a full block
             inflate. nest today has the single largest uncompressed integer surface sitting in
             plain sight: ann/codec.rs writes every HNSW neighbor id as 4 raw LE bytes (line
             29-31), bm25/codec.rs writes every posting as doc:u32+tf:u32 raw (line 33-36), and
             spans.rs stores 16 raw bytes per chunk as two u64 (line 22-23). All three are sorted
             or near-monotone u32/u64 arrays - the textbook delta+bitpack/FOR workload - and all
             three are version-gated by per-section payload versions
             (HNSW_PAYLOAD_VERSION/BM25_PAYLOAD_VERSION) or could carry a new reserved encoding id
             (4-255 free), so the win is fully additive with NEST_FORMAT_VERSION frozen at 1.
             Determinism is free: packing is a pure function of the integers and content_hash
             hashes DECODED values, so byte-identical builds and stable content_hash are
             preserved. The pure-scalar ports are small and fit the 300-line single-responsibility
             cap; SIMD is a later throughput upgrade behind the existing simd dispatcher and is
             unnecessary for correctness since index sections are cold-loaded once at mmap time.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
State of the art is a layered pipeline: (1) a reversible integer transform chosen by data shape -
delta/d1 (gaps) for sorted lists, frame-of-reference (subtract per-block min) for clustered-range
values, zigzag for signed, delta-of-delta+RLE for timestamps; (2) fixed-block binary bit-packing
at a per-block bit-width b = ceil(log2(max_residual+1)), giving a 32/b ratio that decodes
branch-free with shift+mask; (3) an exception mechanism so a handful of outliers do not force a
wide b for the whole block - either patched-FOR (FastPFor/TurboPFor: store outlier positions +
high-bit residuals out-of-band, 128- or 256-int blocks, 2-bit block-type header selecting
constant/bitpack/bitmap-exc/vbyte-exc) or locally re-based partitions (partitioned Elias-Fano: a
linear-time (1+epsilon) DP cuts the sequence into variable chunks, each encoded as the cheapest of
strict-EF / all-ones-run / raw bitvector against its own small universe). The frontier
additionally insists compression must not cost queryability: libfor's for_select_bits() is O(1)
random access by index*b bit-offset, vbyte_select/lower_bound walk a compressed varint stream
without materializing it, and Elias-Fano gives O(1) select and O(log) nextGEQ via broadword
popcount + in-word select tables on 64-bit words (no SIMD needed). SIMD (SIMD-BP128,
TurboPackV256, MaskedVByte, simdcomp) pushes 10-20 Gint/s via vertical lane-transposed layout, but
the scalar layouts are correct and deterministic on their own. Permissive ports exist (FastPFor
Apache-2.0 minus the patented varint-G8IU, simdcomp BSD, quickwit-oss/bitpacking MIT,
libfor/libvbyte Apache-2.0); TurboPFor is GPL-2.0 and must be clean-room reimplemented from the
format description only.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Delta(d1)+bitpack for sorted id lists: gaps in a monotone u32 list pack to ceil(log2(max_gap))
   bits not 32, the single highest-leverage transform for nest because HNSW neighbor ids (sortable
   per-list) and BM25 doc ids (already strictly increasing) are exactly this shape. Source:
   FastPFor SIMD-BP128, simdcomp simdpackd1, libvbyte sorted variants.
2. Frame-of-reference (subtract per-block min, store base+bit-width, pack residuals) with O(1)
   random access via index*b bit-offset arithmetic - the canonical first stage that ALSO keeps
   elements individually addressable off mmap, so spans[i] or neighbor[i] is one bit-extract with
   no whole-section decode. Source: libfor for_select_bits().
3. Patched frame-of-reference / PFOR exceptions: pick b so MOST values fit, store the rare
   outliers out-of-band, so one giant gap or degree spike does not inflate the whole block's
   bit-width. Decodes branch-free. Source: FastPFor/SIMD-FastPFor, TurboPFor 2-bit block-type
   header.
4. Partitioned Elias-Fano: locally re-based variable-length partitions chosen by a deterministic
   (1+epsilon) DP, each chunk the cheapest of EF/all-ones/raw-bitvector, giving 10-30% below plain
   EF on clustered data PLUS O(1) select and O(log) nextGEQ via broadword rank/select - random
   access without any decompression. Source: ot/partitioned_elias_fano.
5. Per-chunk/per-section cheapest-of-N codec selection with a branch-on-tag decode: pick the
   smallest of {raw, delta, FOR, patched, EF, all-ones} at build time, store a tiny tag, decode
   deterministically. A reusable codec-selection pattern that mirrors nest's existing
   encoding-choice axis. Source: partitioned_elias_fano level-2 selector, TurboPFor 2-bit header.
6. Random-access/search-over-compressed primitives (select, lower_bound/nextGEQ) that operate on
   the compressed stream - the property that lets a codec replace zstd on nest's mmap hot path
   instead of forcing a full inflate. Source: libfor, libvbyte, Elias-Fano broadword select.
7. Space-efficient byte-range varint denser than LEB128 (0-176 in 1 byte, then 14/19/32-bit tiers)
   and StreamVByte's control-bytes-separate-from-payload layout for branch-light scalar decode.
   Source: TurboPFor varbyte, FastPFor StreamVByte.
8. Vertical/lane-transposed bit-packing (block of 128 for SSE/NEON, 256 for AVX2, all sharing one
   b) as the SIMD throughput layer that maps cleanly onto nest's existing AVX2/NEON/scalar simd
   dispatcher when/if throughput matters. Source: simdcomp BitPacker4x/8x, TurboPackV128/V256.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add a pure-safe-Rust scalar bitpack+delta(d1) codec module and apply it to HNSW neighbor-id
  lists. Sort each per-layer neighbor list at build time, delta-encode (d1), and pack residuals at
  per-list/per-block bit-width b=ceil(log2(max_gap+1)); store b as one byte. Replace the raw
  4-byte-per-id loop. Bump HNSW_PAYLOAD_VERSION to 2 (NOT NEST_FORMAT_VERSION); decode reproduces
  identical id sets so search and the mandatory exact-cosine rerank are byte-for-byte unchanged.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/ann/codec.rs (replace the raw `for &id in nbrs {
            out.extend_from_slice(&id.to_le_bytes()) }` at lines 29-31 and its mirror parse loop
            at lines 65-71); new shared codec in a new crates/nest-runtime/src/bitpack.rs (<=300
            loc, single-responsibility). HNSW_PAYLOAD_VERSION lives at
            crates/nest-runtime/src/ann/mod.rs:40.
    impact: Largest single shrink of the index section that dominates the tiny preset (0.283 ratio). Neighbor
            ids drop from 32 bits to roughly log2(n) bits (e.g. ~14-17 bits at 100k-1M nodes),
            often <50% of the raw adjacency bytes; pushes tiny/hybrid preset size down with zero
            recall change.
    effort: medium
    from:   lemire/FastPFor, fast-pack/simdcomp, cruppstahl/libfor

* Apply the same delta+bitpack codec to BM25 posting lists: doc ids per term are already strictly
  increasing (deterministic alphabetical term order), so delta-code doc gaps and bitpack them, and
  bitpack tf separately at its own tiny bit-width (tf is small). Bump BM25_PAYLOAD_VERSION to 2.
  Keep df and the alphabetical term order untouched so determinism holds.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/codec.rs (replace the raw `p.doc.to_le_bytes()` +
            `p.tf.to_le_bytes()` posting loop at lines 33-36 and the matching parse at lines
            73-77); reuse the bitpack.rs module from rec #1. BM25_PAYLOAD_VERSION at
            crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/index.rs:8.
    impact: Sorted doc-id gaps routinely pack to 4-8 bits vs 32; tf to ~3-4 bits. Several-fold shrink of
            section 0x08 in the hybrid preset, beating zstd-on-raw-u32 in both ratio and (cold,
            one-time) decode cost, with no scoring change.
    effort: medium
    from:   lemire/FastPFor, fast-pack/simdcomp, cruppstahl/libvbyte

* Re-encode chunks_original_spans as delta-coded varint streams grouped by source_uri. Within one
  source the byte_start/byte_end offsets are monotonically increasing; store each source's offsets
  as delta+varint (or FOR) instead of two raw u64 (16 bytes/chunk). Register a NEW reserved
  encoding id (e.g. SECTION_ENCODING_BITPACK = 4) so old readers reject it cleanly and
  NEST_FORMAT_VERSION stays 1; content_hash is unchanged because it hashes decoded canonical
  OriginalSpan values.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/spans.rs (lines 22-23 emit the raw u64 pair today); add codec
            under crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ and wire the new id into decode_payload in
            crates/nest-format/src/encoding/mod.rs:44 and into SectionEncoding in
            crates/nest-format/src/writer/encoding_choice.rs.
    impact: Cuts a REQUIRED non-embedding section from ~16 bytes/chunk toward ~2-4 bytes for close-packed
            chunks - structurally beats even zstd-on-raw because it exploits monotonicity zstd
            cannot see; shrinks every preset including exact.
    effort: medium
    from:   cruppstahl/libvbyte, cruppstahl/libfor, powturbo/TurboPFor-Integer-Compression

* Make the new integer codec FOR-based with index*b bit-offset random access (libfor select
  semantics) so spans[i] and one neighbor id can be fetched by O(1) bit-extract straight off mmap,
  never decoding the whole section. This preserves nest's zero-copy hot path for citation
  resolution (nest://content_hash/chunk_id materialize) and keeps the option of
  search-over-compressed for bm25 nextGEQ later.
    where:  the codec module shared by recs #1-#3 (crates/nest-runtime/src/bitpack.rs and/or
            crates/nest-format/src/encoding/); a tiny succinct rank/select primitive file kept
            separate (<=300 loc each) if Elias-Fano is added later. Wire select() into the
            materialize / citation path in nest-runtime.
    impact: Keeps compression from costing queryability - the property that justifies these codecs over zstd
            on the mmap-and-score discipline. Enables shrinking spans/index while still serving
            span[i] zero-copy.
    effort: high
    from:   cruppstahl/libfor, cruppstahl/libvbyte, ot/partitioned_elias_fano

* Add patched exceptions (PFOR) once plain bitpack lands: pick b for the common case and store the
  few outliers out-of-band (position + high bits), with a 2-bit block-type header selecting
  constant/plain-bitpack/patched. This stops one huge gap (a sparse posting list term) or one
  high-degree HNSW node from widening b for the whole block. Clean-room from the
  FastPFor/TurboPFor format description only.
    where:  extend crates/nest-runtime/src/bitpack.rs with an exceptions sub-path (separate file if it nears
            300 loc); applies transparently to recs #1 and #2.
    impact: 10-25% further shrink on real (bursty) distributions vs plain per-block bitpack; the difference
            between good and state-of-the-art on clustered posting lists and skewed adjacency.
    effort: high
    from:   lemire/FastPFor, powturbo/TurboPFor-Integer-Compression

* Adopt a cheapest-of-N per-section codec selector with a branch-on-tag decode: at build time try
  {raw, delta+bitpack, FOR, patched, all-ones} on each integer section/list, store the 1-byte
  winning tag, and pick the smallest. Mirror nest's existing SectionEncoding enum so the choice is
  explicit and the DP/selector is a deterministic pure function (byte-identical builds preserved).
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/writer/encoding_choice.rs (extend SectionEncoding beyond Raw/Zstd) and the
            dispatch in crates/nest-format/src/encoding/mod.rs:44; tag read at decode time.
    impact: Guarantees the codec never loses to raw/zstd on adversarial inputs and auto-adapts per dataset
            (e.g. PT-BR fake-news corpora) without manual tuning - turns compression into a
            build-time optimization rather than a fixed guess.
    effort: medium
    from:   ot/partitioned_elias_fano, powturbo/TurboPFor-Integer-Compression

* Replace LEB128-style varints anywhere nest emits length prefixes / counts for integer arrays
  with the denser byte-range varint (0-176 in 1 byte, 14/19/32-bit tiers) or StreamVByte's
  controls-separate-from-payload layout, decoded with a branch-light scalar loop.
    where:  the varint path in crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ and the count/df prefixes in bm25/codec.rs and
            ann/codec.rs headers; keep all-LE per nest invariant.
    impact: Small but free uniform shrink on every count/length field; complements the bitpack codec for the
            non-block scalar fields.
    effort: low
    from:   powturbo/TurboPFor-Integer-Compression, lemire/FastPFor

* Later: add NEON(128)/AVX2(256) vertical bit-pack kernels behind the existing simd dispatcher for
  decode throughput, keeping the scalar path as the deterministic reference. The on-disk bit
  layout must be identical across scalar/SIMD so content_hash and decode are independent of CPU.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/simd (mirror the AVX2/NEON/scalar dispatch already used for dot products);
            SIMD touches DECODE only, never the on-disk layout produced by the python builder.
    impact: Throughput only (10-20 Gint/s vs scalar). Low priority because index sections are cold-loaded once
            at mmap time, but useful if posting-list scan-over-compressed becomes hot.
    effort: high
    from:   fast-pack/simdcomp, lemire/FastPFor, powturbo/TurboPFor-Integer-Compression

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - HNSW neighbor ids (ann/codec.rs:29-31): sort-per-list + delta(d1) + bitpack drops each id from
    32 bits to ~log2(n) bits (~14-17 bits at 100k-1M nodes) plus exceptions for high-degree nodes
    - the single biggest lever on the tiny-preset index section, no recall change since decode
    reproduces identical ids and the exact-cosine rerank is untouched.
  - BM25 doc ids (bm25/codec.rs:33-36): already strictly increasing, so delta-gap + bitpack packs
    to a typical 4-8 bits/id and tf to ~3-4 bits, several-fold over raw doc:u32+tf:u32 and below
    zstd-on-raw, shrinking section 0x08 in the hybrid preset.
  - chunks_original_spans (spans.rs:22-23): delta+varint/FOR per source_uri cuts a REQUIRED
    section from 16 bytes/chunk toward 2-4 bytes for close-packed chunks - a win in EVERY preset
    including exact, exploiting monotonicity zstd cannot.
  - These codecs beat zstd structurally on sorted integer columns AND keep O(1) random access
    (FOR/EF select), so they compress without breaking the mmap-and-score-off-disk hot path - zstd
    would force a full block inflate and forfeit zero-copy.
  - Cheapest-of-N build-time selection guarantees the integer sections are never larger than raw
    and auto-adapt per corpus, compounding toward pushing the tiny preset below its current 0.283
    ratio.
  - All wins are lossless on the integer payload and stack on top of existing zstd/f16/int8 - they
    attack the metadata/index surface (everything except the f32 embeddings that must stay raw for
    scoring), the part nest leaves uncompressed today.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - GPL-2.0 contamination: TurboPFor is GPL-2.0 (dual-licensed). Never vendor or copy - clean-room
    reimplement from the format description only. Prefer the permissive sources (FastPFor
    Apache-2.0, simdcomp BSD, quickwit-oss/bitpacking MIT, libfor/libvbyte Apache-2.0) for
    reference. Avoid FastPFor's varint-G8IU specifically (patented).
  - Determinism: bit-width selection, exception ordering, and any partition DP must be a pure
    deterministic function of the integers (fixed block size, canonical b=max-residual-bits,
    exceptions in ascending index order, fixed epsilon). Any data-dependent or
    floating-point-influenced choice in the encoder would break byte-identical reproducible
    builds. Note HNSW neighbor lists must be sorted in a canonical, seeded-deterministic order
    before delta-coding or the on-disk bytes will vary.
  - content_hash stability: content_hash hashes DECODED canonical sections, so a new INTEGER codec
    on chunks_original_spans is safe (decode yields identical OriginalSpan values). But the codec
    MUST decode to byte-identical canonical integers - any lossy or reordering transform would
    change content_hash and break nest:// citations.
  - Format-freeze discipline: index codecs (HNSW 0x07, BM25 0x08) must bump only their per-section
    PAYLOAD_VERSION (ann/mod.rs:40, bm25/index.rs:8), never NEST_FORMAT_VERSION. A new section
    encoding id for spans must use the reserved 4-255 range and old readers must reject it via the
    existing UnsupportedSectionEncoding path - do NOT change the meaning of an existing field
    (that would be a true v2).
  - Never-panic constraint: bit-cursor unpack, varint continuation, FOR base/width reads, and
    broadword select on malformed/truncated bytes must return typed thiserror errors
    (RuntimeError::Format / NestError::MalformedSectionPayload), never panic or index out of
    bounds. The hand-tuned C kernels assume well-formed input; the Rust port must bounds-check
    every word read.
  - Zero-copy vs decode-into-Vec: zstd already breaks zero-copy by decompressing whole blocks. To
    keep the mmap hot path, prefer FOR/EF layouts that support select() straight off mmap rather
    than codecs that force a full decode into an owned Vec. libfor's bits<=32 cap means 64-bit
    span offsets need a 64-bit path or delta-into-u32 - handle u64 spans explicitly.
  - 300-line file cap: split into single-responsibility modules - block header / bitpack-unpack /
    exception list / delta transform / succinct rank-select each as its own file under the
    300-line (333 hard) cap. Do not let a monolithic codec file grow past it.
  - SIMD is a throughput-only later step and must NOT alter the on-disk bit layout: the python
    builder produces the bytes scalar-deterministically, and AVX2/NEON/scalar decode in
    nest-runtime must all yield identical integers. Vertical lane layout differs between 128-bit
    (NEON, 4x u32) and 256-bit (AVX2, 8x u32) - if SIMD is added, the on-disk format must commit
    to ONE block size (256) and let NEON decode it as two 128-bit halves, or builds become
    CPU-dependent.
  - CJK/Thai BM25 tokenizer gap is orthogonal - posting-list compression does not fix the
    word-segmentation degradation; don't conflate the two.
  - Keep embeddings out of scope: these codecs are for integer sections only. The f32 embeddings
    are scored directly from mmap on the hot path and must stay raw/f16/int8 as today - never
    apply integer bitpacking to embeddings.


##################################################################################################
# hashing-crypto  -  hashing, checksums, content addressing, authenticated encryption
##################################################################################################

summary:     nest already lives in exactly the design space these five repos exemplify: domain-separated,
             length-prefixed preimages (chunk.rs, content_hash_hex), four SHA-256 integrity
             surfaces, and a deterministic SIMD-dispatch discipline (simd/mod.rs OnceLock + scalar
             fallback) that mirrors crc32c/gxhash/RustCrypto runtime detection. The collective
             state of the art has moved past "flat hash over the whole body" in three directions
             that map directly onto nest's roadmap. (1) Merkle-tree-over-fixed-chunks hashing
             (BLAKE3, RustCrypto blake3, bao) turns one stable root commitment into cheap
             partial/incremental verification of any sub-range, plus 5-10x throughput via
             independent-chunk SIMD+rayon -- the exact decoupling nest has between whole-file
             content_hash and per-section/per-chunk integrity, and the substrate for
             content-addressed dedup. (2) Tiered integrity: a near-free hardware checksum (crc32c,
             or a fast non-crypto hash) as a detect-corruption-fast pre-check, falling through to
             a cryptographic hash only on mismatch or when identity is needed. (3) One hash family
             as KDF+MAC+commitment (chacha20-blake3) for an optional committing-AEAD
             encrypted-at-rest container. The high-value, format-safe moves are: factor the four
             hand-rolled SHA-256 call sites into one ~80-line domain-separated hashing.rs trait
             wrapper (digest crate, zero format change); confirm sha2 0.11 runtime SHA-NI/sha2-ext
             dispatch is on (free validate/build speedup); and introduce BLAKE3 as a NEW additive
             hash-algorithm id (never mutating the FROZEN sha256: surfaces -- that is a true v2)
             feeding a content-defined chunk-dedup pass at build time, which is the single biggest
             lever toward "compress more than everyone" on repo/dataset/database corpora where
             cross-document redundancy dominates the codec.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The field has converged on four ideas. (a) Hashing IS a Merkle tree over fixed-size chunks
(BLAKE3: 1024B chunks, 64B blocks, 32B chaining values; parent = hash(left_CV || right_CV)); the
root is one 32B commitment but every interior CV verifies an arbitrary byte range without
re-hashing -- enabling free parallelism (chunks independent until a cheap combine) and verified
streaming/slicing (bao: transmit only path CVs + chunk, ~3.1% metadata at 1KiB chunks, tunable to
~0.1% via chunk-grouped parents). (b) Domain separation by flags/contexts (BLAKE3
ROOT/PARENT/CHUNK/KEYED/DERIVE_KEY) prevents length-extension and tree-substitution and lets one
primitive serve hash, MAC, and KDF -- nest already does the moral equivalent with length-prefixed
name||len framing in content_hash_hex and the "nest:chunk_id:v1\n" domain tag. (c) Runtime
CPU-feature dispatch with a guaranteed byte-identical scalar fallback is universal (crc32c
CanUseSse42/Arm64; RustCrypto cpufeatures zero-sized token; gxhash target_feature) -- and
determinism demands the scalar path produce identical output, which nest enforces and gxhash
notably CANNOT (no scalar fallback). (d) Tiered integrity: hardware CRC32C (3-way interleaved
crc32 + table combine, no PCLMULQDQ dependency) or AES-NI gxhash (>30 GB/s) as fast corruption
detection, reserving cryptographic SHA-256/BLAKE3 for identity. Crypto trend: committing AEAD
(chacha20-blake3) collapses KDF+subkey-separation+MAC+key-commitment into one keyed BLAKE3 XOF
call, binding ciphertext to exactly one key -- the right guarantee for content-addressed
citations. Throughput hierarchy on non-SHA-NI hardware: BLAKE3 ~5-10x SHA-256 (~2x even with
SHA-NI), gxhash >30 GB/s (non-crypto), CRC32C multiple bytes/cycle (non-crypto). All cores share
nest's exact discipline: N independent items per SIMD lane, runtime detection once, repr(C)
little-endian, streaming/incremental.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. BLAKE3 Merkle-tree-over-fixed-chunks: content_hash becomes a ROOT whose interior CVs verify a
   single section/chunk byte-span without re-hashing the file, turning O(file) validate into
   O(touched-bytes)
2. Content-defined chunking + CV-addressed dedup before the entropy coder -- the highest-yield
   path to 'compress more than everyone' on repo/dataset/database corpora
3. Tiered integrity: near-free hardware CRC32C / fast-hash detect-corruption pre-check, falling
   through to SHA-256 only on mismatch or for identity
4. One BLAKE3 family as hash + keyed-MAC + derive_key KDF, giving domain-separated subkeys for all
   four integrity surfaces from a single primitive
5. Runtime CPU-feature dispatch with byte-identical scalar fallback (crc32c CanUseX / RustCrypto
   cpufeatures) as the canonical template for any new hash kernel under nest's determinism
   guarantee
6. Committing AEAD binding ciphertext to exactly one key (Encrypt-then-MAC + keyed-BLAKE3 + LE
   length framing) as the blueprint for an optional encrypted-at-rest codec
7. Chunk-group metadata knob (bao-tree): same root, parent CVs only every N KiB, cutting
   verification overhead from ~3-6% to ~0.1%
8. Generic-over-hash digest trait surface so integrity code is written once and the crate
   auto-selects SHA-NI/sha2-ext/AVX2/NEON backends with no format change

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Extract the four hand-rolled SHA-256 surfaces (header.rs::compute_checksum,
  section_entry.rs::compute_checksum, footer.rs::compute_file_hash,
  decode.rs::file_hash_hex/content_hash_hex) plus chunk.rs::chunk_id into one new ~80-line
  src/hashing.rs that exposes a single domain-separated, length-prefixed preimage builder over
  `impl digest::Update + FixedOutput`. Each surface gets a distinct domain tag (already the
  convention: 'nest:chunk_id:v1\n', section name||len framing). Pure refactor: byte-for-byte
  identical output, validated by existing golden.rs + dual_integrity.rs tests.
    where:  new crates/nest-format/src/hashing.rs; rewire callers in layout/header.rs,
            layout/section_entry.rs, layout/footer.rs, reader/decode.rs, chunk.rs
    impact: Eliminates preimage drift across five sites, makes the integrity spec auditable in one place, and
            creates the single seam where a future BLAKE3 algorithm-id can be added additively.
            Zero format/behavior change.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/RustCrypto/hashes, https://github.com/skerkour/chacha20-blake3

* Confirm sha2 0.11 default runtime-dispatch backends are enabled (no sha2_backend="soft"
  override) so file_hash over the whole pre-footer body, content_hash over decoded canonical
  sections, and per-section checksums get x86 SHA-NI / aarch64 sha2-ext acceleration for free. Add
  a `nest stats`/benchmark line reporting the active SHA backend like SimdBackend::name() already
  does for dot products.
    where:  crates/nest-format/Cargo.toml (verify sha2="0.11" features), reader/decode.rs hot path; surface
            backend in nest-cli/src/cmd/stats.rs + benchmark.rs
    impact: ~2x faster integrity on SHA-NI CPUs (far more vs naive) with no format change -- speeds
            `validate`, build determinism checks, and re-hashing of large compressed sections as
            corpora grow. Keeps deterministic builds cheap.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/RustCrypto/hashes

* Add a build-time content-defined chunk dedup pass: fingerprint each chunks_canonical span (and
  sub-block CDC boundaries) with a fast deterministic hasher, detect duplicate/identical spans
  across the corpus, store each unique span once with refcounts in the chunk_ids section, BEFORE
  zstd runs. Keep the frozen v1 layout unchanged (this is a build-time encoding decision);
  reference each duplicate by its stored index. Use a deterministic fixed-seed hasher so identical
  corpora stay byte-identical.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/writer/encoding_choice.rs and writer/build.rs; sections/chunk_ids.rs for
            refcount/index representation; nest-python writer path
    impact: Highest-yield compression lever on multi-document / repo / dataset / whole-database corpora where
            cross-document redundancy dominates the codec. Dedup-then-compress (restic/borg/casync
            style) can beat raw zstd ratio by a large margin before any codec change.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/BLAKE3-team/BLAKE3, https://github.com/ogxd/gxhash

* Introduce BLAKE3 as a NEW additive hash-algorithm id (e.g. a 'blake3:' chunk_id prefix and a
  hashing-algorithm field) WITHOUT touching any existing sha256: surface. Use BLAKE3's Merkle
  structure so a future large-blob content_hash is a ROOT whose interior CVs verify a single cited
  chunk_id's byte span against the root alone. The sha256: surfaces stay the FROZEN v1 default;
  blake3 is opt-in for large blobs (audio/video/datasets) where 5-10x throughput and
  verified-streaming matter. Feed it large mmap slices (>=16 KiB) to reach full SIMD width.
    where:  new algorithm-id plumbing through the unified src/hashing.rs; chunk.rs chunk_id prefix; a future
            RESERVED merkle_index section id 0x09+ for interleaved parent CVs
    impact: Removes hashing from the critical path for big-file content addressing as nest scales to whole
            databases/repos/video; enables cryptographic verification of just a cited chunk.
            Strictly additive -- v1 stays frozen, never bumps.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/BLAKE3-team/BLAKE3, https://github.com/RustCrypto/hashes

* Add a RESERVED merkle_index / bao_tree section (id 0x09+, additive within v1) holding
  interleaved parent-node chaining values (chunk-grouped so overhead is ~0.1%, not ~3%) so a
  partial or remote consumer of a nest://content_hash/chunk_id citation can cryptographically
  verify just the cited span against the root without the whole file. Tunable chunk-group size is
  the knob that keeps verification metadata from eroding compression-ratio gains.
    where:  new layout constant SECTION_MERKLE_INDEX (0x09) in layout/mod.rs; new sections/ module; consumed
            by nest-cli/src/cmd/cite.rs and validate.rs
    impact: Makes citations cryptographically verifiable for partial/remote consumers at ~0.1% overhead -- a
            real differentiator for sovereign, content-addressed serving of large artifacts.
            Additive, optional, off by default.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/BLAKE3-team/BLAKE3

* Optional fast-integrity pre-check tier for `nest validate`/`inspect`: store a near-free
  non-cryptographic checksum (hardware CRC32C with 3-way interleaving, or an AES-NI/portable hash)
  per section in a reserved metadata field, run it FIRST to detect corruption fast, and only fall
  through to the SHA-256 per-section checksum on mismatch or when cryptographic strength is
  requested. Must ship a deterministic byte-identical scalar fallback (slicing-by-4 reference) to
  honor nest's determinism + never-panic + <=300-line constraints.
    where:  reserved field alongside SectionEntry checksum (or a sidecar reserved section); validate path in
            reader/validate.rs and nest-cli/src/cmd/validate.rs
    impact: Cheap per-chunk corruption detection lets nest push more aggressive lossy int8/f16 quantization
            while still catching decode corruption almost for free. Speeds the validate hot path
            on large files. Note: google recommends crypto/HighwayHash over CRC32C for primary
            content addressing -- keep this as a detect-fast layer only, SHA-256 stays the
            identity.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/google/crc32c, https://github.com/ogxd/gxhash

* Swap the in-memory BuildHasher for hot-path tables that NEVER persist to disk -- bm25/index.rs
  HashMap<String,TermEntry> + per-doc tf_map, bm25/fusion.rs RRF HashMap<usize,f32>, ann/search.rs
  visited HashSet<u32>, ann/mod.rs recall HashSets -- from std SipHash to a fast hasher
  (foldhash/ahash on portable targets, optionally AES-NI gxhash feature-gated behind
  target_feature). Zero format impact because these are query-time/build-time scratch structures,
  not serialized bytes.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/index.rs, bm25/fusion.rs, ann/search.rs, ann/mod.rs
    impact: Cuts BM25 build/query and HNSW traversal CPU (latency win, not size). Helps the known search-text
            overhead. Determinism preserved as long as iteration-order-dependent outputs already
            sort (bm25 alphabetical term order, seeded hnsw) -- verify no output depends on hash
            iteration order.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/ogxd/gxhash, https://github.com/google/crc32c

* Blueprint (not yet build) an optional committing-AEAD encrypted-at-rest codec under a RESERVED
  encoding id (4-255, additive within v1) wrapping non-embedding section payloads: derive
  enc_key|mac_key|nonce from one keyed-BLAKE3 XOF over (master_key, nonce), Encrypt-then-MAC with
  explicit LE length framing, constant-time tag check. Depend on the upstream blake3 crate; do NOT
  vendor a from-scratch ChaCha into never-panic format code -- only the construction/dispatch
  pattern transfers. Determinism holds given (key, fixed-nonce).
    where:  future crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ aead module under a reserved SECTION_ENCODING id; keep
            embeddings unencrypted on the mmap hot scoring path
    impact: Unlocks compress-then-encrypt single-file containers while staying
            offline/single-file/deterministic; committing property binds ciphertext to exactly one
            key -- the right guarantee for content-addressed citations. Orthogonal to ratio but
            expands the sovereign story.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/skerkour/chacha20-blake3, https://github.com/BLAKE3-team/BLAKE3

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Content-defined chunking + CV-addressed dedup BEFORE zstd: store each unique chunks_canonical
    span once with refcounting, deduping identical content across documents/datasets/versions. On
    repo/database/multi-doc corpora cross-artifact redundancy usually dwarfs the codec's
    per-stream gain -- this is the single biggest lever toward 'compress more than everyone', and
    it stays additive (a build-time encoding decision, frozen v1 layout unchanged).
  - Sub-block near-duplicate detection via fast fingerprints (gxhash-speed hashing of CDC
    boundaries) lets nest find and collapse near-identical spans across whole-repo ingestion,
    extending dedup beyond exact matches before the entropy coder runs.
  - Chunk-group Merkle metadata tuning (bao-tree): emit parent CVs only every N KiB so
    verification/proof metadata is ~0.1% instead of ~3-6% -- keeps the integrity layer from
    eroding the compression ratio when nest adds verifiable partial citations to large artifacts.
  - Cheap per-chunk corruption detection (hardware CRC32C / fast non-crypto checksum tier) makes
    aggressive lossy quantization (int8, f16, future entropy coders) safer to push harder, because
    decode integrity is verifiable almost for free per chunk -- indirectly enabling smaller files
    at a given recall.
  - Hardware-accelerated SHA-256 (SHA-NI/sha2-ext, free in sha2 0.11) plus optional BLAKE3 for
    large blobs keep re-hashing of heavily-compressed/deduplicated content off the critical path,
    so nest can afford to hash on every build/validate as corpora grow -- removing the
    disincentive to compress more aggressively.
  - Identical-subtree hashing (BLAKE3 Merkle): two artifacts sharing a sub-range hash that range
    to the same CV, making block-level dedup across datasets/repos/whole-databases detectable by
    hash equality -- the structural prerequisite for corpus-level (not just per-file) compression.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - DETERMINISM: nest guarantees same chunks + same model fingerprint => byte-identical file. Any
    new hasher used in build output (dedup fingerprints, chunk_id) MUST be fixed-seed and
    cross-platform-stable (gxhash only guarantees stability within a major version and needs its
    deterministic feature flag; foldhash/ahash default to randomized seeds). The scalar and SIMD
    paths of any new kernel MUST produce byte-identical output, exactly as simd/mod.rs already
    enforces.
  - FROZEN FORMAT: the four sha256: surfaces (header_checksum, per-section checksum, file_hash,
    content_hash) and the 'sha256:' chunk_id prefix are identity/citation anchors. Changing the
    meaning of any existing hash field is a TRUE v2 (bump NEST_FORMAT_VERSION) -- do NOT swap
    SHA-256 for BLAKE3 in place. BLAKE3 must enter only as a NEW additive algorithm-id; new
    sections only at id 0x09+, new encodings only at id 4-255.
  - gxhash NO SCALAR FALLBACK: requires AES+SSE2 (x86) or AES+NEON (aarch64) and fails to compile
    on unsupported targets -- a direct collision with nest's scalar-fallback/portability
    guarantee. It must be feature-gated behind target_feature detection with a portable fallback
    hasher, NEVER a hard dependency, and confined to runtime in-memory tables / build-time dedup
    -- never into never-panic nest-format library code.
  - NEVER-PANIC + heavy unsafe: gxhash and the from-scratch ChaCha in chacha20-blake3 use
    aggressive unsafe and are explicitly 'not for safety-critical use'. They must not enter
    nest-format library code (typed thiserror, no panics). Depend on the upstream permissive
    blake3 crate (no_std, scalar reference path) rather than re-vendoring crypto primitives; only
    the construction/dispatch PATTERN transfers.
  - EMBEDDINGS HOT PATH: embeddings are scored directly from mmap via SIMD dot products and are
    never zstd-compressed. Do NOT route embeddings through an encrypted/AEAD codec or any
    transform that breaks zero-copy mmap scoring -- confine any encryption codec to non-embedding
    sections, exactly as zstd is.
  - CRC32C IS NOT IDENTITY: google explicitly recommends a stronger hash (HighwayHash/crypto) over
    CRC32C for new file formats. Use CRC32C/fast hashes only as a detect-corruption-fast tier;
    SHA-256 content_hash must remain the stable content-addressing identity behind nest://
    citations.
  - BLAKE3 SIMD WIDTH: BLAKE3 update() only reaches full SIMD width on large buffers (>=16 KiB for
    AVX-512; 8 KiB hits AVX2). Feeding it small per-chunk slices forfeits the throughput win --
    only adopt it where nest can hand it large mmap slices (large blobs), not for tiny per-section
    hashing where SHA-NI SHA-256 is already competitive.
  - FILE HYGIENE: <=300 lines/file (333 hard cap), single-responsibility, repr(C), little-endian
    unsigned, lowercase docs no emoji/em-dash. The unified hashing.rs, any merkle_index module,
    and dispatch glue must each stay within these limits and split rather than grow.


##################################################################################################
# simd-lowlevel  -  SIMD, vectorization, portable intrinsics, fast string ops
##################################################################################################

summary:     This category is fundamentally a serve-side accelerator for nest's compression mission, not a
             compressor itself: none of the 12 repos add an entropy coder, but collectively they
             answer the one question that gates how far nest can push quantization: "can the
             smallest-on-disk embedding format also be the fastest to score directly off mmap,
             bit-identically on every CPU?" The state of the art has converged hard on one
             dispatch idiom that nest already implements correctly: detect CPU features once,
             cache in a OnceLock/atomic-bitset, then indirect-call a per-arch kernel with zero
             per-element CPUID cost (stdarch, Highway, memchr, StringZilla, ACL all do exactly
             this; nest's simd/mod.rs is a faithful copy). nest's current code is solid but leaves
             three concrete, code-confirmed wins on the table. (1) The AVX2 f16 path in
             simd/mod.rs lines 104-112 explicitly bails to scalar to dodge the F16C detection
             branch, taxing the compressed/tiny presets on x86; stdarch's vcvtph2ps
             (_mm256_cvtph_ps) converts 8 f16->f32 in one instruction. (2) The int8 path (avx2.rs
             lines 36-60, neon.rs 68-95) widens i8->i32->f32 then FMAs in f32, which is both slow
             AND reduction-order-dependent (float horizontal sum is not associative, so the
             cross-arch parity tests only pass within 1e-4, not bit-exact); ACL/stdarch show
             vpdpbusd (AVX-VNNI) and vdotq_s32 (NEON dotprod) do 4 int8 MACs/lane into i32 in ONE
             instruction with reduction-order-INDEPENDENT integer accumulation, which is both ~4x
             faster on the tiny preset's hot path AND restores true bit-determinism (a nest hard
             requirement that float reduction currently only approximates). (3) Top-k selection is
             a full sort_by over all n scored pairs (search.rs lines 128, 213, 260), where
             x86-simd-sort/Highway's branch-free compressstore partition + select gives a faster,
             branchless top-k that keeps the mandatory exact-cosine rerank cheap as corpora grow.
             The int8 codec's 8-byte prefix (payload_version + scale_kind discriminators, int8.rs
             lines 17-19) confirms asymmetric scale+zero_point quant and sub-byte formats
             (int4/binary) are genuinely additive - new scale_kind/payload_version values, no
             NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump, no new encoding id needed. The deepest structural steal is
             the witness-token / write-once-generic-kernel pattern (fearless_simd, memchr's Vector
             trait): nest currently hand-maintains a 3 dtype x 3 ISA matrix of near-duplicate
             unsafe functions, and every new quantized dtype (int4, bf16, binary) multiplies that
             matrix; one generic dot_f32<S: Simd> body with thin per-arch shims plus a verified
             scalar oracle collapses it, keeps files under the 300-line cap, and removes unsafe
             from the hot path - the enabler for pushing the tiny preset below its current 0.283
             ratio without a portability tax. Watch-outs: never adopt faster's compile-time-only
             dispatch (pins the binary to the build host ISA, breaks the
             single-portable-offline-binary promise); vendor patterns clean-room rather than
             depending on experimental crates (fearless_simd is explicitly pre-stable) to protect
             the frozen-format/determinism guarantees; and the mandatory scalar fallback must
             remain a first-class verified sibling for sovereign portability to old-x86/RISC-V
             where the dispatcher drops straight to scalar.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The field has fully converged on "runtime feature dispatch over a write-once generic kernel":
compile every kernel variant once (per ISA), detect CPU capability ONCE via getauxval(AT_HWCAP) on
aarch64 / CPUID leaf 7 on x86, cache the result lock-free (atomic bitset in stdarch, OnceLock
function-pointer in nest/Highway/memchr), then indirect-call the chosen kernel per hot-path entry
with zero per-element branch cost. The frontier has moved to TWO refinements nest has not yet
adopted. First, FINE-GRAINED feature detection: not just "avx2" but f16c, avxvnni/avx512vnni,
aarch64 dotprod/i8mm as SEPARATE gates, because the single most valuable instructions for
quantized scoring (vcvtph2ps for f16->f32, vpdpbusd/vdotq_s32 for 4-int8-MACs-into-i32) live
behind those finer flags. Second, INTEGER-DOMAIN quantized dot products (ACL's GEMMLowp):
accumulate int8*int8 into int32 with hardware dot-product instructions and apply scale/zero_point
only at the end, which is both ~4x faster than widen-to-f32-then-FMA AND
reduction-order-independent (hence bit-reproducible across CPUs, unlike float horizontal sums).
The portable-kernel-authoring frontier is the witness-token pattern (fearless_simd: a ZST
type-level proof of an enabled target_feature makes core::arch intrinsics safe to call) and the
write-once Vector-trait shim (memchr, Highway, SIMDe's per-function cascade), both of which
collapse the NxM (dtype x ISA) unsafe kernel matrix into one generic body. Two reusable SIMD
primitives recur as the engine behind everything else: the nibble-LUT-via-byte-shuffle
(pshufb/vpshufb/vtbl) for branchless byte classification (simdutf UTF-8 validation at <1
instr/byte, StringZilla's 21 GB/s sz_lookup, base64 codecs), and compressstore/Compress
stream-compaction (vpcompressd on AVX-512, table-driven permute-LUT emulation on AVX2,
shuffle-table on NEON) for branch-free partition/top-k/filter (x86-simd-sort, Highway vqsort).
Correctness discipline has standardized on QEMU/SIMDE_NO_NATIVE differential testing (the scalar
path is a verified equivalence sibling, not an afterthought) and stdarch's assert_instr
(disassemble the compiled shim to prove the intended instruction was actually emitted). The one
anti-pattern the field flags explicitly is compile-time-only width selection (faster, and any -C
target-feature global build): it pins the binary to the build host's widest ISA, which is
incompatible with shipping one portable offline binary.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Integer-domain int8 dot product via hardware dot-product instructions (ACL GEMMLowp): vpdpbusd
   (AVX-VNNI) / vdotq_s32 (NEON dotprod) do 4 int8 MACs/lane into int32 in one instruction, scale
   applied at the end. ~4x faster than nest's i8->i32->f32-widen-then-FMA AND
   reduction-order-independent, making the tiny preset both fastest AND truly bit-deterministic.
2. Fine-grained feature gates + single-instruction conversion (stdarch):
   is_x86_feature_detected!("f16c") -> _mm256_cvtph_ps (8 f16->f32 in one vcvtph2ps);
   avxvnni/dotprod gates. Fixes the perf gap nest's own mod.rs 104-112 documents.
3. Witness-token / write-once generic SIMD kernel (fearless_simd + memchr Vector trait): one
   dot_f32<S: Simd> body per dtype with thin per-arch shims and a verified scalar oracle,
   collapsing nest's 3x3 unsafe matrix and removing unsafe from the hot path.
4. Branch-free compressstore stream-compaction (x86-simd-sort + Highway): mask+compressstore for
   branchless top-k/threshold filter (vpcompressd / AVX2 permute-LUT / NEON shuffle-table),
   replacing the full sort_by top-k and keeping exact-cosine rerank cheap at scale.
5. Asymmetric scale+zero_point quantization (ACL): more fidelity per int8 than symmetric
   max_abs/127, enabling aggressive int4 without recall loss; additive via a new int8 scale_kind
   discriminator.
6. Default-value tail padding (faster): pad the final partial vector with the FMA identity so one
   full-width FMA finishes the dim, removing the hand-written remainder loops and per-query tail
   branch.
7. Lane-widening upcast/downcast + vtbl/pshufb bit-unpack (faster + StringZilla): the blueprint
   for int4 (2 lanes/byte) and binary kernels that push the tiny preset below 0.283.
8. Nibble-LUT-via-byte-shuffle (simdutf + StringZilla): <1 instr/byte UTF-8 validation before
   content_hash, plus the primitive for a codepoint-aware bm25 tokenizer that fixes the CJK/Thai
   gap.
9. Packed-pair rare-byte prefilter (memchr): allocation-free vectorized term scan over mmapped
   chunks_canonical for bm25/search-text, and selective prefilters over int8 dimensions.
10. MinHash/Rabin retrieve-then-rerank sketch (StringZilla): deterministic near-duplicate dedup to
   drop chunks before encoding (raising ratio) and a candidate pre-filter mirroring nest's
   HNSW+rerank contract.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add fine-grained feature tiers to the int8 hot path: gate
  is_x86_feature_detected!("avxvnni")/("avx512vnni") to use _mm256_dpbusd_epi32 (vpdpbusd) and
  is_aarch64_feature_detected!("dotprod") to use vdotq_s32, accumulating int8*int8 into i32 and
  multiplying by the per-vector scale ONCE at the end. Keep the current widen-to-f32 path as the
  fallback tier. Crucially this also makes int8 scoring reduction-order-independent (integer
  accumulation), restoring true cross-CPU bit-determinism that the current float-FMA path only
  approximates.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/avx2.rs (new dot_i32_i8_vnni alongside dot_f32_i8_avx2 lines 36-60),
            crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/neon.rs (dot_i32_i8_dotprod alongside dot_f32_i8_neon
            lines 68-95), dispatch + new SimdBackend variants (Avx2Vnni, NeonDotprod) in
            simd/mod.rs; score path unchanged in search.rs score_all/score_subset DType::Int8
            arms.
    impact: high: ~4x int8 throughput on the tiny preset (the most-compressed embedding format, 0.283 ratio,
            recall@10 0.992), turning the smallest-on-disk format into the fastest-to-search, AND
            restoring true bit-determinism on the int8 path
    effort: medium: two new kernels + scalar i32 reference + two dispatch tiers; integer accumulation
            simplifies the parity test (exact equality, not epsilon); stays under the 300-line
            file cap
    from:   https://github.com/ARM-software/ComputeLibrary, https://github.com/rust-lang/stdarch

* Add an F16C tier to the AVX2 f16 path so the compressed/tiny f16 presets stop falling back to
  scalar on x86. Gate is_x86_feature_detected!("f16c") and use _mm256_cvtph_ps (vcvtph2ps) to
  convert 8 f16 lanes to f32 in one instruction, then FMA against the f32 query, mirroring the
  existing NEON fcvtl path (neon.rs dot_f32_f16_neon lines 39-66).
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/avx2.rs (new dot_f32_f16_avx2), wired into simd/mod.rs
            dot_f32_f16_bytes which currently bails to scalar at lines 104-112 with a code comment
            admitting the gap
    impact: high: removes a documented, self-flagged perf gap; gives the compressed preset (zstd/f16) a real
            x86 SIMD dot path instead of scalar, directly lowering per-query latency on the
            second-most-compressed embedding format
    effort: low: ~25 lines, one new feature tier, parity test already exists (tests.rs
            f16_simd_matches_scalar)
    from:   https://github.com/rust-lang/stdarch, https://github.com/nemequ/simde

* Replace the full sort_by top-k (over all n scored pairs) with a branch-free SIMD
  select/partition: compute scores in a register, mask=(score>=running_threshold), compressstore
  the surviving (score,index) pairs densely (vpcompressd on AVX-512, 256-entry permute-LUT on
  AVX2, NEON shuffle-table, scalar fallback), then a partial top-k on the survivors. Carry the
  index payload in lockstep with the score (x86-simd-sort key-value pattern) so the sorted top-k
  comes out without a separate gather.
