    where:  new crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/compress.rs (table-driven Compress + select), called from
            search.rs sort_scores_desc/score_all top-k (lines 128, 260) and the ANN/hybrid rerank
            truncation (lines 163, 213-216)
    impact: medium: keeps the mandatory exact-cosine rerank cheap as corpora grow; branchless filtering pairs
            directly with the dot-product kernel; pure compute with fixed LUTs => byte-identical
            deterministic results, respects additive-format/never-panic
    effort: medium-high: compressstore is AVX-512-native but must be permute-LUT-emulated on AVX2 and
            shuffle-table on NEON, plus a mandatory scalar fallback; this is the highest-effort
            item and only pays once n is large
    from:   https://github.com/intel/x86-simd-sort, https://github.com/google/highway

* Refactor the 3-dtype x 3-ISA unsafe kernel matrix into ONE generic body per dtype using a
  vendored (not crate-dependency) witness-token / Vector-trait pattern: dot_f32<S: Simd>,
  dot_f32_f16<S: Simd>, dot_f32_i8<S: Simd> with thin per-arch shims, the scalar path as a
  verified equivalence sibling. Factor the f16->f32 and i8->i32 widening steps (currently inlined
  in avx2.rs/neon.rs) into small shared inlined helpers so adding int4/bf16/binary becomes one
  widen-helper + one scalar reference, not a new per-arch matrix.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/ - collapse avx2.rs/neon.rs/scalar.rs duplication; keep the
            OnceLock+function-pointer dispatch in mod.rs unchanged (it is already the correct
            model)
    impact: medium: removes unsafe from the hot path (satisfies never-panic/clean-core), keeps every file
            under the 300-line cap, and is the structural enabler for cheaply adding sub-byte
            quantized dtypes that push compression below 0.283
    effort: medium: vendor the pattern clean-room (do NOT depend on the experimental fearless_simd crate);
            requires careful #[inline(always)] discipline (a missed inline silently de-optimizes);
            verify byte-identical machine code via assert_instr before/after
    from:   https://github.com/linebender/fearless_simd, https://github.com/BurntSushi/memchr,
            https://github.com/nemequ/simde

* Add an additive asymmetric int8 codec (scale + zero_point) as a new int8 scale_kind, plus design
  an int4 packed codec as a new payload_version, both behind the existing 8-byte int8 prefix
  discriminators. Asymmetric quant packs more fidelity per int8, enabling more aggressive
  quantization toward int4 without recall loss. The integer-domain VNNI/dotprod kernels above fold
  the (a-zp_a)*(b-zp_b) expansion into a precomputed offset-correction term so the hot loop stays
  a single dot instruction.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/int8.rs - new INT8_SCALE_KIND_ASYMMETRIC (=1) writing per-vector
            zero_point alongside scale; a future int4.rs as a new encoding id (4-255
            reserved/additive) or new payload_version; reader/validate.rs int8 parse arm; runtime
            dot_i32_i8 kernels consume the zero_point
    impact: high (compression frontier): more fidelity per byte than nest's current symmetric max_abs/127, the
            lever that beats the 0.283 tiny ratio while keeping real-cosine rerank and recall;
            genuinely additive (scale_kind/payload_version discriminators already exist in int8.rs
            lines 17-19), no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump
    effort: medium-high: codec + offset-correction math + parity/recall validation; sub-byte int4 unpack needs
            the vtbl/pshufb lane-widening kernel; must keep deterministic build (same chunks+model
            => byte-identical)
    from:   https://github.com/ARM-software/ComputeLibrary, https://github.com/AdamNiederer/faster,
            https://github.com/ashvardanian/StringZilla

* Kill the hand-written scalar remainder loops in the SIMD kernels with default-value tail
  padding: pad the embedding row's final partial vector with the FMA identity (0.0) so one
  full-width FMA finishes the dim, removing the per-query tail branch. (Embedding dims 384-1536
  are usually multiples of 8/4 but not guaranteed; padding makes the kernel uniform.)
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/avx2.rs (remove tail loops lines 18-28 and 52-58),
            crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/neon.rs (lines 19-29, 57-64, 89-92)
    impact: low-medium: shrinks each kernel, removes a per-query branch on the row-scoring hot path that runs
            for EVERY row off mmap; faster's benches show uneven-within-1%-of-even after padding
    effort: low: a few lines per kernel, but must handle the mmap boundary safely (pad into a local buffer for
            the final partial vector, never read past the section), and re-run parity tests
    from:   https://github.com/AdamNiederer/faster

* Vendor simdutf's lookup-table UTF-8 validation pattern (or depend on the MIT/Apache
  rusticstuff/simdutf8 Rust port) to validate/normalize chunks_canonical at <1 instr/byte BEFORE
  computing content_hash, killing a class of non-deterministic decode bugs that would silently
  break content_hash stability across encodings.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/canonical.rs (validate before the content_hash surface), reachable
            from the writer encode path and reader/validate.rs
    impact: medium (correctness): hardens the content_hash STABLE-across-encodings guarantee, which is
            load-bearing for citations (nest://content_hash/chunk_id) and deterministic builds
    effort: low: rusticstuff/simdutf8 is a drop-in zero-dep crate with the exact lookup algorithm; respects
            offline/never-socket constraint
    from:   https://github.com/simdutf/simdutf, https://github.com/BurntSushi/memchr

* Adopt stdarch's assert_instr verification discipline plus SIMDe's NO_NATIVE differential-test
  idea in the SIMD test suite: a disassembly check that the int8/f16/f32 kernels actually emit
  vfmadd/vpdpbusd/vcvtph2ps/vdotq_s32, and a forced-scalar-vs-SIMD parity gate (NEST_FORCE_SCALAR
  already exists). For the new integer VNNI/dotprod int8 path, assert EXACT equality (not epsilon)
  since integer accumulation is reduction-order-independent.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/tests.rs (extend the existing parity tests at lines 45-99; the
            NEST_FORCE_SCALAR env hook is already wired in mod.rs detect_backend)
    impact: medium: catches a refactor that silently drops a fused instruction or de-optimizes (a real risk of
            the witness-token refactor); proves the three+ backends return identical results on
            the same .nest file, protecting exact-rerank ground truth and deterministic builds
    effort: low: test-only, zero runtime cost; assert_instr is a proc-macro shim
    from:   https://github.com/rust-lang/stdarch, https://github.com/nemequ/simde,
            https://github.com/DLTcollab/sse2neon

* Add a deterministic MinHash/Rabin-fingerprint sketch as an optional section (id 0x09+, reserved)
  and/or a build-time near-duplicate dedup pass: drop redundant chunks before encoding to raise
  the effective compression ratio, and use the compact sketch as a candidate pre-filter that
  shrinks the set before the mandatory exact-cosine rerank, mirroring nest's existing HNSW+rerank
  contract.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/ (new sketch section, deterministic alphabetical/seeded like
            bm25/hnsw); build-time dedup in nest-python build path; runtime pre-filter in
            crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs before score_subset
    impact: medium (compression + serve): dedup directly raises effective ratio toward
            compress-more-than-everyone; pre-filter keeps rerank cheap. Indirect/second-order vs
            the int8/int4 codec wins
    effort: medium-high: new optional section + deterministic sketch construction + must preserve
            seeded/reproducible-build guarantee; larger surface than the kernel-level steals
    from:   https://github.com/ashvardanian/StringZilla

* Add a binary/Hamming embedding kernel as the most extreme tiny-class preset: pack embeddings to
  1 bit/dim (sign), score with popcount(xor) (vpternlogq+vpopcntq on AVX-512, NEON cnt+addv,
  scalar count_ones), and ALWAYS run the existing exact-cosine rerank on the survivors so the
  returned score stays real cosine. This is the lane-widening/bit-unpack pattern taken to its
  limit (32x smaller than f32).
    where:  new crates/nest-format/src/encoding/binary.rs (new encoding id, additive),
            crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/ binary dot kernel, search.rs DType::Binary arm feeding
            the existing rerank
    impact: high (compression frontier, speculative): 1 bit/dim is the single biggest lever to beat 0.283;
            mandatory exact rerank preserves the real-cosine contract so recall is recoverable.
            Highest-risk/highest-reward, recall must be validated on the demo corpus
    effort: high: new codec + popcount kernels per ISA + recall validation + rerank integration; recall@10
            must be proven on the PT-BR corpus before shipping as a preset
    from:   https://github.com/AdamNiederer/faster, https://github.com/ARM-software/ComputeLibrary,
            https://github.com/google/highway

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Asymmetric scale+zero_point int8 quantization (ACL GEMMLowp) packs more fidelity per byte than
    nest's current symmetric max_abs/127 scheme (encoding/int8.rs line 34), enabling more
    aggressive quantization toward int4 without recall loss - additive via a new scale_kind
    discriminator already reserved in the int8 8-byte prefix, no format bump.
  - Integer-domain VNNI/dotprod scoring (vpdpbusd/vdotq_s32) makes the smallest-on-disk int8
    format (tiny preset, 0.283 ratio, recall@10 0.992) ALSO the fastest to score off mmap,
    removing the latency tax that currently makes quantized dtypes slower per query than f32 - so
    nest can DEFAULT to harder compression without losing the serve-from-disk speed promise.
  - An int4 packed codec (new payload_version behind the existing int8 prefix) unpacked via the
    lane-widening vtbl/pshufb pattern: ~2x smaller than int8 on the embedding section (the bulk of
    bytes, never zstd-compressed because scored from mmap), the realistic next step below 0.283
    with a mandatory exact-cosine rerank guarding recall.
  - A binary/Hamming 1-bit-per-dim codec scored with popcount(xor) is the single largest
    embedding-size lever (32x smaller than f32); the mandatory exact-cosine rerank on survivors
    keeps the returned score real cosine, so recall is recoverable - the most aggressive path
    toward compress-more-than-everyone.
  - MinHash/Rabin-fingerprint near-duplicate dedup (StringZilla) drops redundant chunks BEFORE
    encoding, raising the effective compression ratio on the whole .nest file without any new
    entropy coder - a deterministic, seeded, reproducible-build-safe win.
  - Branch-free top-k/compressstore + faster dispatched kernels free up the rerank budget, which
    is what lets nest AFFORD finer per-block quantization (int4/int8 with SIMD dequant) at serve
    time without blowing the mandatory exact-cosine rerank latency target - the serve-side
    headroom that makes harder on-disk compression viable.
  - Sorted/clustered embeddings (cheap branch-free argsort from x86-simd-sort at build time)
    improve residual locality, so delta+int8 quantization of reordered rows compresses better - a
    real but second-order assist for the tiny/compressed presets.
  - Keeping more NON-embedding sections zstd-compressed is safe for latency because
    memchr/StringZilla give allocation-free vectorized term/substring scan over decoded text, so
    bm25/search-text stay fast even with compressed sections - buying ratio on the text side
    without hurting serve speed.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Do NOT adopt faster's (or any -C target-feature global) compile-time-only width selection: it
    pins the binary to the build host's widest ISA, directly breaking nest's
    single-portable-offline-binary promise. Borrow faster's tail-padding/lane-widening kernel
    tricks ONLY; keep nest's runtime OnceLock function-pointer dispatch, which is strictly better
    here.
  - Vendor patterns clean-room, never depend on the experimental crate: fearless_simd is
    explicitly 'extremely early experimental, no stability guarantees'; a frozen-format,
    deterministic-build database cannot pin its hot path to a pre-stable dependency. Same for any
    crate whose codegen could drift and break byte-reproducibility.
  - Float horizontal-sum reduction is NOT associative, so the current f32/f16/i8-widened paths are
    only ~1e-4 close across CPUs, not bit-identical - a latent risk to nest's deterministic
    guarantee if any caller ever hashes or compares raw scores. The integer-domain int8 path
    (VNNI/dotprod) FIXES this (reduction-order-independent); prefer it precisely for determinism,
    not just speed.
  - The mandatory scalar fallback must stay a first-class, parity-verified sibling (SIMDe
    NO_NATIVE discipline), because nest's dispatcher drops straight to scalar on old x86 / RISC-V
    / non-NEON ARM - sovereign portability means the scalar path is load-bearing, not an
    afterthought. Keep it shape-friendly (fixed chunked loops, fixed-width types) so it still
    auto-vectorizes off-target.
  - compressstore (vpcompressd) is AVX-512-native and must be permute-LUT-emulated on AVX2 and
    shuffle-table-emulated on NEON, with a mandatory scalar fallback - the top-k/compress steal is
    the highest-effort item and only pays once n is large; don't pay its complexity cost
    prematurely on small corpora.
  - Any new int8/int4/binary codec must keep the int8 prefix discriminators
    (payload_version/scale_kind) additive and must NOT touch existing field meanings, or it forces
    a true v2 NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump. Confirmed safe today: int8.rs lines 17-19 already version
    the layout. Validate that the writer stays deterministic (same chunks+model => byte-identical)
    for every new codec.
  - sse2neon-class semantic gaps are real on the quantized path: _mm_rsqrt_ps(0.0) is NaN on NEON
    vs INF on SSE, and min/max NaN ordering differs unless PRECISE flags are set. nest's NaN guard
    runs before rerank (search.rs validate_query lines 28-31) but any new SIMD reduction must
    preserve identical NaN/rounding behavior across backends or the cross-arch parity tests will
    (correctly) fail.
  - Sub-byte and SIMD kernels must not blow the <=300-line-per-file cap or the
    single-responsibility rule; the witness-token/generic-body refactor is what KEEPS them under
    the cap, so do that refactor BEFORE multiplying dtypes, not after. A missed #[inline(always)]
    in that pattern silently de-optimizes the hot loop - gate with assert_instr.


##################################################################################################
# serialization-parsing  -  zero-copy serialization, fast JSON/CSV parsing, mmap-friendly layouts
##################################################################################################

summary:     The seven repos cluster into two complementary disciplines nest must master to deliver its
             "compress AND serve json/yaml/parquet/datasets" north star: (1) zero-copy/mmap LAYOUT
             (serde borrowed-view codecs, FlatBuffers offset-tables + vtable dedup + FlexBuffers
             min-width packing, rkyv archived-struct casts via mmap-sync, simd-r-drive 64-byte
             alignment + backward-link recovery, csv2 lazy-offset cursors) and (2) SIMD STRUCTURAL
             PARSING (simdjson/simd-json two-stage parse: branch-free bitmask classification +
             prefix-XOR quote masking into a fixed-stride u64 tape). Grounding against the actual
             code: nest already has the right bones - decode_payload returns Cow (serde-style
             borrow vs copy), SECTION_ALIGNMENT=64 is declared in layout/mod.rs,
             Int8EmbeddingsView borrows rows zero-copy, and the simd dispatcher uses
             OnceLock+cpuid exactly like simdjson's runtime dispatch. The gaps the extracts target
             are real and verifiable in-tree: chunk_ids stores 71-byte ASCII "sha256:<64hex>"
             strings (sections/chunk_ids.rs) instead of 32 raw bytes; chunk_ids/canonical/spans
             are sequential length-prefixed strings with NO offset table so reaching chunk N is
             O(n) (sections/spans.rs, canonical.rs); materialize_f32_vectors allocates n*dim*4 per
             call even though a borrowed int8 view exists; and the bm25 tokenizer is a scalar
             per-char loop (bm25/tokenize.rs), the documented CJK/Thai gap. The highest-value,
             lowest-risk steals are pure-data-layout wins that move the size ratio with zero
             hot-path cost and respect the additive-only v1 contract (new encoding ids 4-255, new
             section ids 0x09+, never re-meaning an existing field). The tape/structural-index
             ideas are the strategic bridge to ingesting and sub-citing structured documents, but
             they are bigger and should land behind the layout wins.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
State of the art in serialization-parsing for an mmap-first embedded DB is a clean separation of
"find the structure" from "interpret the values," with the structure stored once as a low-entropy,
fixed-stride, position-independent offset table that is cheap to mmap, cheap to zstd, and supports
O(1) random access. simdjson/simd-json define the parse frontier: a branch-free SIMD stage-1
classifies 64-byte chunks into structural/whitespace/quote bitmasks, resolves string interiors
with a single prefix-XOR carry-less-multiply (parity bit carried across chunks), validates UTF-8
in the same pass at 2-6 GB/s, then emits a flat u64 tape (type-tag in high bits + offset/length
payload, container links for O(1) skip) with strings in a separate length-prefixed blob; On-Demand
defers per-field decode for 2-4x on partial reads. The layout frontier is set by FlatBuffers
(vtable indirection with deduplicated vtables so N homogeneous rows cost one vtable; self-relative
u32/u16 offsets making buffers position-independent and DAG-shareable; absent-field-as-default
needs no presence bitmap) and FlexBuffers (per-value width tag narrowing every scalar to
8/16/32/64 bits + pooled strings/keys, often smaller than a typed format while keeping O(1)
indexed access). serde sets the API frontier: an N+M data-model split with lifetime-parameterized
Deserialize<'de> that returns &'de str/&[u8] borrowed straight out of the input, falling back to
owned (Cow) only when escapes force it - monomorphized, no reflection, no intermediate Value tree.
rkyv (via cloudflare mmap-sync) and simd-r-drive push the zero-copy-read extreme: cast repr(C) LE
bytes to typed archived structs with no deserialize step, read in tens of nanoseconds, with
64-byte payload alignment so &[u8] reinterprets directly as SIMD &[f32]; mmap-sync adds a
packed-u64 {index|size|checksum} version word for wait-free RCU publish/swap, and simd-r-drive
adds a backward-linked metadata chain for index-free, torn-write-safe recovery. csv2 shows the
lazy-offset endpoint: the mmap'd buffer IS the data structure, cells are [start,end) spans
computed at deref, unread columns cost nothing. The consensus lesson for nest: keep canonical text
byte-exact (content_hash stability), store all derived structure as separate fixed-stride
little-endian offset/tape arrays (low-entropy, zstd-friendly, mmap-scannable), decode by borrowed
reference, and gate any SIMD kernel behind cpuid with a deterministic scalar fallback.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. FlatBuffers/FlexBuffers compact integer encoding for cold metadata: store sha256 as 32 raw
   bytes not 71-byte ASCII 'sha256:<64hex>' (~2.2x pre-zstd on chunk_ids), and
   min-width/delta+varint pack offsets, lengths and provenance ints. Pure entropy reduction, zero
   hot-path decode cost, fully deterministic - the single highest size/effort ratio in the
   category.
2. FlatBuffers uoffset offset-table layout for variable-length sections: prepend a u32 (or
   width-narrowed) offset array to chunk_ids/canonical/spans so cite/materialize jumps to chunk N
   in O(1) off the mapped page instead of the current O(n) sequential length-prefixed scan.
   Compression-neutral but the structural enabler for serving compressed corpora and
   nest://content_hash/chunk_id byte-span reopen.
3. serde borrowed-view codec discipline (Deserialize<'de> / visit_borrowed_str / Cow): make the
   section decode path return &'a [u8]/&'a str/&'a [f32] borrowed from mmap, copying only when
   zstd forces it. nest already does this for embeddings via decode_payload->Cow; extend it to
   text sections and kill the per-call n*dim*4 allocation in materialize_f32_vectors by threading
   a reused scratch buffer (serde DeserializeSeed pattern).
4. simdjson two-stage parse with a fixed-stride u64 tape + separate length-prefixed string blob:
   the on-disk representation for ingested json/yaml as a new RESERVED section (0x09+).
   Low-entropy offset arrays that zstd crushes, mmap-scannable, pointer-free, enabling
   sub-document citation (nest://content_hash/chunk_id#json_pointer) without re-parsing on the hot
   path.
5. simdjson/simd-json branch-free SIMD byte classification + prefix-XOR parity scan as a reusable
   region/delimiter kernel: powers a SIMD bm25 word-boundary tokenizer (fixing the scalar per-char
   CJK/Thai gap in bm25/tokenize.rs) and future CSV/JSONL delimiter scanning, wired through the
   existing avx2/neon/scalar dispatcher. Implement portable (no PCLMULQDQ dependency) to stay
   deterministic.
6. rkyv/mmap-sync archived-struct zero-copy reads: formalize nest's mmap-cast of repr(C) LE
   structs (header, section table, hnsw/bm25 nodes) as typed views with no parse step, tens-of-ns
   open. nest's repr(C)+LE invariants already match the archived-layout discipline.
7. simd-r-drive 64-byte payload-alignment guarantee + verification on read: nest declares
   SECTION_ALIGNMENT=64 but does not verify it on the embeddings section at open; verifying it is
   the missing precondition for aligned SIMD loads (vmovaps vs vmovups) with zero copy/bounds
   fixups on the dot-product hot path.
8. simd-r-drive backward-linked 'stop at last valid section' recovery scan as an additive nest-cli
   validate mode: walk sections from the footer backward, verify each per-section SHA-256, report
   the last intact boundary without trusting the manifest - hardens the single-file/offline
   guarantee against torn writes, no format bump.
9. mmap-sync packed-u64 version word {index|size|checksum} + double-buffer RCU swap as the
   substrate for nest's missing real-time edit/hot-reload: publish a rebuilt container and query
   it wait-free while old mmap readers drain, implemented as an in-process atomic-swap sidecar
   OUTSIDE the frozen format (never a socket).
10. FlexBuffers/FlatBuffers self-relative offsets + structural dedup (shared substrings/vtables):
   store a repeated source_uri in chunks_original_spans once and reference it by offset (most
   spans share one source doc), and resolve absent optional provenance fields to defaults instead
   of storing nulls.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Store chunk ids as 32 raw SHA-256 digest bytes instead of the 71-byte ASCII string 'sha256:<64
  hex>'. Reconstruct the 'sha256:' prefix and hex on decode. This is an additive encoding change
  to the chunk_ids section payload (or a new encoding id in the 4-255 reserved space), not a
  format-version bump, and is a pure function of inputs so determinism/reproducibility hold.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/chunk_ids.rs (encode_chunk_ids/decode_chunk_ids); add a 16/32-bit
            width tag in the 12-byte SECTION_PAYLOAD_PREFIX or a new SECTION_ENCODING_* id in
            layout/mod.rs. content_hash in reader/decode.rs must hash the canonical decoded
            representation so citations stay stable across the encoding switch.
    impact: ~2.2x reduction on chunk_ids section before zstd, plus removal of the redundant per-id 'sha256:'
            prefix; direct entropy win toward 'compress more than everyone' on every corpus with
            zero hot-path cost.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/google/flatbuffers

* Add a u32 (width-narrowed where possible) offset table at the head of variable-length sections
  (chunk_ids, chunks_canonical, chunks_original_spans) so reaching chunk N is O(1) off the mapped
  page instead of an O(n) sequential scan of length-prefixed strings. Offsets are
  self-relative/section-local and little-endian; offset deltas are low-entropy and zstd-friendly.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/{chunk_ids,canonical,spans}.rs encode/decode; consumed by
            crates/nest-runtime/src/materialize.rs and the cite path (nest-cli/src/cmd/cite.rs) to
            jump directly to chunk N for nest://content_hash/chunk_id reopen.
    impact: Unlocks O(1) random-access materialize/cite off compressed corpora (currently must decode earlier
            chunks first); compression-neutral but a structural prerequisite for serving large
            compressed datasets and sub-citation.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/google/flatbuffers, https://github.com/p-ranav/csv2

* Eliminate the per-call n*dim*4 f32 allocation in the materialize/ANN path by threading a
  reusable scratch out-buffer through dequant (serde DeserializeSeed-style state param), and
  return borrowed &[f32]/&[i8] views where the dtype permits (raw f32 and int8 already borrow via
  Int8EmbeddingsView; only zstd/f16 conversions need to own).
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/materialize.rs (materialize_f32_vectors reallocates vec![0.0f32; n*dim]
            every call) and crates/nest-format/src/encoding/int8.rs; mirror the encoding-driven
            borrow-vs-copy already in nest-format/src/encoding/mod.rs decode_payload (Cow).
    impact: Removes hot-path allocation on repeated search/ANN-build calls, serving the light/cheap pillar;
            lets aggressive int8/f16 encodings cost zero runtime allocation, keeping the SIMD
            dot-product loop reading lanes straight from mmap.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/serde-rs/serde, https://github.com/cloudflare/mmap-sync

* Replace the scalar per-char bm25 tokenizer with a SIMD word-boundary scanner: classify ASCII
  alnum vs separator bytes into a bitmask over 64-byte chunks (simdjson stage-1 style) for fast
  Latin splitting, with a scalar fallback for non-ASCII codepoints. Use a portable
  carry-less/parity scan (no PCLMULQDQ dependency) to stay deterministic.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/tokenize.rs (currently a char-by-char is_alphanumeric loop), wired
            through the existing crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/{avx2,neon,scalar}.rs OnceLock+cpuid
            dispatcher.
    impact: Speeds bm25 build/query on large corpora (a documented throughput gap) and is the seam to later
            improve CJK/Thai segmentation; serves the cheap/light pillar without touching size
            ratio.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/simdjson/simdjson, https://github.com/simd-lite/simd-json

* Add an additive backward 'stop at last valid section' recovery mode to validate: walk the
  section table from the footer backward, verify each per-section SHA-256, and report the last
  intact section boundary without trusting the manifest, so a torn/partially-written .nest is
  recoverable up to the last good section.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/reader/validate.rs and crates/nest-format/src/manifest/validate.rs,
            surfaced via crates/nest-cli/src/cmd/validate.rs. Additive, no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION
            bump.
    impact: Hardens the single-file/offline guarantee against crashes/torn writes (current weak spot for a
            single-file format with 4 SHA surfaces but no partial-recovery path); reuses existing
            per-section checksums.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/jzombie/rust-simd-r-drive

* Verify (not just declare) 64-byte alignment of the embeddings section payload at reader open,
  and assert it so the SIMD dispatcher can use aligned loads. The pad formula pad=(64-(off%64))&63
  is already implicit in SECTION_ALIGNMENT=64; add a read-side guard plus a padded/bounds-guarded
  tail so 64-byte SIMD scans never read past the mapped buffer.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/layout/mod.rs (SECTION_ALIGNMENT already 64, align_up exists) + reader open
            in crates/nest-format/src/reader/parse.rs; consumed by
            crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/{avx2,neon}.rs.
    impact: Precondition for aligned vmovaps loads and safe 64-byte structural scans off mmap with zero copy;
            validates the 'never zstd embeddings, score straight from disk' design and prevents
            read-past-end UB if SIMD structural scanning is added later.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/jzombie/rust-simd-r-drive, https://github.com/simdjson/simdjson

* Refactor the Encoding enum into a small visitor-style codec registry where each encoding id
  (raw/zstd/float16/int8 and future delta/varint/bitpack/dictionary) implements one
  decode(&[u8])->Cow view method in its own <300-line file, keeping the additive 4-255 encoding-id
  space clean and extensible without rewriting every section reader.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/mod.rs (decode_payload match) and
            crates/nest-format/src/sections/codec.rs; one file per codec under encoding/.
    impact: Makes the codec layer extensible - the prerequisite for adding delta/varint/bitpacking/dictionary
            codecs that push the compression frontier on chunk_ids/spans/provenance without
            touching readers; respects the 300-line/single-responsibility hygiene rule.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/serde-rs/serde

* Define a fixed-stride repr(C) u64 tape (type-tag in high bits + LE offset/length) plus a
  separate length-prefixed string/value blob as a new RESERVED section (id 0x09+) for ingested
  json/yaml structured documents, enabling pointer-free mmap reopen and sub-document citation
  (nest://content_hash/chunk_id#json_pointer) without re-parsing. Build it with a portable
  two-stage structural indexer at ingest time.
    where:  new module crates/nest-format/src/encoding/structure.rs + a new section file under
            crates/nest-format/src/sections/; ingest in nest-python build path
            (crates/nest-python/src/build_fn.rs) or a build-time path that may use
            serde_json/serde_yaml as a build-only dep (never in the frozen container).
    impact: Strategic enabler of the 'compress AND serve json/yaml/parquet/datasets' north star: low-entropy
            offset tape zstd-compresses extremely well as a non-embedding section and is cheaply
            re-scannable; keeps canonical text byte-exact for content_hash. Higher risk, land
            after the layout wins.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/simdjson/simdjson, https://github.com/simd-lite/simd-json

* Deduplicate the repeated source_uri in chunks_original_spans: store each distinct uri once in a
  small string pool and reference it by offset/index from each span (most spans in a corpus share
  one source document), and resolve absent optional provenance fields to schema defaults instead
  of storing nulls (FlatBuffers vtable-default trick).
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/spans.rs (OriginalSpan currently inlines a full length-prefixed
            source_uri per chunk) and crates/nest-format/src/sections/provenance.rs.
    impact: Direct size reduction on spans/provenance when many chunks share a source uri (the common case for
            chunked documents); pure entropy reduction, deterministic, zero hot-path cost.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/google/flatbuffers

* Add an in-process atomic-swap sidecar for real-time edit/hot-reload using mmap-sync's packed-u64
  {active-index|size|truncated-checksum} version word + double-buffer RCU drain, so a rebuilt
  .nest is published and queried wait-free while old mmap readers finish. Keep the frozen v1
  container immutable; the swap layer lives entirely outside the format and never opens a socket.
    where:  new module in crates/nest-runtime/src/ (e.g. swap.rs) layered over
            crates/nest-runtime/src/mmap_file.rs; surfaced to nest-python. Outside
            crates/nest-format entirely.
    impact: Delivers the stated-but-missing real-time edit / concurrent-serving mission goal without
            compromising single-file/offline/deterministic guarantees; the version-word checksum
            gives O(1) integrity gating per swap.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/cloudflare/mmap-sync, https://github.com/jzombie/rust-simd-r-drive

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - chunk_ids: store 32 raw SHA-256 bytes instead of 71-byte ASCII 'sha256:<64 hex>' - ~2.2x
    shrink pre-zstd and removes the redundant repeated 'sha256:' prefix; verified concrete in
    sections/chunk_ids.rs.
  - Structural/offset/provenance integers: FlexBuffers-style minimum-width + delta+varint packing
    (offsets and lengths are monotonic/low-entropy) trims non-embedding overhead with zero
    hot-path decode cost, stacking on the zstd nest already applies to non-embedding sections.
  - chunks_original_spans: dedup the per-chunk source_uri into a single string pool referenced by
    offset - large win when many chunks share one source document (the common chunking case).
  - Ingested json/yaml as a fixed-stride u64 offset tape + separate length-prefixed blob: offset
    deltas are textbook low-entropy and zstd-crush far better than raw nested text, while
    canonical text stays byte-exact for content_hash stability.
  - Offset-table layout makes per-cell/per-chunk lazy zstd decode possible (csv2
    decode-on-demand), so a compressed table/text section can be unpacked one chunk at a time
    instead of whole-section, lowering working-set memory during ingest and serve.
  - An extensible codec registry (one encoding id per file in the additive 4-255 space) is the
    prerequisite to later add delta/RLE/dictionary/bitpacking codecs - the column-oriented
    per-encoding path (rust-llkv/Arrow direction) is where extreme compression of parquet/datasets
    actually comes from.
  - Embeddings stay uncompressed by design (scored from mmap) - none of these wins touch the hot
    path; the 64-byte alignment guarantee is what KEEPS embeddings serveable at full SIMD speed
    while every cold section is squeezed.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Do NOT pull the serde crate into the frozen container: the format is repr(C) little-endian and
    must stay dependency-light and byte-deterministic. Steal the patterns (borrowed-view codec,
    encoding-driven Cow, N+M codec separation), not the dependency. serde_json/serde_yaml are
    acceptable only in nest-python or a build-time ingest path, never in the on-disk reader.
  - Do NOT use PCLMULQDQ/vmull_p64 carry-less multiply directly for the prefix-XOR trick if it
    could vary across CPUs - implement a portable parity/carry-less scan so structural indexing
    and tokenization stay bit-for-bit deterministic and reproducible (same chunks + model
    fingerprint => byte-identical file).
  - Any SIMD structural scanner needs input padding (SIMDJSON_PADDING) so 64-byte loads never read
    past the mmap buffer end; reading past the mapped region is UB. nest must pad the section tail
    or bounds-guard the final chunk - and never panic in library code (typed thiserror only).
  - Every new layout (offset tables, tapes, raw-digest chunk_ids, min-width ints, string dedup)
    must be additive within v1: use reserved encoding ids 4-255 and section ids 0x09+, and only
    bump NEST_FORMAT_VERSION for a true v2 where an existing field changes meaning. content_hash
    must keep hashing a stable canonical decoded representation so the chunk_ids encoding switch
    and offset tables do not break existing citations.
  - FlexBuffers/min-width packing trades read speed for size via per-value width-tag dispatch -
    apply it ONLY to cold structural/metadata sections, NEVER the embedding hot path, which must
    stay fixed-stride aligned for SIMD.
  - rkyv (used by mmap-sync) pins a specific version and brings unsafe archived-cast machinery;
    reimplement the lightweight archived-struct cast directly on nest's existing repr(C)+LE
    structs rather than adding the rkyv dependency, to keep the small dependency-light core.
  - csv2 and FlatBuffers are C++/Apache-2.0 and simd-r-drive/simd-json are Rust Apache/MIT - all
    permissive and safe to reimplement, but reimplement the algorithms in pure safe-where-possible
    Rust rather than vendoring C++, to preserve the single-binary, no-FFI, offline posture.
  - mmap-sync's RCU swap is single-writer/multi-reader and tmpfs-oriented; the real-time-edit
    sidecar must stay in-process and never open a socket (offline by construction), live entirely
    outside the frozen .nest container, and handle crashed-reader drain without blocking the read
    path.
  - Keep SHA-256 for the four integrity/citation surfaces - CRC32C (simd-r-drive) and truncated
    WyHash (mmap-sync) are cheaper but non-cryptographic and only acceptable as a fast-path
    liveness check during streaming edits, never as a replacement for the citation/integrity
    hashes.
  - File hygiene: every new codec/section/tape module must stay under 300 lines (333 hard cap),
    single-responsibility - split a codec registry into one file per encoding rather than growing
    encoding/mod.rs.


##################################################################################################
# ann-index  -  approximate nearest neighbor indexes and libraries
##################################################################################################

summary:     The ann-index field has converged on one dominant pattern that nest is architecturally primed to
             exploit: aggressive lossy vector quantization (down to 1 bit/dim) used purely as a
             candidate generator, paired with an exact rerank that recovers real distances. Nest
             already enforces that exact-cosine rerank in search_ann/search_hybrid (search.rs:
             idx.search -> score_subset -> sort), so the "risky" part of extreme quantization
             (lossy scores) is already neutralized. That makes the embeddings section -- nest's
             largest, currently floored at int8/4x because it must be SIMD-scored from mmap -- the
             single highest-leverage compression target in the whole project. The state of the art
             splits cleanly: (a) CODECS for the embeddings bytes (RaBitQ 1-bit with an unbiased
             estimator and zero codebook; TurboQuant 2/4-bit data-oblivious quantization scored in
             the compressed domain; PQ4/OPQ trained product quantization; NF4 fixed-codebook
             4-bit) and (b) GRAPH-LAYOUT efficiency for the HNSW section 0x07 (flat fixed-stride
             mmap-able records, uint40 neighbor ids, generational visited sets, Algorithm-4
             diversity pruning to lower M). Nest's current HNSW uses Vec<Vec<u32>> adjacency, a
             fresh HashSet per layer_search, the _simple top-m selection, and a scalar cosine_dist
             loop that bypasses its own AVX2/NEON dispatcher -- all of which the surveyed
             libraries fix. The clear ranking for nest: RaBitQ-style 1-bit codec first
             (deterministic, error-bounded, popcount-cheap, 8x over int8), then route HNSW through
             the existing SIMD dispatcher + generational visited + Algorithm-4
             (correctness/latency/recall with zero format risk), then a flat fixed-stride 0x07
             layout with uint40 ids, then PQ/OPQ as a higher-accuracy mid tier. Three watch-outs
             dominate: never vendor nightly portable_simd (kannolo/turbovec) or unmaintained
             packed_simd (hora) against nest's stable edition-2024; never adopt LMDB/serde/bincode
             persistence (arroy/hora/instant-distance) against the single-file frozen repr(C)
             constraint; and keep every new codec deterministic by seeding rotations/k-means from
             the model fingerprint and using stable tie-breaks so builds stay byte-identical.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The category has settled on "compress hard, score in the compressed domain, rerank exact."
Embedding codecs now reach 1 bit/dimension: RaBitQ (VSAG/Knowhere, SIGMOD 2024) random-rotates a
centroid-centered unit vector, takes sign bits, and recovers an UNBIASED inner-product estimate
with O(1/sqrt(D)) error using only AND+popcount over a 4-bit-decomposed query, needing zero
codebook training and storing only ~2 f32 scalars/vector -- the rare 1-bit scheme with a real
theoretical error bound (exactly what a deterministic, sovereign DB needs). TurboQuant (turbovec)
makes the rotation data-oblivious so quantizer boundaries are a pure function of (dim, bits): no
k-means, no per-corpus artifact, fully deterministic by construction, scored via FastScan
nibble-LUT SIMD. FAISS PQ4 FastScan packs 4-bit product-quantization codes in interleaved
32-vector blocks and evaluates distances with register-resident pshufb/vqtbl shuffles (no RAM
lookups), reaching ~280k QPS WITH rerank; OPQ/Hadamard rotations decorrelate dimensions to raise
recall per byte. NF4 (vectro) is a fixed 16-level normal-float codebook (deterministic, 8x). On
the graph side, hnswlib/USearch/instant-distance/rust-cv converge on a FLAT fixed-stride
single-allocation layout (one base+offset index, zero pointer-chasing, mmap-able), uint40 (5-byte)
neighbor ids to cut the dominant edge cost ~37.5%, generational/epoch-tagged visited sets to kill
per-query HashSet allocation, and the Malkov-Yashunin Algorithm-4 diversity-pruning heuristic that
raises recall at fixed degree -- letting M drop (smaller index) without losing recall, which
compounds beautifully with a mandatory exact rerank. Annoy/arroy show random-projection forests
compress far better under a generic second-pass entropy coder than HNSW (no back-edges), and a
headerless self-describing flat-array layout (count = byte_len/stride) eliminates offset tables.
kannolo/vectorium add the orthogonal lesson that index quality must be built on FULL-PRECISION
vectors while only stored payload is quantized (why they beat FAISS up to 11x on build), plus
Stream-VByte + recursive-graph-bisection reordering for sparse/BM25 postings. Universal contract
across the strong libraries: the compressed code drives candidate generation only; an exact pass
produces the returned score.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. RaBitQ 1-bit quantization with an unbiased, error-bounded (O(1/sqrt(D))) estimator scored by
   AND+popcount and NO codebook -- 8x over nest's int8, 32x over f32, deterministic via a seeded
   rotation, and its lossy nature is fully absorbed by nest's already-mandatory exact-cosine
   rerank. The single highest-value steal (VSAG, Knowhere).
2. Build the ANN graph on full-precision f32 while storing a quantized payload -- quantization
   never pollutes graph topology, only size. Nest already snapshots f32 into HnswIndex.vectors via
   attach_vectors, so it gets this for free and can quantize the embeddings section arbitrarily
   hard without degrading recall (kannolo/vectorium).
3. Route HNSW traversal through the existing AVX2/NEON/scalar dispatcher instead of the scalar
   cosine_dist loop in ann/mod.rs, and replace the per-query HashSet<u32> in layer_search with a
   generational visited buffer (Vec<u8>+epoch tag) -- pure latency win, zero format change, stays
   deterministic (hnswlib VisitedListPool, instant-distance Visited, USearch SIMD).
4. TurboQuant data-oblivious quantization: rotation+quantizer boundaries are a pure function of
   (dim, bits) so nothing per-corpus is stored and builds are byte-identical by construction -- a
   determinism-native path to 2/4-bit codes scored in the compressed domain (turbovec).
5. Flat fixed-stride single-allocation graph layout with uint40 (5-byte LE) neighbor ids -- makes
   section 0x07 directly mmap-traversable as &[u8] with zero decode/alloc and shrinks the edge
   arrays ~37.5% vs u64 / drops Vec<Vec> overhead vs nest's current adjacency (hnswlib, USearch,
   instant-distance, rust-cv).
6. Malkov-Yashunin Algorithm-4 diversity neighbor selection (keep a candidate only if it is closer
   to the query than to any already-chosen neighbor) -- nest's mod.rs already flags this as
   planned Phase 2; it raises recall at fixed M, so the tiny/nano presets can LOWER M and shrink
   the index without losing recall@10 (instant-distance, hnswlib, rust-cv).
7. PQ4 FastScan: 4-bit product-quantization codes in interleaved 32-vector blocks scored by
   register-resident pshufb/vqtbl shuffle LUTs (no RAM lookups), ~280k QPS with rerank -- a
   higher-accuracy mid tier between int8 and 1-bit (FAISS).
8. Hamming/binary navigation: pack sign bits 64 dims/u64 and traverse the HNSW graph on
   XOR+popcount instead of float cosine, ~order-of-magnitude cheaper per hop, recall recovered by
   exact rerank (rust-cv hnsw, USearch b1x8, vectro Binary).
9. Stream-VByte (delta-gap, 1 control-bit/elem) + recursive-graph-bisection term reordering for
   BM25/sparse postings -- compresses the 0x08 inverted index well below raw u32 pairs while
   staying deterministic via alphabetical tie-break (kannolo/vectorium).
10. Budget-driven codec inversion: solve codec+params from a target .nest size or recall floor (PQ
   cost = m+8 bytes/vec; compression = 4*dim/(pq+8)) instead of fixed presets, turning 'compress
   more than everyone' into an optimization (autofaiss).

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add a RaBitQ-style 1-bit embedding codec as a new additive encoding id (e.g. 4 = 'rabitq') with
  a corresponding manifest dtype. Store: u32 payload_version, a seeded rotation descriptor (seed
  derived from the model fingerprint -- store the seed, regenerate the rotation, or use a
  structured Hadamard rotate to avoid persisting a dense matrix), a shared centroid block, packed
  D sign bits row-major, plus the ~2 f32 side scalars per vector for the unbiased estimator. Score
  candidates via a new AND+popcount kernel, then feed the EXISTING exact-cosine rerank unchanged
  so the returned score stays real cosine.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/rabitq.rs (mirror int8.rs: versioned prefix + per-vector side data
            + packed codes); register the id in encoding/mod.rs decode_payload +
            expected_embeddings_size; add the variant to writer/encoding_choice.rs EmbeddingDType
            and layout/mod.rs (SECTION_ENCODING_RABITQ=4). New kernel
            crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/popcount.rs dispatched from simd/mod.rs; wire a new DType
            arm in mmap_file.rs + search.rs score_all/score_subset (candidate gen on codes, rerank
            on materialized f32/f16).
    impact: Highest. 8x smaller embeddings than int8, 32x vs f32, on nest's largest section -- pushes the tiny
            preset (0.283) toward a sub-0.1 'atom'/'micro' preset. Error-bounded and
            deterministic, so recall@10 stays near-lossless behind the mandatory rerank. Directly
            serves the north star.
    effort: high
    from:   antgroup/vsag (RaBitQ), zilliztech/knowhere (RaBitQ + refine_k rerank), konjoai/vectro (Binary
            path)

* Route HNSW traversal (greedy_search, layer_search, cosine_dist) through nest's existing simd
  dispatcher (vectors are L2-normalized so distance = 1 - dot, the kernel already on the rerank
  hot path), and replace the per-query HashSet<u32> in layer_search with a reusable generational
  visited buffer (Vec<u8> + u8/u16 generation counter, O(1) reset by bumping the tag). Pool the
  candidate/frontier heaps across queries.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/ann/mod.rs (cosine_dist -> simd::dot_f32_*), ann/search.rs (HashSet ->
            generational Visited; reuse heaps via a Searcher struct). No format change, no
            determinism change.
    impact: Pure latency/correctness win with zero format risk: kills per-query malloc + cache-cold HashSet
            rehash and stops the ANN path from silently bypassing SIMD. Closes the gap where exact
            rerank is SIMD but graph descent is scalar.
    effort: low
    from:   nmslib/hnswlib (VisitedListPool), djc/instant-distance (generational Visited), unum-cloud/usearch
            (SIMD descent)

* Implement the Malkov-Yashunin Algorithm-4 diversity neighbor selection (keep a candidate only if
  it is closer to the query than to every already-selected neighbor) to replace the current
  _simple top-m selection, which mod.rs already flags as planned Phase 2. Then lower DEFAULT_M for
  the tiny/nano presets and lean on the heuristic + exact rerank to hold recall@10.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/ann/select_neighbors.rs (add the heuristic variant), ann/build.rs (call it
            during insert). Determinism preserved with stable distance tie-breaks (by id).
    impact: Higher recall per edge -> fewer neighbor ids/node -> smaller 0x07 index at equal recall. Compounds
            with the flat-layout and the exact rerank: a direct lever on 'smallest container at
            fixed recall@10'.
    effort: medium
    from:   djc/instant-distance (select_heuristic), nmslib/hnswlib (getNeighborsByHeuristic2), rust-cv/hnsw

* Redesign the 0x07 HNSW payload from Vec<Vec<u32>>-style variable count+ids into a FLAT
  fixed-stride layout: a small LE header (m, m_max0, entry, max_level, n) then a contiguous
  level-0 block of fixed [count:u16, then m_max0 neighbor ids] records plus a sparse upper-layer
  block, with u32::MAX as the empty-slot sentinel. Adopt uint40 (repr(C) [u8;5] LE) for neighbor
  ids. Bump HNSW_PAYLOAD_VERSION only (NOT NEST_FORMAT_VERSION) since this is an internal section
  payload change.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/ann/codec.rs (to_bytes/from_bytes -> fixed-stride read of neighbor ids as
            offset slices off mmap), ann/mod.rs Node (fixed-width neighbor storage). Section stays
            raw on the index hot path but the regular byte structure also zstd-compresses better
            for tiny/hybrid presets (0x07 is NOT SIMD-scored, so it CAN be zstd'd unlike
            embeddings).
    impact: Drops per-Vec ptr/len/cap overhead, makes the graph mmap-zero-copy, and uint40 shrinks the
            often-second-largest section ~37.5%. Smaller index across all hnsw presets.
    effort: medium
    from:   unum-cloud/usearch (flat tape + uint40), nmslib/hnswlib (flat single-malloc level-0),
            djc/instant-distance (fixed-arity nodes), spotify/annoy (headerless count=len/stride)

* Add an 8-bit Product Quantization codec (with optional OPQ/Hadamard pre-rotation) as a
  mid-accuracy tier between int8 and 1-bit. Store a small deterministic codebook (seeded k-means,
  fixed Lloyd iterations, index-order tie-breaks) plus m-byte codes per vector. Use ADC
  (precompute a per-query [m x 256] distance table once, sum m LUT gathers per candidate) for
  candidate generation only; exact rerank still produces the returned score. Reimplement ADC on
  nest's own AVX2/NEON/scalar dispatcher -- do NOT vendor packed_simd/portable_simd.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/pq.rs (codec + codebook, <=300 loc), kernel in
            crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/pq.rs (ADC table build + 4-way-ILP accumulate). New
            encoding id (e.g. 5) + dtype, wired like int8 through encoding_choice.rs and
            search.rs.
    impact: 16-32x on embeddings at higher recall than 1-bit; gives a 'compressed-hybrid' tier and a
            budget-tunable knob. Codebook is tiny vs the per-vector savings.
    effort: high
    from:   facebookresearch/faiss (PQ4 FastScan, OPQ), hora-search/hora (PQ/IVFPQ + ADC), criteo/autofaiss
            (OPQ + budget inversion), TusKANNy/kannolo (8-bit PQ SoA ADC)

* Add a Stream-VByte (delta-gap + 1 control-bit/elem) codec for the BM25 0x08 postings and apply
  deterministic recursive-graph-bisection term reordering before encoding, storing the term-id
  permutation in the bm25 header and inverting at query time. Keeps the 'never compress
  embeddings' rule intact -- this only touches the sparse index. Optionally stack the existing
  zstd second pass.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/vbyte.rs (codec), crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/codec.rs (currently
            writes raw u32 doc/tf pairs sorted alphabetically -> delta-gap + vbyte), bm25/index.rs
            + a nest-python build step for the RGB permutation (alphabetical tie-break keeps
            builds byte-identical).
    impact: Meaningfully shrinks the BM25 section for hybrid/tiny presets without touching the recall contract
            or the embedding hot path. Reordering minimizes d-gaps so vbyte/zstd compress
            near-optimally.
    effort: medium
    from:   TusKANNy/kannolo + vectorium (DotVByteFixedU8 + rgbisect)

* Add a binary/Hamming HNSW navigation mode: pack embedding sign bits 64 dims/u64 as a
  navigation-only side array, traverse the existing graph on XOR+popcount instead of float cosine,
  then exact-rerank the candidate set on the materialized f32/f16. This is navigation compression
  (cheaper hops, smaller in-RAM nav vectors) distinct from the stored embedding codec.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/ann (build.rs/search.rs traverse on Hamming via simd/popcount.rs), reusing
            the kernel from the RaBitQ recommendation. Optional: store the 1-bit nav codes in 0x07
            or derive on open from the embeddings.
    impact: ~order-of-magnitude cheaper graph hops (12 u64 popcounts vs 768 FMAs for a 768-d vector) and a
            smaller navigation footprint; recall recovered by rerank. Enables a 'nano' preset
            (binary nav + f16 rerank).
    effort: medium
    from:   rust-cv/hnsw (Hamming traversal, hamming-heap), unum-cloud/usearch (b1x8), meilisearch/arroy
            (sign-bit + popcount)

* Add a budget->codec inversion in the nest-python builder: user passes a target .nest size ratio
  or a recall floor and the builder deterministically solves dtype/codec + HNSW M/ef parameters
  (PQ: m from 4*dim/(m+8) >= target ratio; nlist heuristics; binary-search ef_search to a recall
  floor measured against exact). Replaces or augments the fixed exact/compressed/tiny/hybrid
  presets.
    where:  crates/nest-python/src/build_fn.rs (param solver feeding writer/encoding_choice.rs), validated
            against the exact search() ground truth already in nest-runtime/src/search.rs.
    impact: Turns 'compress more than everyone' from fixed presets into a solvable optimization; lets users
            dial size vs recall directly. Determinism preserved since the solve is a pure function
            of corpus stats + seeds.
    effort: medium
    from:   criteo/autofaiss (PQ-budget inversion, nlist=4*sqrt(N), binary-search ef tuner)

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - RaBitQ 1-bit embedding codec: 8x smaller than nest's current int8 floor and 32x vs f32 on the
    largest section, with an unbiased error bound and a popcount hot path -- the clearest single
    path past the tiny preset's 0.283 ratio toward sub-0.1. Lossiness is free against the recall
    contract because nest already mandates exact-cosine rerank.
  - PQ8/OPQ mid tier: 16-32x on embeddings (m=32 on a 768-d vector is ~96x raw, ~24x vs int8) via
    tiny shared codebooks + m-byte codes, scored by ADC for candidate gen and exact-reranked -- a
    higher-recall option than 1-bit for a 'compressed-hybrid' preset.
  - TurboQuant 2/4-bit data-oblivious codec: 16x at 2-bit (6144B f32 -> 384B for d=1536), scored
    directly in the compressed domain off mmap, with NOTHING per-corpus stored so builds stay
    byte-identical -- breaks the 'cannot compress the mmap-scored embeddings' wall without adding
    a stateful trained artifact.
  - uint40 neighbor ids + flat fixed-stride 0x07 layout: ~37.5% off the HNSW edge arrays plus
    elimination of Vec<Vec> ptr/len/cap overhead; the regular byte structure also zstd-compresses
    better (0x07 is not on the SIMD hot path), shrinking tiny/hybrid further.
  - Algorithm-4 diversity neighbor selection lets the tiny/nano presets LOWER M (fewer u32/uint40
    ids per node) at equal recall@10 -- a compression win that costs only build-time CPU and rides
    entirely on the existing exact rerank.
  - Stream-VByte + recursive-graph-bisection on BM25 0x08 postings: replaces raw u32 doc/tf pairs
    with delta-gap byte-packed postings whose d-gaps are minimized by term reordering -- shrinks
    the hybrid preset's lexical index, and the existing zstd second pass compounds it, all without
    touching embeddings or the recall contract.
  - Binary/Hamming navigation vectors (1-bit, 64 dims/u64) as a navigation-only side
    representation: shrinks the in-RAM nav footprint ~32x vs the f32 snapshot and makes graph hops
    popcount-cheap, enabling a 'nano' preset (binary nav + f16/RaBitQ rerank embeddings).

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - NIGHTLY-ONLY SIMD: kannolo/vectorium use std::simd (portable_simd) and gen blocks; hora uses
    packed_simd_2 (unmaintained, nightly-only). Both break nest's stable Rust edition-2024 build.
    Reimplement every kernel (PQ ADC, popcount, FastScan) on nest's existing AVX2/NEON/scalar
    dispatcher; vendor the ALGORITHM, never the SIMD layer.
  - EXTERNAL STORAGE ENGINES: arroy is built on LMDB (heed), hora/instant-distance use
    serde+bincode blobs. These violate nest's single-file frozen-format and
    offline-by-construction constraints. Keep every structure as repr(C) little-endian inside
    nest's own sections; do not pull in a KV store or a serde graph dump.
  - DETERMINISM OF QUANTIZERS: any trained codec (PQ/OPQ k-means, RaBitQ/TurboQuant rotation) must
    be a pure function of the model fingerprint -- fix the seed, Lloyd iteration count, sampling,
    and use index-order/alphabetical tie-breaks, and store codebooks/seeds in a stable order.
    Otherwise same chunks + same model no longer yields a byte-identical file (breaks
    nest://content_hash citations and reproducibility).
  - NEVER ZSTD THE EMBEDDINGS: USearch/hnswlib interleave the raw vector INTO the graph record,
    and several libs lean on generic entropy coders over vectors. Nest's embeddings must stay
    directly mmap-SIMD-scored, so any embedding shrink must be a quantization codec scored
    in-place (RaBitQ/PQ/TurboQuant), never a zstd wrapper. Steal the FLAT GRAPH LAYOUT for
    neighbor IDS ONLY -- keep embeddings in their own section.
  - ARM NEON PARITY: FAISS PQ4 FastScan and several popcount/shuffle kernels are
    AVX2/AVX512-tuned; AVX2 lacks VPOPCNTDQ (use the Mula nibble-LUT pshufb popcount) and the
    256-bit FastScan needs the two-128-bit-NEON-registers trick (Matsui arXiv:2203.02505) to match
    on aarch64. Nest treats NEON as first-class, so every new kernel needs a real NEON path, not a
    scalar fallback that emulates vector registers (very slow).
  - SCALAR FALLBACK MUST STAY SIMPLE: FAISS notes the block/interleaved FastScan layout is
    pathological for scalar (it emulates vector registers in memory). Nest's scalar tier should
    keep a plain unpacked PQ/popcount loop, not the SIMD block layout, so the scalar-fallback
    target stays correct and not catastrophically slow.
  - 300-LINE FILE CAP / SINGLE RESPONSIBILITY: each new codec and kernel must split cleanly (codec
    in nest-format/src/encoding/<name>.rs, kernel in nest-runtime/src/simd/<name>.rs) to respect
    the <=300 (333 hard cap) line rule and typed thiserror no-panic convention -- do not port
    monolithic C++ index files wholesale.
  - ADDITIVE-ONLY DISCIPLINE: new embedding codecs take RESERVED encoding ids 4-255 and new ANN
    backends take section ids 0x09+; internal graph changes bump HNSW_PAYLOAD_VERSION only. Do NOT
    bump NEST_FORMAT_VERSION (that is reserved for a true v2 where an existing field changes
    meaning). A naive port that rewrites the header/footer would violate the FROZEN format
    guarantee.


##################################################################################################
# vector-quantization  -  vector quantization and bit-compression of embeddings (the compression key for vectors)
##################################################################################################

summary:     The whole category converges on one move that is nearly tailor-made for nest: RaBitQ (SIGMOD 2024,
             NTU) replaces trained PQ/OPQ codebooks with a fixed, deterministic codebook
             (sign-corners of the unit sphere) plus a single shared random orthogonal rotation P,
             giving 1 bit/dim plus ~2 f32 correction scalars per vector (~32x vs f32, ~8x vs
             nest's current int8 floor) AND a distance estimator that is provably unbiased with a
             sharp O(1/sqrt(D)) error bound, computed as bitwise-AND + popcount straight off mmap
             with no decompression. It is patent-free, openly published, and reimplementable
             clean-room in Rust. The bitsandbytes/INC/llm-compressor cluster supplies the
             complementary mid-tier: codebook (NF4 quantile-optimal for Gaussian-shaped normalized
             embeddings), block/group-wise absmax scaling, microscale block-float (MXFP4 E2M1 +
             power-of-two E8M0 scale that decodes by exponent-add, no multiply), and build-time
             accuracy recovery (GPTQ inverse-Hessian error compensation, AutoRound learned
             rounding). These are the 4-bit tier that sits between int8 and RaBitQ's 1-bit. nest
             is unusually well positioned to adopt all of this because (a) the format is
             additive-only with encoding ids 4-255 RESERVED in v1, so new codecs need no version
             bump, (b) the runtime already mandates a rerank stage, and (c) rotation/codebook
             determinism maps cleanly onto seeded reproducible builds. CRITICAL FINDING confirmed
             by reading the code: nest's current rerank in
             crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs::score_subset reranks against the SAME lossy dtype
             stored on disk (int8/f16), NOT against full-precision f32. The search_contract
             docstring claims 'real cosine, never an ann proxy', but for quantized presets the
             returned score already carries quantization error. Aggressive 1-bit/4-bit
             quantization makes this latent gap material: to keep the contract honest, nest must
             either keep a full-precision (or f16) shadow copy of survivors for rerank, or accept
             that 'exact cosine' means 'exact at the stored precision'. This decision gates the
             entire category and must be made before shipping a rabitq/nf4 preset.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
As of 2024-2025 the frontier for vector-byte compression splits into two regimes. (1) Sub-byte
randomized quantization: RaBitQ and its B-bit extension are the state of the art for reaching ~1
bit/dim while preserving recall, because the random orthogonal rotation (Johnson-Lindenstrauss)
turns naive sign quantization into an UNBIASED estimator with an O(1/sqrt(D)) error bound that
matches the Alon-Klartag FOCS17 lower bound (asymptotically optimal). The estimator <o,q> ~=
<o_bar,q>/<o_bar,o> reduces to AND+popcount against a 4-bit query, ~3x faster than PQ table
lookups at equal accuracy, and the B-bit dial (1/4/5/7 bits via an 'ex-code' refinement) reaches
~90/95/99% recall with NO rerank. Library maturity is high (VectorDB-NTU CPU/GPU, cuVS IVF-RaBitQ
fork reporting >2.7x throughput over IVF-PQ). (2) Codebook + block-scaling at 4 bits: from the
LLM-weight world, NF4 (quantile-optimal 16-level codebook for N(0,1) data), blockwise absmax
scaling (block=32/64, one scale per block absorbs outliers), double/nested quantization of the
scales (~0.4 bits/param reclaimed), microscale block-float MXFP4 (E2M1 nibble + shared E8M0
power-of-two scale, decode = exponent add) and NVFP4 (two-level E4M3 fp8 scale per 16). Accuracy
at 4 bits is recovered to ~99% of FP32 by build-time-only passes: GPTQ (inverse-Hessian error
compensation, one Cholesky over the calibration covariance) and AutoRound/SignRound
(SignSGD-learned per-weight rounding offset, fixed ~200 steps, zero inference overhead). The
unifying principle across both regimes: separate storage dtype from compute dtype, keep the dot
product in f32, and let the data's own distribution (Gaussian for L2-normalized embeddings) or
geometry (random rotation) dictate where the few code points go. For an L2-normalized-embedding
database with a rerank stage, RaBitQ is the clear headline lever and NF4/MXFP4 are the safe 4-bit
intermediate.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. RaBitQ 1-bit randomized quantizer: rotate by a shared seeded orthogonal P, store sign(P^-1
   (o-c)) as D bits + 2 f32 scalars (<o_bar,o> and ||o-c||). ~32x vs f32, ~8x vs nest int8,
   distance = AND+popcount off mmap. THE headline compression lever for nest's embeddings section.
2. Unbiased estimator with provable O(1/sqrt(D)) error bound: turns ANN distance into a quantity
   with a confidence interval, so nest's search_contract can cite a recall GUARANTEE, not just an
   empirical number. This is what makes 1-bit safe where naive binary quantization collapses.
3. Asymmetric bit allocation: data side 1 bit/dim, query side 4 bits/dim (4 bit-planes). Error is
   dominated by data quantization, so query precision is cheap insurance; distance = sum_k 2^k *
   popcount(code & q_plane_k).
4. B-bit ex-code refinement dial (RaBitQ extended, Sep-2024): one codec spanning 1/4/5/7 bits
   (~90/95/99% recall no-rerank). Gives nest a single tunable knob from a 'nano' preset up to
   near-lossless, replacing the discrete float32/float16/int8 ladder.
5. NF4 quantile-optimal codebook: 16 reconstruction levels at the quantiles of N(0,1), strictly
   better than uniform int8 for L2-normalized embeddings whose per-dim components are ~zero-mean
   Gaussian. 4 bits/dim, near-lossless, dequant is a 16-entry LUT gather (AVX2 vpermps / NEON
   vqtbl).
6. Blockwise absmax + double quantization: per-block (32/64) scale bounds error and absorbs
   outliers; quantizing the scales themselves reclaims ~0.4 bits/dim. The reason int4 is viable
   where flat int4 collapses.
7. MXFP4 microscale block-float: E2M1 4-bit element + shared E8M0 power-of-two scale per 32 dims.
   Decode is an EXPONENT-ADD, not a multiply, which is the cheapest possible dequant on nest's
   SIMD hot path.
8. GPTQ inverse-Hessian error compensation (build-time only): quantize dims left-to-right, push
   each rounding error onto not-yet-quantized dims weighted by H^-1 of the embedding covariance.
   One Cholesky (O(d^3), trivial at d=384/768/1024). Recovers most of the recall lost at 4 bits,
   zero runtime cost.
9. AutoRound/SignRound learned rounding (build-time only): SignSGD over per-dim rounding offset in
   [-0.5,0.5], fixed seeded ~200 steps. Deterministic, zero inference overhead, free recall
   recovery at 4 bits.
10. FastScan batched layout: reorganize 32 codes at index time for SIMD lane-parallel
   popcount/shuffle scan (AVX2/AVX512 VPOPCNTDQ, NEON vcnt+vaddv). Makes the 1-bit scan
   memory-bandwidth-bound rather than compute-bound.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add a `rabitq.rs` codec under crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ as encoding id 4 (additive in the
  RESERVED 4-255 space, NO NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump). Layout mirrors int8.rs: u32 payload_version,
  u32 variant/Bq, a single deterministic rotation SEED (store the seed, never the dense matrix),
  the global centroid c once, then per vector D/8 packed sign bits as LE u64 words + 2 LE f32
  factors (<o_bar,o>, ||o-c||). Add EmbeddingDType::RaBitQ + 'rabitq' manifest_str in
  writer/encoding_choice.rs and expected_embeddings_size in encoding/mod.rs.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/rabitq.rs (new, keep <300 lines), encoding/mod.rs (pub use +
            expected size + decode_payload passthrough like int8), writer/encoding_choice.rs (new
            enum arm), layout/mod.rs (SECTION_ENCODING_RABITQ=4)
    impact: Highest-leverage compression in nest: embeddings section (the dominant payload) drops from int8's
            ~1 byte/dim to ~1 bit/dim + 8 bytes/vec, ~8x over int8 and ~32x over f32. Directly the
            biggest single move on the 'compress more than everyone' north star for vector bytes.
    effort: high
    from:   gaoj0017/RaBitQ, VectorDB-NTU/RaBitQ-Library, Stardust-SJF/cuvs_rabitq

* Add a RaBitQ AND+popcount distance kernel beside the existing dot dispatchers in the simd
  module. New rabitq.rs with NEON (vcnt + vaddv), AVX2 (popcount over 4 query bit-planes;
  VPOPCNTDQ when detected), and a scalar u64::count_ones fallback, wired through detect_backend()
  exactly like dot_f32_i8. Scores codes straight off mmap as the cheap ANN/coarse stage. Extend
  score_all/score_subset in search.rs with a DType::RaBitQ arm.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/rabitq.rs (new) + simd/mod.rs dispatch arm,
            crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs (DType::RaBitQ in score_all/score_subset),
            mmap_file.rs DType enum
    impact: Makes the 1-bit codec actually scorable on the hot path with no decompression (the property zstd
            lacked), at ~3x PQ speed; 1-bit codes touch ~8x fewer bytes/vector than int8, so the
            mmap scan becomes bandwidth-light.
    effort: high
    from:   gaoj0017/RaBitQ, VectorDB-NTU/RaBitQ-Library

* Decouple rerank precision from storage precision: keep a full-precision (f32) or f16 SHADOW copy
  of embeddings available so score_subset reranks survivors against true precision, not the lossy
  stored dtype. Either (a) store both rabitq codes AND an f16 embeddings blob (still ~17x smaller
  total than f32, exact-on-rerank), or (b) explicitly redefine the search_contract to say 'exact
  cosine at stored precision' and surface dtype in SearchHit. This resolves the confirmed gap
  where score_subset currently reranks int8 against int8.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs (score_subset rerank source),
            crates/nest-format/src/sections/contract.rs + search_contract docs, decode.rs
            content_hash semantics
    impact: Keeps nest's headline 'returned score is real cosine, never an ann proxy' guarantee honest under
            aggressive quantization. Without this, the 1-bit/4-bit presets quietly degrade the
            contract. This is the gating design decision for the whole category.
    effort: medium
    from:   gaoj0017/RaBitQ, vllm-project/llm-compressor

* Add an NF4/quantile codebook codec as encoding id 5: per vector [block_scales | packed 4-bit
  indices], block=32, with a single shared 16-entry f32 codebook (fixed NF4 constants OR a
  seeded/sorted quantile estimate over the corpus) stored once in the section header.
  Double-quantize the block scales (group=256 to int8) to shave scale overhead. Dequant kernel =
  16-entry LUT gather (AVX2 vpermps / NEON vqtbl) * block absmax, then the existing SIMD dot.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/nf4.rs (new), encoding/mod.rs, writer/encoding_choice.rs,
            crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/nf4.rs (new dequant-and-dot kernel)
    impact: 4-bit tier between int8 and rabitq: ~0.5 byte/dim, near-lossless because the codebook matches the
            Gaussian shape of normalized embedding components. A safer intermediate preset and a
            fallback if RaBitQ recall is unsatisfactory on the PT-BR corpus.
    effort: high
    from:   bitsandbytes-foundation/bitsandbytes, intel/neural-compressor

* Add a 'nano' preset (zstd text / rabitq embeddings / hnsw) and a 'micro' preset (zstd / nf4 /
  hnsw) to the preset resolver, below the existing 'tiny'. Expose the B-bit dial as a build kwarg
  (rabitq_bits=1|4|5|7). Pair rabitq codes with the existing HnswIndex so the popcount scan
  feeds/replaces the HNSW shortlist.
    where:  crates/nest-python/src/build_fn.rs (preset match arms + new dtype/bits kwargs), crates/nest-cli
            build surface
    impact: Turns the codec work into a user-facing one-flag win and pushes tiny's 0.283 ratio toward sub-0.1
            on embedding-dominated corpora. The bits dial gives one tunable knob spanning
            nano->near-lossless.
    effort: low
    from:   VectorDB-NTU/RaBitQ-Library, gaoj0017/RaBitQ

* Add a build-time-only GPTQ/AutoRound accuracy-recovery pass in the python builder, run before
  quantizing to 4-bit codes: compute X^T X once over corpus embeddings, Cholesky inverse,
  error-compensated rounding (or SignSGD learned rounding, fixed seed + fixed step count).
  Produces better int4/nf4 codes; pure build-time, zero runtime cost, keeps the rust runtime
  offline and deterministic.
    where:  crates/nest-python (builder quantization stage, before encode), feeding the nf4/int4 codec bytes;
            no rust runtime change
    impact: Recovers most of the recall lost at 4 bits (INC reports ~99% of FP32), making the micro/nano
            presets viable without a precision shadow copy. Free quality at build time.
    effort: medium
    from:   vllm-project/llm-compressor, intel/neural-compressor, bitsandbytes-foundation/bitsandbytes

* Add an MXFP4-style block-float codec (E2M1 nibble + per-32 E8M0 power-of-two scale) as a third
  sub-byte option, chosen specifically because its decode is an exponent-add not a multiply,
  minimizing hot-path dequant cost during rerank. Nibble-unpack is a shift+mask, vectorizable on
  AVX2/NEON.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/mxfp4.rs (new), crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/mxfp4.rs
            dequant-and-dot
    impact: ~4x over f32 with the cheapest possible dequant; a principled float sub-byte path alternative to
            integer NF4. Useful if NF4's LUT gather proves slower than exponent-add on aarch64.
    effort: medium
    from:   intel/neural-compressor, vllm-project/llm-compressor

* Implement the rotation P as a Fast Hadamard Transform (FHT) variant rather than a dense D*D
  matmul, so per-query inverse rotation P^-1 q is O(D log D) and per-vector build-time rotation is
  cheap. Store only the seed + sign-flip pattern. Keeps the codec file small and the build
  deterministic.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/rabitq.rs (rotation helper) shared with crates/nest-runtime
            query-side rotation in search.rs/simd
    impact: Avoids an O(D^2) dense rotation per query and a D*D matrix in memory; matters at D=768/1024/1536.
            Keeps the codec mmap-light and the build fast on brenner's minimal hardware.
    effort: medium
    from:   Stardust-SJF/cuvs_rabitq, VectorDB-NTU/RaBitQ-Library

* Group-wise int8 (per-group fp16 scale, group=16/32) as the lowest-risk first step before
  committing to 1-bit/4-bit: a tiny upgrade to the existing int8.rs that swaps per-vector scale
  for per-group scale. Lands recall improvement and de-risks the layout/scale plumbing the harder
  codecs reuse.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/int8.rs (add scale_kind=1 per-group, additive within the existing
            int8 encoding), simd/mod.rs score_int8_section group-aware
    impact: Immediate recall win at the same ~4x ratio, and proves out the contiguous per-group-scale layout
            that nf4/mxfp4/rabitq all depend on. Low blast radius.
    effort: low
    from:   intel/neural-compressor, vllm-project/llm-compressor

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - RaBitQ 1-bit embeddings: 768-dim f32 vector 3072 bytes -> ~768 bits + 8 bytes = ~104 bytes,
    ~30x on embedding bytes (~0.034 ratio), vs int8's 4x. 1024-dim: 4096 -> ~136 bytes. The single
    largest available lever on nest's headline ratio since embeddings dominate compressed-preset
    file size.
  - ~8x cut specifically over nest's current floor (int8 at 1 byte/dim -> ~1 bit/dim), pushing the
    'tiny' 0.283 ratio toward sub-0.1 on embedding-heavy corpora, while staying directly scorable
    from mmap via popcount (no decompression on the hot path, the property that banned zstd on
    embeddings).
  - NF4 / quantile codebook at 4 bits/dim: ~0.5 byte/dim (half of int8), near-lossless on
    L2-normalized embeddings because the codebook sits at N(0,1) quantiles. A safe intermediate
    that beats int8 with one LUT gather of dequant cost.
  - MXFP4 block-float: ~4x over f32 (4 bits/dim + one E8M0 byte per 32 dims) with
    decode-by-exponent-add, the cheapest sub-byte dequant for the rerank path.
  - Double/nested quantization of block scales reclaims ~0.4 bits/dim of scale overhead, and
    storing the rotation as a SEED (not a D*D matrix) keeps the rabitq codec header to a few bytes
    regardless of dimension.
  - B-bit ex-code dial gives a continuous size/recall tradeoff (1/4/5/7 bits) from one codec,
    letting nest ship a 'nano' preset and a near-lossless preset from the same code path instead
    of discrete f32/f16/int8 tiers.
  - Combined stack potential: zstd on text sections + RaBitQ on embeddings + HNSW pruned by
    popcount can plausibly take a vector-dominated corpus well below the current 0.283 tiny ratio
    while the rerank (against a precision shadow) keeps returned scores honest.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - CONTRACT INTEGRITY (highest): confirmed in code that search.rs::score_subset reranks against
    the SAME lossy stored dtype, not full-precision f32. The search_contract claims 'real cosine,
    never an ann proxy', but quantized presets already violate the spirit of that. Shipping
    1-bit/4-bit without either a precision shadow copy or a redefined contract turns the headline
    guarantee into a half-truth. Decide this first.
