  - CONTENT_HASH STABILITY: nest's content_hash is documented as STABLE across encodings, but for
    quantized embeddings it hashes the on-disk bytes (per decode.rs). A RaBitQ file and an f32
    file of the same corpus will NOT share content_hash, so citations nest://content_hash/chunk_id
    are tied to a specific quantization. This is acceptable but must be documented; do not claim
    cross-precision citation stability.
  - DETERMINISM of the rotation: RaBitQ determinism hinges on a seeded orthogonal P AND identical
    floating-point ops across the three SIMD backends. If P^-1 q or the rotation matmul is
    computed with FMA on AVX2 but separate mul/add on scalar, sign() can flip near zero, breaking
    'same chunks => byte-identical codes' and the reproducible-build guarantee. The codec
    generation (build-time) must be backend-independent; only the popcount scan may vary.
  - AutoRound/GPTQ determinism: SignSGD and Cholesky must use fixed seed, fixed iteration count,
    fixed column/group order (nest already mandates alphabetical/index order). Any
    nondeterministic BLAS threading in the python builder would break reproducibility. Pin it.
  - NO-PANIC RULE: every new codec parse path (rabitq/nf4/mxfp4) must return typed thiserror
    NestError on malformed payloads (bad payload_version, size mismatch, unknown variant),
    mirroring Int8EmbeddingsView::parse. No unwrap on untrusted mmap bytes. The unsafe i8/u8
    bitcast pattern in int8.rs is fine but the bit-unpacking must bounds-check section length
    first.
  - FILE HYGIENE: <=300 lines/file (333 hard cap), single-responsibility. RaBitQ encode + B-bit
    ex-code + rotation will not fit one file; split codec (format), kernel (runtime simd), and
    rotation helper. Do not bloat encoding/mod.rs.
  - SMALL-CORE / DEPENDENCY CREEP: do NOT vendor Eigen/cuVS/bitsandbytes; all are CUDA/C++ and
    would violate the offline single-file Rust runtime. Reimplement only the (patent-free,
    published) algorithm in pure Rust. The python builder may use numpy for the build-time
    GPTQ/quantile pass, but the rust runtime must stay dependency-light and socket-free.
  - QUERY-SIDE ROTATION COST: P^-1 q is one O(D^2) op per query (or O(D log D) with FHT) amortized
    over the probe list. For nest's small/embedded corpora the probe list may be short, so the
    rotation could dominate per-query latency. Benchmark vs the int8 flat scan before declaring a
    speed win; the compression win is real regardless, the speed win is corpus-size-dependent.
  - LICENSE diligence: RaBitQ algorithm is patent-free and openly published (SIGMOD 2024, arXiv
    2405.12497); upstream code is MIT/Apache but one extract could not confirm the library license
    from the README. nest only needs the algorithm (clean-room Rust), so this is a reference
    concern, but record the citation/DOI in provenance rather than copying code.
  - BM25/CJK gap is orthogonal but interacts: aggressive vector quantization makes the lexical
    path relatively more important for the PT-BR (and any CJK) corpora; don't let a 1-bit vector
    preset ship without bm25 in the hybrid path as a recall backstop.


##################################################################################################
# vector-db  -  vector databases and sqlite vector/columnar extensions
##################################################################################################

summary:     The entire vector-db field has converged on ONE pattern that nest already half-implements:
             rotate-then-quantize embeddings to 1-4 bits, score the tiny codes from mmap with
             integer/bitwise SIMD as a cheap candidate stage, then rerank a small over-fetched set
             against full-precision originals to return a true score. nest's mandatory
             exact-cosine rerank IS the rescore stage every one of these systems (qdrant,
             milvus/knowhere, chroma, weaviate, lance, pgvector, VectorChord,
             sqlite-vec/vector/vss) bolts on for safety; nest is therefore uniquely pre-positioned
             to adopt the most aggressive quantizers without a recall penalty. The frontier splits
             into two families. (1) DATA-OBLIVIOUS rotation quantizers (TurboQuant, RaBitQ,
             Weaviate RQ): a seeded fast Walsh-Hadamard / random orthogonal rotation makes any
             embedding's coordinates near-iid, so a single compile-time scalar codebook or a sign
             bit suffices -- NO trained codebook, NO corpus statistics. This family is the perfect
             fit for nest because seeded rotation keeps builds byte-identical/deterministic, which
             trained product quantization (milvus FastScan, lance PQ) fundamentally cannot
             guarantee without pinning k-means seeds and iteration order. (2) Beyond quantization,
             lance contributes the transparent-vs-opaque encoding taxonomy and byte-stream-split
             (the conceptual framework nest needs to add aggressive codecs without breaking the
             mmap hot path), plus FSST for O(1)-random-access text compression that preserves
             citation spans where block-zstd would destroy them; sqlite-columnar contributes
             zone-map block-skipping that can make nest's exact scan sublinear while still
             returning TRUE cosine. The decisive architectural finding from reading nest's own
             code: today score_subset reranks against the SAME embeddings section it scored, so
             once embeddings are stored at 1-2 bits the 'exact rerank' is no longer exact.
             Shipping sub-int8 quantization therefore REQUIRES a dual-section layout -- a tiny
             coarse-codes section for candidate generation plus a separate full-precision (or f16)
             rerank section -- which is itself a compression win because the coarse section can be
             brutally small. Highest-leverage path: RaBitQ/TurboQuant 1-2 bit codes as a new
             additive encoding id on a coarse embeddings sub-section, popcount/LUT SIMD kernels
             beside the existing dot kernels, a 'nano' preset well below the 0.283 'tiny' floor,
             with the existing f32/f16 embeddings kept as the rerank source.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Convergent SOTA: a two-stage retrieve-then-rerank pipeline where lossy rotation-quantized vectors
live hot (mmap/page-cache) and full-precision originals are touched only to rescore a small
over-fetched candidate set. The compression frontier is rotation-based low-bit quantization in two
families. Data-oblivious (deterministic, the one nest needs): seeded fast Walsh-Hadamard /
structured random rotation in O(d log d) makes coordinates near-iid so a fixed/compile-time
codebook or a bare sign bit is near-optimal -- RaBitQ 1-bit (~28x, unbiased O(1/sqrt(D))
estimator, AND+popcount ~111ns/code), Weaviate RQ 8-bit (4x, 98-99% recall, decomposed int8 dot),
TurboQuant 1/2/4-bit (compile-time Lloyd-Max codebook, asymmetric full-precision query, SIMD
nibble LUT). Trained (higher recall, NOT determinism-friendly): milvus/knowhere FastScan 4-bit PQ
with in-register LUT (pshufb/vpermb/vqtbl1q) + anisotropic ranking loss, ~5x QPS, 8-64x. Multi-bit
query bit-planes keep DATA at 1 bit while tuning query precision. Adjacent: lance per-buffer
compression cascade with transparent (O(1) access) vs opaque (decode-required) taxonomy,
byte-stream-split, FSST; sqlite-columnar zone-map chunk-skipping (248-273x on range queries).
Universal safety net is an exact full-precision refine/rerank (qdrant rescore, knowhere reorder_k,
weaviate rescoreLimit, lance refine_factor, pgvector ORDER BY exact) -- identical to nest's
mandatory exact-cosine rerank, which makes aggressive quantization safe and validates nest's
contract as prior art. Critical caveat from nest's own code: the rerank currently reads the same
section it scored, so sub-int8 storage requires a separate full-precision rerank source to keep
returns true-cosine.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. RaBitQ 1-bit quantization (chroma/VectorChord, SIGMOD 2024): seeded rotation + sign bit + 2
   corrective f32 scalars, unbiased O(1/sqrt(D)) estimator, ~28x compression, AND+popcount off
   mmap, data-oblivious so byte-identical builds -- highest-leverage codec for nest.
2. TurboQuant (qdrant/sqlite-vector/sqlite-vec, Google arXiv:2504.19874): Hadamard rotation +
   compile-time Lloyd-Max const codebook + asymmetric scoring, 1/2/4-bit, no training, beats int8
   at half the bytes, scored via SIMD nibble LUT with zero decompression.
3. Weaviate 8-bit Rotational Quantization: rotate-then-int8 with decomposed dot, a drop-in upgrade
   of nest's int8.rs pushing recall@10 0.992 -> ~0.998 at the same 4x size; seed derivable from
   the existing model fingerprint.
4. Mandatory exact refine/rerank as a universal contract (all repos): validates nest's existing
   exact-cosine rerank as prior art and is what makes aggressive lossy quantization safe.
5. Multi-bit query bit-plane trick (VectorChord/TurboQuant): store at 1 bit/dim, tune recall on
   the query side -- decouples file size from query precision.
6. FastScan in-register LUT product quantization (milvus/knowhere/ScaNN, MLR 2020) + anisotropic
   loss: highest recall-per-bit, 8-64x, but trained k-means breaks determinism -- adopt only with
   pinned seeds.
7. Lance transparent-vs-opaque encoding taxonomy + byte-stream-split: the framework to add
   aggressive codecs without breaking the mmap hot path; BSS for high-ratio cold embedding
   archives.
8. FSST transparent text compression (lance): ~2x over raw text while preserving O(1)
   citation-span access that block-zstd destroys.
9. Zone-map block-skipping (sqlite-columnar): per-block centroid+radius prunes whole blocks before
   the SIMD scan, making the exact path pruned-exact while still returning true cosine.
10. Validity-bitmap tombstones + byte-identical dedup (sqlite-vec/pgvector): deletes and dedup
   without rewriting embeddings -- foundation for the real-time-edit north star; sqlite-vss
   monolithic-blob is the cautionary counter-example.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add a dual-section quantized-embeddings layout: a new RESERVED encoding id 4 = 'rabitq1' storing
  per-vector [sign-code dim/8 bytes][2 f32 corrective scalars] in a coarse embeddings sub-section,
  while keeping the existing f32 (or f16) embeddings as a separate full-precision rerank source.
  This is the structural prerequisite for ALL sub-int8 quantization, because today score_subset
  reranks against the SAME section it scored -- so 1-bit codes alone would make the 'exact' rerank
  no longer exact and silently break the 'returned score is real cosine' contract.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/rabitq.rs (new, <=300 lines, mirror int8.rs layout); register id 4
            in layout/mod.rs SECTION_ENCODING_*; the rerank source needs either a second
            embeddings section (cleanest, needs a reserved section id 0x09 'embeddings_fp' since
            0x04 is fixed in CANONICAL_SECTIONS) OR a manifest flag pointing rerank at f16; wire
            candidate-gen vs rerank-source split in nest-runtime/src/search.rs score_subset. Seed
            the rotation from the model fingerprint already in the manifest so builds stay
            byte-identical.
    impact: Unlocks ~28x embedding compression (vs current 8x int8) on nest's largest,
            currently-incompressible section while preserving true-cosine returns -- the single
            biggest step toward 'compress more than everyone'. Enables a 'nano' preset well below
            the 0.283 'tiny' floor.
    effort: high
    from:   chroma RaBitQ, VectorChord RaBitQ, pgvector binary-quantize-then-rerank, sqlite-vec binary coarse
            + float rescore, qdrant two-stage rescore

* Upgrade int8.rs in place to Rotational Quantization (RQ): add a seeded fast Walsh-Hadamard
  rotation before the existing per-vector min/max int8 step, store the rotation seed (derived from
  model fingerprint) in the payload prefix, and use the decomposed dot product where only the
  int8.int8 term hits SIMD. Register as a NEW additive encoding id (e.g. 5 = 'rq8') so the FROZEN
  format and existing int8=3 stay byte-compatible.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/int8.rs (add rotation; bump a payload sub-version, NOT
            NEST_FORMAT_VERSION); new fwht.rs helper module (<=300 lines, repr(C), little-endian,
            no panics) shared by rabitq.rs; the decomposed kernel lands beside dot_f32_i8 in
            crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/{avx2,neon,scalar}.rs.
    impact: Pushes the 'tiny' preset recall@10 from 0.992 toward 0.998+ at the SAME 4x size and ~2.3x faster
            scoring -- a near-free recall/latency win on the existing preset, and the FWHT helper
            is the shared primitive every other rotation codec reuses.
    effort: medium
    from:   weaviate RQ, VectorChord random rotation, qdrant Hadamard rotation

* Add a TurboQuant codec with a COMPILE-TIME-baked Lloyd-Max codebook as a Rust const (tq4=id 6,
  tq2=id 7), scored asymmetrically via a SIMD nibble lookup-table kernel (pshufb+maddubs on AVX2,
  NEON tbl, scalar fallback). Codes are scored in place with zero decompression, so this respects
  the never-decompress-embeddings-on-hot-path rule exactly.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/turboquant.rs (const codebook keeps builds byte-identical, no
            corpus stats); LUT kernel in crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/ beside the dot kernels;
            reuse the shared fwht.rs rotator. Per-vector store = [scale:f32][packed 2/4-bit
            codes]; section header carries rotation seed + qbits surfaced by nest-cli
            inspect/stats.
    impact: 4-bit matches int8 at HALF the bytes with equal-or-better recall; 2-bit reaches 16x.
            Data-oblivious + const codebook is the only high-end quantizer that keeps nest
            deterministic without pinning k-means. Enables a 'micro'/'nano' preset.
    effort: high
    from:   sqlite-vector TurboQuant, qdrant TurboQuant, sqlite-vec, milvus FastScan LUT kernel

* Add a Hamming popcount kernel and a multi-bit query bit-plane scorer to the SIMD dispatcher, so
  1-bit codes can be candidate-scored by AND+popcount and query precision tuned WITHOUT
  re-encoding the file (split the f32 query into weighted bit-planes at query time).
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/mod.rs (new dot_hamming/score_bit_section beside dot_f32_i8) +
            avx2.rs (_mm256 popcnt path), neon.rs (vcntq_u8), scalar.rs (count_ones); wired as the
            coarse stage in search.rs feeding the existing rerank.
    impact: popcount is one instruction so the kernel is trivial across all three backends; ~35x cheaper per
            candidate than f32 dot. The bit-plane trick gives a recall knob that costs zero extra
            storage -- decouples file size from query precision.
    effort: low
    from:   VectorChord multi-bit query, TurboQuant query bit-planes, chroma AND+popcount, pgvector Hamming,
            sqlite-vec Hamming

* Add zone-map block-skipping to the exact scan: an OPTIONAL additive sidecar (reserved section id
  0x09+) storing, per fixed block of ~1024 vectors, a centroid + max-radius bound. Before the
  linear SIMD loop, compute a per-block upper-bound cosine and skip blocks that cannot beat the
  current top-k threshold. Returns TRUE cosine, so the search-contract guarantee is untouched.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs score_all/score_subset (add the prune check ahead of the dot
            loop); new sidecar encoder in crates/nest-format/src/sections/ (deterministic if
            centroids computed in fixed order); nest-cli stats reports blocks-scanned vs
            blocks-pruned.
    impact: Turns the unconditional O(n) exact scan into pruned-exact and accelerates the MANDATORY rerank
            after HNSW/BM25, without weakening any guarantee. The per-block centroid doubles as a
            residual-quantization anchor (vector = centroid + small residual) for a future
            structural codec, and the dirty-block model seeds the real-time-edit feature.
    effort: medium
    from:   sqlite-columnar zone maps, VectorChord IVF residual centroids, lance IVF clustering

* Add FSST as a transparent text codec id for the chunks_canonical section, replacing
  section-level zstd ONLY for the text section, so single chunks stay O(1) random-accessible by
  offset for citations while still getting ~2x compression.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/fsst.rs (new transparent codec, new reserved encoding id); decode
            path in encoding/mod.rs::decode_payload must keep the transparent guarantee (currently
            raw/f16/int8 are Cow::Borrowed, zstd is Cow::Owned -- FSST needs per-span decode, not
            whole-section); citation reopen in nest-cli cite + nest-runtime materialize.
    impact: Advances compress-more-than-everyone on the text section WITHOUT breaking
            nest://content_hash/chunk_id span reopening that block-zstd currently forces a
            full-section decode for. Directly improves citation latency on compressed files.
    effort: medium
    from:   lance FSST, lance transparent-vs-opaque taxonomy

* Add a one-bit 'transparent' tag per section/encoding in the manifest+SectionEntry so
  nest-runtime knows whether a section permits zero-copy single-value extraction
  (raw/f16/int8/rabitq/FSST) vs needs a decode buffer (zstd block). Formalizes the constraint nest
  currently enforces only by convention and is the guardrail that lets future aggressive codecs be
  added safely.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/layout/section_entry.rs (use a reserved bit/byte, additive),
            manifest/mod.rs, and reader/decode.rs; checked in reader/validate.rs so an opaque
            codec on the embeddings/hot-path section is rejected at open time.
    impact: Low byte cost, high architectural payoff: makes the never-compress-the-scored-embeddings rule
            machine-enforced rather than tribal knowledge, preventing a future codec from silently
            breaking the mmap hot path or citation spans.
    effort: low
    from:   lance transparent-vs-opaque taxonomy, sqlite-vss negative lesson (monolithic blob forbids random
            access)

* Add byte-stream-split as a COLD/archival-only embeddings encoding (de-interleave f32 into 4 byte
  planes, then zstd) under a new reserved encoding id, used exclusively for a non-scored frozen
  variant -- never the mmap-scored path. Keeps the never-zstd-scored-embeddings rule intact while
  cutting size for archival .nest files.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/bss.rs (new); guarded so the reader refuses to mark a BSS section
            as the scored embeddings section (cross-check with the transparent tag above); offered
            only via a dedicated 'archive' preset in nest-cli, decoded back to f32 on open.
    impact: High ratio on float embedding archives (exponent byte planes RLE/zstd hugely) for cold
            storage/transfer, advancing compress-more-than-everyone for the archival use case
            without touching the hot path.
    effort: medium
    from:   lance byte-stream-split, lance compression cascade

* Add validity-bitmap tombstones + byte-identical-embedding dedup to the writer: collapse
  duplicate vectors to one stored row (heaptids-style), and reserve a per-section validity bitmap
  so a future delete marks a chunk dead without rewriting embeddings -- treating tombstones as
  content keeps the build deterministic.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/writer/build.rs + payload.rs (dedup at encode time, maps duplicate
            chunk_ids to a shared embedding row); reserved validity-bitmap sub-section;
            nest-runtime search skips dead rows.
    impact: Dedup shrinks corpora with repeated text (the PT-BR fake-news demo set has near-duplicates) at
            zero recall cost; the bitmap is the deterministic foundation for the real-time-edit
            north star without the O(N) rewrite that sqlite-vss/vss suffers.
    effort: medium
    from:   pgvector heaptids dedup, sqlite-vec validity-bitmap tombstones, sqlite-columnar dirty-chunk model

* Persist a small declarative index-spec string in the search_contract section (e.g.
  'hnsw(m=16,ef=200)+bm25(rrf)+rerank(exact)') so a .nest file self-describes deterministically
  how to search itself, generalizing the preset names into a portable contract.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/contract.rs (add an index_spec field to SearchContract, additive),
            cross-checked against the manifest in reader/validate.rs; nest-cli presets emit the
            spec.
    impact: Strengthens the sovereign/single-file/self-describing guarantee: the file carries its own search
            semantics, not the CLI version. Low risk, additive, makes presets future-proof as new
            quantized index types are added.
    effort: low
    from:   sqlite-vss Faiss factory-string DSL, lance vector-index decomposition (IVF x sub-index x
            quantization)

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - RaBitQ 1-bit on embeddings: ~28x vs f32 / ~8x vs current int8 (768-dim 3KB -> ~96 bytes +
    scalars), recall recovered by nest's existing rerank -- biggest single win on the dominant
    section.
  - TurboQuant 4-bit matches the int8 'tiny' preset at half the bytes (~8-16x) at equal-or-better
    recall; 2-bit 16x, 1-bit 32x, compile-time codebook so no determinism loss.
  - Rotational Quantization upgrade to int8: same 4x size, recall@10 0.992 -> ~0.998 -- free
    quality dividend buying room for a more aggressive global preset.
  - Dual-section split is itself a win: coarse section 1-2 bit + rerank source at f16 (2x) instead
    of f32 -> nano preset far below the 0.283 'tiny' ratio while keeping true-cosine returns.
  - FSST on chunks_canonical: ~2x over raw text while preserving O(1) citation-span access that
    block-zstd destroys.
  - Byte-stream-split + zstd for cold/archival embeddings: high ratio on f32 byte-planes for an
    'archive' preset, off the scored hot path.
  - Byte-identical embedding dedup: collapses duplicate vectors to one row at zero recall cost --
    helps the near-duplicate-heavy PT-BR fake-news demo corpus.
  - Multi-bit query bit-planes: ship 1-bit storage (smallest file) and recover recall on the query
    side -- smallest file no longer forces worst recall.
  - Zone-map centroids double as residual-quantization anchors (vector = centroid + small
    residual) for a future structural codec beyond float16/int8.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - DETERMINISM TRAP: trained PQ/IVF (milvus/knowhere, lance) needs k-means that is not
    byte-reproducible unless seed/init/iterations/tie-breaking are all pinned, and SIMD float
    non-associativity can still perturb centroids -- conflicts with nest's byte-identical-build
    constraint. Prefer the data-oblivious family; treat PQ as a later pinned-only option.
  - BROKEN-RERANK TRAP (in nest's own code): score_subset reranks against the SAME embeddings
    section it scored, so 1-2 bit storage silently makes the 'exact' rerank lossy and the returned
    score no longer real cosine. Any sub-int8 codec MUST ship a separate full-precision/f16 rerank
    source section.
  - FORMAT-FREEZE: every codec must be a new additive encoding id (reserved 4-255) and any new
    section a reserved id 0x09+, never bump NEST_FORMAT_VERSION; the rerank-source section must be
    a new OPTIONAL id kept OUT of content_hash so it cannot invalidate citations (embeddings is
    pinned at 0x04 in CANONICAL_SECTIONS).
  - HOT-PATH PURITY: RaBitQ/TurboQuant codes are scored in place (fine), but byte-stream-split and
    FSST are decode-required and must be restricted to cold/text sections, never the scored
    embeddings path -- enforce with the proposed transparent/opaque tag, not convention.
  - LICENSE: do NOT vendor VectorChord (AGPL/ELv2-style) or sqlite-vector/sqlite-columnar (Elastic
    License 2.0, source-available). Reimplement from the public papers; even permissive repos
    (qdrant/milvus/chroma/lance/pgvector) should be reimplemented, not copied.
  - SMALL-CORE: each codec/kernel <=300 lines (333 cap), repr(C), little-endian, typed errors,
    never panic; share ONE FWHT rotator across RQ/RaBitQ/TurboQuant; land incrementally (RQ first,
    then RaBitQ, then TurboQuant) to respect the ~3k/~4.9k loc budgets.
  - RECALL CLIFFS: 1-bit excels at high dim (1536+) but fails at low dim (sift-128, gist-960) --
    validate recall per demo model before any 1-bit default; keep int8/RQ as safe default, 1-bit
    opt-in nano.
  - DON'T copy sqlite-vss/sqlite-columnar on-disk shapes (monolithic blob caps at 1GB + O(N)
    write-amplification + no mmap; columnar store is 1.2x LARGER) -- steal only the algorithmic
    ideas.
  - Per-vector corrective scalars (2-3 f32) erode the ratio at low dim -- account for them so the
    nano size-ratio claim is honest.


##################################################################################################
# graph-db  -  graph databases, engines, and traversal
##################################################################################################

summary:     Across 13 graph engines the single convergent idea is: compress structured integer/ID streams
             (adjacency lists, ID columns, postings) with sort -> delta -> minimum-bit-width
             packing, and keep them searchable/traversable WITHOUT decoding by mmapping a flat CSR
             layout. Every serious engine (Neo4j, GDS, Kuzu, GraphScope/GraphAr, JanusGraph,
             Nebula, TuGraph) lands on the same Pareto winner over varint+zstd for monotone
             columns; Dgraph's sroar pushes it further (the compressed bytes ARE the in-memory
             layout, zero decode tax - exactly nest's hot-path rule); Kuzu's ALP is the lone idea
             that legitimately challenges nest's "never compress embeddings" axiom with a LOSSLESS
             float codec that still decodes to exact bits for SIMD scoring. FalkorDB contributes
             per-section density-tiered codec auto-selection (iso/bitmap/sparse), Blazegraph
             contributes front-coding that is binary-searchable on compressed bytes (ideal for
             nest's alphabetical bm25 vocabulary and sha256 chunk_ids), and VPack/Memgraph/TypeDB
             contribute packed-metadata-byte + key-interning for the structured/json payloads nest
             wants to ingest next. nest currently stores all of these as raw or zstd: HNSW
             neighbor lists as fixed u32 (ann/codec.rs:30), bm25 postings as raw u32 doc/tf pairs
             (bm25/codec.rs:33-36), spans as raw u64 pairs. Grounding correction to the extracts:
             nest's chunk_ids are sha256:<hex> STRINGS not dense ints, so the dense-ordinal delta
             trick applies to the implicit 0..N row index (HNSW/graph/bm25 doc ids) and to span
             offsets, not to chunk_ids values directly; front-coding is the right codec for the
             sha256 strings. The mission-critical sequence: (1) ship one delta+bitpack
             integer-stream codec in encoding/ (reserved id 0x04..0xFF), (2) re-encode hnsw_index
             and bm25_index postings with it, (3) add a CSR graph_adjacency section (0x09) reusing
             it for the north-star graph-search gap, all additive within format v1, all
             deterministic, none touching the embedding hot path. Then evaluate ALP as the
             high-risk/high-reward lossless embedding path.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The field has converged on a single canonical recipe for graph/ID storage: sort target IDs
ascending, delta-encode into small monotone gaps, then bit-pack per fixed-size block to the
minimum bit width the local maximum needs ("integer packing" / PForDelta family), laid out as flat
CSR (offsets array + degrees + packed-targets blob). This beats both global encodings and
per-value continuation-bit varints on the speed/ratio Pareto front because block unpack is
branchless, cache-local and SIMD-friendly, and crucially it is mmap-pure: no dictionary/window
state, decodes a single block in place off disk (Neo4j, GDS report ~6x over uncompressed at memcpy
decode speed; GraphAr reports 27% of plain+offset; GDS ~1.4 bytes/edge). The frontier idea is
making the compressed representation directly operable: Dgraph's sroar serialized roaring bitmaps
run set-intersection (graph join = bitmap AND) with zero unmarshal (~7x faster, ~15x less memory
than reference roaring); FalkorDB auto-selects the most compact of four sparse tiers per matrix
plus an iso single-scalar mode for boolean adjacency; Blazegraph front-codes sorted keys so they
stay binary-searchable on the compressed bytes (search-on-compressed, no inflate). For the float
problem specifically, Kuzu's ALP/ALPRD is state-of-the-art lossless float compression (beats
Gorilla/Chimp/Patas/Elf, matches/beats zstd, far faster, auto-vectorizing 1024-value blocks) and
is the only credible path to compress embedding-grade arrays while keeping exact in-place SIMD
scoring. For structured/document payloads the consensus is self-describing trailing-offset tables
with smallest-width offset selection plus a per-file key dictionary (VPack ~2-3x on
repeated-schema json; Memgraph/TypeDB packed-metadata-byte + name interning). Licensing reality:
the algorithms (delta, varint, bit-packing, CSR, front-coding, roaring) are decades-old
public-domain; the GPL/BSL/SSPL engines (Neo4j, GDS, memgraph, FalkorDB, blazegraph) are
learn-from-only, while Kuzu (MIT), GraphScope/GraphAr, JanusGraph, Nebula, TuGraph, sroar/badger
(Apache-2.0) and SurrealDB's revision/storekey sub-crates (MIT/Apache) are clean-room or
vendorable references.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Delta + per-block minimum-bit-width integer packing (PForDelta family) over sorted ID streams:
   the universal codec that beats zstd on monotone integers at memcpy decode speed and is
   mmap-seekable per-block. Drawn from Neo4j integer packing, GDS PackedAdjacencyList, GraphAr
   delta CSR, TuGraph length-varint. This is the single highest-value steal: nest stores HNSW
   neighbor lists, bm25 postings and span offsets uncompressed/zstd today, and all three are
   textbook inputs.
2. Flat CSR adjacency (u64 offsets array + packed neighbor-id blob) as a frozen, mmap-traversable
   graph section: one node's neighbors are a single contiguous slice walked with zero allocation,
   identical discipline to nest's embedding rows. Drawn from Neo4j/GDS CSR, Nebula
   prefix-locality, Kuzu disk-CSR, GraphAr, TuGraph. This is the concrete delivery vehicle for
   nest's named graph-search north-star gap.
3. ALP / ALPRD lossless float compression decoded in-register before SIMD dot-product: the only
   idea that challenges nest's 'embeddings can never be compressed' axiom while keeping
   exact-cosine rerank byte-true. Drawn from Kuzu (MIT, vendorable). Highest payoff (embeddings
   are the dominant byte cost and the largest section) but highest risk to the hot path.
4. Serialized roaring bitmaps where the compressed bytes ARE the in-memory layout, enabling
   set-intersection with zero decode: matches nest's no-decode-on-hot-path rule exactly. Drawn
   from Dgraph sroar (Apache-2.0). Best fit for bm25 postings (term -> sorted doc-id set) and a
   boolean chunk-to-chunk graph.
5. Per-section density-tiered codec auto-selection driven by measured min/max/cardinality stats
   (raw vs delta-pack vs roaring vs iso-single-scalar), chosen deterministically at build time.
   Drawn from FalkorDB 4-tier sparse + iso, Kuzu per-chunk adaptive codec, GraphAr. Raises nest's
   compression ceiling for EVERY section, not just graphs, replacing today's per-preset
   hardcoding.
6. Front-coding (every Nth sorted key full, rest as shared-prefix-len + suffix) that stays
   binary-searchable on the compressed bytes: search-on-compressed with no inflate. Drawn from
   Blazegraph raba coders (learn-only, GPL). Ideal for nest's sha256:<hex> chunk_ids and the
   alphabetically-ordered bm25 term dictionary.
7. VPack trailing-offset-table + smallest-width offset selection + object-key dictionary for
   structured/json payloads: zero-parse random sub-document access off mmap plus 2-3x shrink on
   repeated-schema corpora. Drawn from ArangoDB VelocyPack (Apache-2.0), reinforced by Memgraph
   packed-metadata-byte and TypeDB inline-vs-hash. The codec for nest's json/yaml/parquet ingest
   roadmap.
8. SurrealDB revision pattern (leading varint version tag + positional non-self-describing fields
   with per-field start=N/default_fn vs convert_fn): turns nest's prose 'additive-within-v1 vs
   true-v2' rule into machine-checked per-field metadata, far more byte-frugal than the current
   JSON manifest. revision/storekey sub-crates are MIT/Apache, vendorable.
9. Inline distance co-location: store the f16/int8 edge distance inline next to each neighbor id
   in the CSR adjacency so graph expansion skips a separate distance array lookup. Drawn from
   Nebula rank-as-sort-key, Kuzu. Cache-locality win for the future graph traversal kernel.
10. Order-preserving / sort-as-index byte layouts (lexicographic byte order == logical order) so
   range scans need no separate index. Drawn from JanusGraph edge-as-cell, TypeDB memcmp keys,
   storekey. Reinforces nest's determinism: canonical sort + delta layout is inherently
   content_hash-stable.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add one delta + zigzag + minimum-bit-width block-packing integer-stream codec as a new module
  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/intpack.rs (delta -> zigzag for signed gaps -> pack each fixed
  block of N=128 to ceil(log2(max_in_block)) bits, with a tail strategy for the final partial
  block). Register it as a new SECTION encoding id in the reserved 0x04..0xFF space (next free
  after int8=3, e.g. 0x05) and wire it into decode_payload() in encoding/mod.rs. Pure function of
  sorted input, no dictionary state, thiserror-typed, never panics, under 300 lines.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/intpack.rs (new) + register in encoding/mod.rs decode_payload
            (currently matches RAW/ZSTD/FLOAT16/INT8 at lines 44-55) + new const
            SECTION_ENCODING_INTPACK in layout/mod.rs (next to lines 211-214). Apply to
            chunks_original_spans byte_start/byte_end u64 columns first.
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   neo4j/neo4j, neo4j/graph-data-science, alibaba/GraphScope, TuGraph-family/tugraph-db,
            vesoft-inc/nebula

* Re-encode the HNSW neighbor lists with the intpack codec instead of fixed u32. In ann/codec.rs
  to_bytes() (lines 24-34) neighbor ids per node/layer are written as raw u32; sort each neighbor
  list, delta+bitpack it, and store per-list bit-width. This directly shrinks the hnsw_index(0x07)
  section that the 'tiny' preset (0.283 ratio) already ships, with zero recall impact (neighbor
  SET is unchanged, only its byte encoding) and a branchless unpack on read in from_bytes (lines
  40-89).
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/ann/codec.rs to_bytes/from_bytes (HNSW_PAYLOAD_VERSION stays 1; this is an
            internal section re-encoding, additive). Reuse the intpack kernel from nest-format.
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   neo4j/neo4j, kuzudb/kuzu, neo4j/graph-data-science

* Re-encode bm25_index postings as delta-coded doc-id runs (and optionally serialized roaring
  bitmaps for high-df terms). In bm25/codec.rs to_bytes() (lines 33-36) each term's postings are
  raw u32 doc + u32 tf; the doc ids within a term are already implicitly ascending, so
  delta+varint the doc column and bitpack tf. The alphabetical term order (already mandated for
  determinism, lines 26-28) makes this a pure deterministic re-encode. Shrinks the hybrid preset's
  bm25 section materially.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/codec.rs to_bytes/from_bytes. For dense terms, a sroar-style bitmap
            whose bytes equal the in-memory layout lets the fusion path (bm25/fusion.rs) run
            posting intersections with no decode.
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   dgraph-io/dgraph (with companion repos dgraph-io/sroar and dgraph-io/badger), blazegraph/database,
            alibaba/GraphScope

* Add an optional CSR graph_adjacency section (new section id 0x09, additive within v1) holding
  chunk-to-chunk edges (semantic-neighbor graph, citation links, or serialized HNSW level-0
  adjacency): a u64 offsets array indexed by chunk ordinal + a delta+bitpacked neighbor-id blob
  (reusing intpack), optionally with inline f16/int8 edge distances co-located per neighbor. Add a
  graph::CsrIndex reader in nest-runtime beside ann::HnswIndex that mmaps offsets and unpacks one
  node's run branchlessly, feeding candidates into the EXISTING mandatory exact-cosine rerank
  (search.rs score_subset) so graph traversal is a candidate generator, never a score source -
  identical contract to HNSW.
    where:  new SECTION_GRAPH_ADJACENCY=0x09 in layout/mod.rs (after BM25=0x08, line 234) + OPTIONAL_SECTIONS
            list (lines 256-258); new crates/nest-runtime/src/graph/ module (mod.rs + csr.rs, each
            <300 loc); new nest-cli subcommand search-graph mirroring search-ann. Delivers the
            named graph-search north-star gap.
    impact: high
    effort: high
    from:   vesoft-inc/nebula, TuGraph-family/tugraph-db, alibaba/GraphScope, neo4j/neo4j, apache/tinkerpop

* Add deterministic per-section codec auto-selection driven by measured statistics: at build time
  compute min/max/cardinality/monotonicity for each non-embedding section and pick the smallest of
  {raw, zstd, intpack, roaring, iso-single-scalar} rather than a fixed per-preset choice. Record
  the chosen encoding id per section (already supported by the manifest's per-section encoding
  field). Iso mode stores ONE scalar when all values are identical (e.g. unweighted/boolean
  adjacency).
    where:  build-time codec chooser in crates/nest-format/src/writer/ (Python build stage feeds stats);
            selection is a pure function of section bytes so content_hash stays stable. Each
            section's encoding already lives in SectionEntry, so this is additive.
    impact: medium
    effort: medium
    from:   FalkorDB/FalkorDB, kuzudb/kuzu, alibaba/GraphScope

* Front-code the sha256:<hex> chunk_ids section and the alphabetical bm25 term dictionary: every
  Nth sorted key stored full, the rest as (shared_prefix_len varint, suffix), plus a tiny offset
  table to binary-search the full keys. sha256 hex strings share long common prefixes after
  sorting and the bm25 vocabulary is already alphabetical, so both shrink losslessly AND stay
  binary-searchable on the compressed bytes (no decompress on the search/cite path). New encoding
  id in the reserved range, applied only to these sorted-string sections.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/frontcode.rs (new, <300 loc) applied in sections/chunk_ids.rs
            (currently plain length-prefixed strings, lines 8-15) and bm25/codec.rs term
            dictionary. Enables nest://content_hash/chunk_id resolution via memcmp prefix scan
            instead of a hashmap.
    impact: medium
    effort: medium
    from:   blazegraph/database, typedb/typedb

* Build a VPack-style structured-payload codec for the json/yaml/parquet ingest roadmap: a
  [tag][smallest-width 1/2/4/8 LE offsets][values] trailing-offset-table layout plus a per-file
  object-key dictionary (collect+count keys, intern repeated keys to u16 ids, sorted bytewise for
  determinism). Enables mmap sub-path resolution (doc.field[3].name) with pure base+offset
  arithmetic and zero decode, complementing zstd (store VPack-encoded, optionally zstd the cold
  parts). 2-3x on repeated-schema corpora like the PT-BR datasets.
    where:  new crates/nest-format/src/encoding/vpack.rs + a structured-document section id 0x0a; the
            key-dictionary two-pass runs in the Python build stage, the Rust serve path only does
            base+offset reads. Reinforced by Memgraph packed-metadata-byte (1-byte
            {type,len-width,int-width} tag) for scalar columns.
    impact: medium
    effort: high
    from:   arangodb/arangodb (core innovation lives in https://github.com/arangodb/velocypack),
            memgraph/memgraph, typedb/typedb

* Evaluate ALP/ALPRD as a LOSSLESS embeddings codec (new dtype/encoding for section 0x04) that
  decodes per 1024-value block in-register immediately before the SIMD dot product, so the
  exact-cosine rerank in search.rs stays byte-true. This is the only path to compress nest's
  largest section without the lossiness of today's float16/int8. Gate it behind a benchmark
  proving the fused ALP-decode+dot kernel does not regress search latency past the boot-overhead
  budget; keep the scalar auto-vectorizing kernel under 300 loc per simd file.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/ (new alp_dot path beside avx2.rs/neon.rs/scalar.rs, dispatched in
            simd/mod.rs) + ALP encode in nest-format/encoding for SECTION_EMBEDDINGS (0x04).
            Highest reward, highest risk; do AFTER the integer-codec wins land and only if
            benchmarks pass. Kuzu is MIT so the kernel can be ported clean.
    impact: high
    effort: high
    from:   kuzudb/kuzu, surrealdb/surrealdb

* Adopt the SurrealDB revision pattern for the manifest and future binary sub-records: a leading
  varint version tag + positional fields with per-field start=N (additive, default_fn => stays
  format v1) vs convert_fn (meaning change => forces true v2). This makes nest's prose
  'additive-only within v1, bump NEST_FORMAT_VERSION only on meaning change' machine-checked
  per-field, and is far more byte-frugal than the current canonical-JSON manifest.
  revision/storekey are MIT/Apache (vendorable after SPDX check).
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/manifest/ (canonical.rs currently JCS-canonical JSON); add a binary
            revisioned envelope option. Pure positional layout keeps determinism; no socket,
            offline-safe.
    impact: low
    effort: medium
    from:   surrealdb/surrealdb, apache/tinkerpop

* When the CSR graph section ships, support a delta-plus/delta-minus overlay for the planned
  real-time edit: keep the frozen mmap'd adjacency immutable and merge a tiny
  pending-add/pending-delete overlay at read time, flushing to a new deterministic container on
  commit. Preserves single-file + determinism (the committed file is always a clean re-serialize,
  the overlay never persists into the canonical bytes).
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/graph/ overlay reader (read-time three-way merge), with commit going
            through the normal nest-format writer so content_hash is recomputed canonically.
            Reference design only until real-time edit is on the roadmap.
    impact: low
    effort: high
    from:   FalkorDB/FalkorDB, memgraph/memgraph

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - chunks_original_spans byte_start/byte_end: u64 pairs that are locally monotone (byte offsets
    into source docs). Delta+zigzag+bitpack collapses these from 16 bytes/span toward 1-3
    bytes/span losslessly, decode at memcpy speed off mmap (vs raw today). First and cheapest
    intpack target - pays off before any graph work.
  - hnsw_index(0x07) neighbor lists: currently fixed u32 per neighbor (ann/codec.rs:30-31).
    Sort+delta+bitpack each list to ceil(log2(max_gap)) bits - GDS reports ~6x on adjacency;
    directly improves the tiny preset's 0.283 ratio with zero recall change (the neighbor SET is
    identical, only its encoding shrinks).
  - bm25_index(0x08) postings: raw u32 doc + u32 tf pairs (bm25/codec.rs:33-36) with doc ids
    already implicitly ascending. Delta-code the doc column and bitpack tf; for high-df terms
    switch to a serialized roaring bitmap (sroar-style, bytes==memory layout) so fusion runs
    posting AND with no decode. Shrinks the hybrid preset and speeds fusion.
  - chunk_ids sha256:<hex> strings: after sorting, the hex strings share long common prefixes -
    front-coding (every Nth full, rest as shared-prefix-len+suffix) is lossless, stays
    binary-searchable on compressed bytes, and shrinks a section that currently stores 70+ bytes
    per id raw. Correction to the extracts: chunk_ids are strings not dense ints, so front-coding
    (not delta) is the right codec here.
  - Future graph_adjacency(0x09) section: delta+varint CSR is proven at 27% of plain+offset
    (GraphAr); with FalkorDB iso mode an unweighted/boolean chunk graph costs ~N row-indices +
    offsets + ONE value scalar. Adds graph search to the mission at near-zero extra bytes, keeping
    the .nest container compact.
  - Structured json/yaml/parquet ingest (roadmap): VPack key-dictionary + smallest-width offsets
    gives 2-3x on repeated-schema data like the PT-BR datasets while preserving zero-decode random
    field access - the compress-AND-serve combination, beating naive zstd-of-json on access cost.
    Composes with zstd on the cold parts.
  - provenance(0x05) JSON blob + repeated metadata keys: name-to-id interning (Memgraph/VPack)
    replaces repeated long keys/model-fingerprints with u16 ids, deterministic via alphabetical
    key order; shrinks the structured metadata before zstd.
  - Embeddings(0x04), the dominant byte cost and the section nest currently refuses to compress:
    ALP/ALPRD is the only LOSSLESS path that keeps exact in-place SIMD scoring (decodes to exact
    bits per 1024-block in-register before the dot product), advancing compress-more-than-everyone
    past today's LOSSY float16/int8 - gated on a no-latency-regression benchmark.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Do NOT vendor source from GPL/BSL/SSPL engines: Neo4j + GDS (GPLv3), memgraph (BSL 1.1),
    FalkorDB core (SSPL), blazegraph (GPLv2). These are learn-from-only. The underlying algorithms
    (delta, varint, bit-packing, CSR, front-coding, roaring) are decades-old public-domain and
    must be clean-room reimplemented from spec. Safe to port/vendor: Kuzu (MIT), sroar/badger +
    GraphScope/GraphAr + JanusGraph + Nebula + TuGraph (Apache-2.0), SurrealDB revision/storekey
    sub-crates (MIT/Apache - verify SPDX first). VPack is Apache-2.0 (the ArangoDB server moved to
    BSL but the format lib/spec did not).
  - NEVER apply the integer codec, roaring, or zstd to the embeddings section on the current hot
    path - they require decode/window state and would break the score-directly-from-mmap rule. The
    ONLY embedding-compression candidate is ALP because it decodes to EXACT bits in-register per
    block; even then it must be benchmarked against the search-latency budget before landing, and
    the search contract's mandatory exact-cosine rerank must remain byte-true.
  - Block/page co-location and alignment (Neo4j Block format, Kuzu 4KB pages) can use MORE disk
    than tightly-packed layouts due to padding/headers - directly against nest's size-first goal.
    Take the integer codec and CSR layout, NOT the page-padding. Likewise reject the in-RAM
    structures (memgraph skip-lists, Edge>=88B / Vertex>=136B; TypeDB auto-built bidirectional
    indices ~24GB vs 17GB) - those optimize RAM/write, the opposite of nest's compact read-only
    file.
  - Skip every server/LSM/multi-file storage engine wholesale: Badger (Dgraph), RocksDB
    (TypeDB/Nebula/SurrealDB backends/ArangoDB), LMDB (TuGraph), Redis modules (FalkorDB),
    Cassandra/HBase (JanusGraph), Gremlin Server/OLAP/Spark (TinkerPop). They violate
    single-file/offline/no-socket. Only the on-disk byte-layout discipline transfers.
  - Determinism must hold for every new codec: bit-packing block size must be FIXED, sort order
    canonical (ascending ids / alphabetical terms - already nest's rule), roaring container choice
    is a pure function of cardinality, no entropy-coder dictionary state. content_hash must stay
    STABLE across encodings - so decode-then-hash must yield identical canonical bytes regardless
    of whether intpack/roaring/front-code was used (mirror the existing 'content_hash hashes
    decoded bytes' rule in encoding/mod.rs lines 15-18).
  - Endianness: GraphBinary (TinkerPop) is big-endian and many graph engines use big-endian keys
    for lexicographic ordering - nest mandates little-endian unsigned everywhere. Adapt the
    layout, do not copy byte order. For order-preserving keys, replicate storekey's
    sign-flip/IEEE754-twiddle in LE rather than relying on raw BE byte order.
  - File-hygiene: each new codec (intpack, frontcode, vpack, alp) must be a single-responsibility
    file <=300 lines (333 hard cap), repr(C), all integers LE-unsigned, typed thiserror errors,
    never panic in library code. The graph module must split mod.rs/csr.rs/overlay to respect the
    cap.
  - Two-pass build-time costs (VPack key dictionary, sort+id-assignment, FalkorDB stat-driven
    codec selection, front-code offset tables) are acceptable in the Python BUILD stage but must
    NEVER appear on the Rust serve path. Keep the runtime to mmap + branchless unpack + dot
    product. Beware the search-text ~300-500ms python boot overhead is already a known gap - do
    not add per-query planning cost (TinkerPop notes strategy application costs ~5-6x bytecode
    construction; run any graph query rewrite ONCE at plan time, not per-traverser).
  - Roaring/sroar zero-decode benefit only materializes if the on-disk container layout EQUALS the
    in-memory layout with correct LE alignment; a naive roaring port that requires an unmarshal
    step loses the entire hot-path advantage and is then just worse zstd. Mirror sroar's
    single-buffer contract exactly or use plain delta+bitpack instead.


##################################################################################################
# semantic-rdf  -  RDF triple stores, semantic web, graph query languages
##################################################################################################

summary:     The semantic-rdf category (Jena, RDF4J, Oxigraph, RDFLib, terminusdb-store/tdb-succinct,
             openCypher) converges on one architecture nest can mine hard: store data as
             integer-ID triples, dictionary-encode every repeated term once, and index those IDs
             in multiple sorted permutations so any query shape is a prefix range scan. The
             frontier splits into two storage philosophies. (A) Dictionary + B-tree/LSM (Jena
             TDB2, RDF4J, Oxigraph): map terms to compact IDs, inline small scalars/strings into
             the ID itself to skip the dictionary, and keep multiple permutation indexes. The
             standout trick is value inlining: Jena packs
             i56/decimal/date/bool/range-reduced-double into the 8-byte NodeId, Oxigraph inlines
             <16-byte strings + native i64/i128/dateTime into a fixed 17-byte record, RDF4J uses
             an order-preserving varint whose length is recoverable from byte 1 so encoded keys
             binary-search WITHOUT decoding. (B) Succinct query-in-place (terminusdb-store,
             tdb-succinct): the file IS the index, no decompress step, reaching ~13 bytes/triple
             on billions of triples via Plain Front Coding string dictionaries, bit-packed
             LogArrays (ceil(log2(max+1)) bits/int instead of fixed 32/64), and two-level
             rank/select BitIndex over adjacency lists. This succinct family is the single most
             important steal for nest: it is the missing middle between "raw f32 (fast, big)" and
             "zstd block (small, must decompress to read one element)", and it is architecturally
             identical to nest's mmap-and-scan-off-disk model. Grounding the analysis in the
             codebase: nest's HNSW codec (crates/nest-runtime/src/ann/codec.rs) stores neighbor
             IDs as fixed u32 regardless of N, and the BM25 codec
             (crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/codec.rs) stores raw term strings and u32 doc/tf
             postings with no front-coding or delta/varint, so both optional sections carry
             exactly the high-bit slack and structural redundancy LogArray/PFC/varint remove. The
             encoding dispatch (crates/nest-format/src/encoding/mod.rs::decode_payload) and
             reserved encoding IDs 4-255 plus reserved section IDs 0x09+
             (crates/nest-format/src/layout/mod.rs) give clean additive extension points, so every
             recommendation lands inside v1 with no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump. openCypher is a
             non-storage spec (low value): it contributes only a conformance-harness methodology
             for proving search determinism, not compression.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Two coexisting storage philosophies. (1) Dictionary + permutation indexes: every distinct RDF term
is mapped to a small integer ID; indexes store only IDs (4-17 bytes/term) in multiple sorted
permutations (SPO/POS/OSP for triples, +context for quads) so any bound-prefix pattern is a sorted
range scan with no query planner needed. Three increasingly aggressive inlining variants eliminate
the dictionary for common values: Jena TDB2 inlines scalars into a tagged 8-byte NodeId (bit63
ptr-vs-value, then i56/decimal/date/bool/range-reduced-double); RDF4J packs a 2-bit type tag into
the ID and uses an order-preserving varint (length recoverable from byte 1, byte-order ==
numeric-order so you binary-search encoded keys without decoding); Oxigraph uses a fixed 17-byte
type-byte+16-byte record that inlines <16-byte UTF-8 strings and native
i64/i128-fixed-point-decimal/dateTime, hashing only large strings into an id2str side table
(content-addressed: identical value -> identical id, no forward index). (2) Succinct
query-in-place (terminusdb-store, tdb-succinct): the compressed bytes ARE the queryable index, no
decompression step. Plain Front Coding (sort strings, bucket by 8, store shared-prefix-len +
suffix, implicit ID = sorted position, bidirectional via bucket binary search); LogArray (pack N
ints at ceil(log2(max+1)) bits with an on-disk width header, entry(i) touches at most 2 words);
two-level rank/select BitIndex (64-bit blocks, 52-block superblocks, precomputed popcount tables,
O(1) rank / O(log superblocks) select, plus a cache-line linear-bitscan shortcut); adjacency lists
as LogArray-of-values + BitIndex-marks so navigation needs zero stored pointers. This reaches
~13.6 bytes/triple at 17B triples with a full 6-way index. Cross-cutting: big-endian keys so
lexicographic order == numeric order for range scans (nest must use little-endian per constraint,
so it pays an explicit comparator or stores keys sorted and binary-searches by decoded value);
content-addressed immutable delta layers (terminusdb) for git-for-data append-only edits with
newest-wins resolution + periodic rollup. openCypher contributes only conformance methodology
(canonical result serialization, ordered-vs-set comparison, side-effect-as-delta accounting), no
storage or compression machinery.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Succinct LogArray (bit-pack ints at ceil(log2(max+1)) bits/slot, not fixed 32/64, with on-disk
   width header) - query-in-place, O(1) random access off mmap, the highest-leverage steal because
   it directly shrinks nest's existing fixed-u32 HNSW neighbor IDs and span/offset arrays with
   zero decode (tdb-succinct, terminusdb-store)
2. Plain Front Coding string dictionary (sort, bucket by 8, store shared-prefix-len + suffix,
   implicit ID = sorted position, bidirectional binary search) - crushes URI/path/repeated-prefix
   data below generic zstd while staying binary-searchable from mmap; directly attacks BM25
   vocabulary, chunk_ids, provenance, and future json/yaml/dataset keys (tdb-succinct,
   terminusdb-store)
3. Dictionary-encode-then-store-only-IDs with order-preserving varint (length recoverable from
   byte 1, byte-order == numeric-order so encoded keys binary-search without decoding) - removes
   structural redundancy before the entropy stage so it stacks under zstd (RDF4J, terminusdb TFC)
4. Hash-or-inline typed-value encoding (inline <16-byte strings + native
   i64/i128-fixed-point/dateTime/bool; replace only large strings with a content hash into id2str)
   - turns repetitive structured metadata into fixed content-addressed records, deterministic and
   dedup-at-value-level before bytes (Oxigraph, Jena NodeId)
5. Two-level rank/select BitIndex (64-bit blocks, 52-block superblocks, precomputed popcount
   LogArrays, O(1) rank, cache-line linear-bitscan shortcut) - pointer-free navigation primitive
   for sparse presence/tombstone bitmaps, BM25 posting lists, and graph adjacency (tdb-succinct)
6. Multi-permutation sorted-ID indexes (SPO/POS/OSP) so any bound-prefix graph pattern is an
   index-only range scan with no query planner - the on-disk substrate for nest's north-star graph
   search (Jena, RDF4J, Oxigraph, RDFLib)
7. Adjacency-list-as-LogArray+BitIndex (HDT bitmap-triples): store edges with zero pointers,
   navigate src->rel->dst via rank/select - ~13 bytes/triple, makes graph edges highly
   compressible (terminusdb-store)
8. Content-addressed immutable delta layers (each commit = a hash-named layer of adds/removes,
   newest-wins, periodic rollup) - blueprint for nest's real-time-edit goal without breaking
   single-file determinism (terminusdb-store)
9. Most-bound-first BGP triple-pattern reordering by count of bound variables (no cost statistics
   needed) - a deterministic, seeded multi-hop graph join order that stays reproducible (RDFLib,
   Jena stats.opt)
10. xloader external-merge-sort bulk build (stream tuples, sort via external sort, compress
   intermediate spill runs, build each index as a separate streamed pass) - removes the in-RAM
   ceiling from nest's deterministic builder for corpora exceeding memory (Jena)

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add a LogArray codec: pack any monotone/bounded unsigned-int section at ceil(log2(max+1))
  bits/slot into LE 64-bit words plus a trailing control word (u64 N, u8 width). entry(i) reads at
  most 2 contiguous words and masks W bits, zero decode, O(1) random access off mmap. Register a
  new reserved encoding id (4-255) in decode_payload's match arm.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/logarray.rs (new, <300 lines) wired into
            crates/nest-format/src/encoding/mod.rs::decode_payload and a new
            SECTION_ENCODING_LOGARRAY const in crates/nest-format/src/layout/mod.rs (id 4);
            reader-side view in nest-runtime
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   terminusdb-store (tdb-succinct LogArray), rdf4j (order-preserving varint)

* Shrink the HNSW section (0x07) by replacing fixed u32 neighbor IDs with LogArray-packed
  ceil(log2(N))-bit IDs. The codec currently writes every neighbor as 4 raw bytes regardless of
  corpus size (to_bytes loops `out.extend_from_slice(&id.to_le_bytes())`); for the tiny preset's
  small/medium N this wastes 10-20 high bits per edge across the densest section. Directly attacks
  the 0.283 size ratio while keeping deterministic seekable layout.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/ann/codec.rs::HnswIndex::to_bytes / from_bytes (bump HNSW_PAYLOAD_VERSION
            inside the section, not NEST_FORMAT_VERSION); reuse the new LogArray reader
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   terminusdb-store (LogArray over adjacency lists), rdflib (densified u32 arrays + binary-search
            prefix walks)

* Add a Plain Front Coding (PFC) dictionary codec for sorted repetitive string sections: sort
  strings, bucket by 8, store bucket-head verbatim then each successor as
  vbyte(shared-prefix-len)+suffix; implicit ID = sorted position; bidirectional id<->string via
  bucket binary search off mmap. Apply first to the BM25 vocabulary, which today stores every term
  as raw [len u32][bytes] with no prefix sharing, then to chunk_ids and provenance. Stays
  binary-searchable and stacks under zstd on cold sections.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/pfc.rs (new) + FrontCodedDict reader; apply to term storage in
            crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/codec.rs::to_bytes/from_bytes and to chunk_ids/provenance
            section codecs in crates/nest-format/src/sections
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   terminusdb-store (PFC dictionary, Martinez-Prieto), rdf4j (IRI namespace+localname factoring)

* Delta+varint the BM25 posting lists. Postings are currently written as raw [doc u32][tf u32]
  pairs per term; doc IDs within a term are monotonically increasing, so store first-doc then
  gap-deltas with an order-preserving varint (length from byte 1) and small-value varint tf. Term
  order is already alphabetical (deterministic constraint satisfied), so this is a pure size win
  on the largest part of the bm25 section with no semantic change.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/codec.rs::to_bytes/from_bytes (bump BM25_PAYLOAD_VERSION); add a
            shared LE varint helper in nest-format encoding
    impact: medium
    effort: low
    from:   rdf4j (order-preserving varint), terminusdb-store (vbyte gap encoding in dictionary blocks)

* Add an optional typed-column section (reserved id 0x09+) for structured payloads
  (json/yaml/parquet cells, dataset columns, repeated provenance): each cell is a tagged word, bit
  indicating inline-vs-span, then a small type tag + native payload (i56/i64 int, 8-bit-scale
  i48/i128-fixed-point decimal, ms-epoch date, bool, f16). Numbers/dates/bools cost zero
  dictionary bytes and zero chunk-span indirection; only strings keep a chunks_original_spans
  pointer or an id2str dictionary ref. Reuse nest's existing SHA-256 content_hash machinery (not
  SipHash) to dedup long strings, keeping the four-integrity-surface story. Fully additive: new
  section id + the typed-dict can be zstd-wrapped since it is non-embedding.
    where:  new section id in crates/nest-format/src/layout/mod.rs (0x09) + CANONICAL/OPTIONAL_SECTIONS; codec
            in crates/nest-format/src/encoding/typed_dict.rs; builder support in
            crates/nest-python/src/build_fn.rs; gate behind a new capabilities flag
    impact: high
    effort: high
    from:   apache/jena (tagged-NodeId inline scalars), oxigraph (hash-or-inline 17-byte typed value, i128
            fixed-point, dateTime-as-decimal)

* Add a graph-search section (reserved id 0x09+) storing edges as integer-densified (src,rel,dst)
  triples in two-or-three sorted LE permutations (SPO + at least one inverse such as OSP), each a
  LogArray, plus a PFC term dictionary. A graph::TripleIndex in nest-runtime mmaps the arrays and
  answers any bound-prefix pattern via binary-search range scan with zero allocation. Keep
  permutation count minimal (RDF4J reports ~120-130 bytes/quad with 3 permutations) and make the
  whole section optional so default builds stay tiny. Port most-bound-first BGP reordering as a
  deterministic, id-order-tiebreak join order so multi-hop stays reproducible and never panics
  (typed thiserror).
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/graph/{term_dict.rs,index.rs,join.rs} (each <300 lines); new section id +
            capability in nest-format; a search-graph subcommand in crates/nest-cli/src/cmd;
            expose pattern queries in nest-python
    impact: high
    effort: high
    from:   rdflib (3-permutation index, most-bound-first reorder), oxigraph (SPOG permutations + prefix range
            scans), rdf4j (permutation indexes, varint IDs), terminusdb-store
            (adjacency-as-LogArray+BitIndex)

* Add a two-level rank/select BitIndex primitive (64-bit blocks, 52-block superblocks, popcount
  tables stored as LogArrays, plus the cache-line linear-bitscan shortcut when a range fits 512
  bytes) for sparse presence/tombstone bitmaps at ~1.05 bits/bit with O(1) navigation. Enables
  optional-field presence maps and a deleted-chunks tombstone bitmap for real-time edit, and
  underpins pointer-free graph adjacency and BM25 posting navigation.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/succinct/{logarray.rs,bitindex.rs} (each <300 lines, little-endian words
            per constraint); consumed by the graph and bm25 modules
    impact: medium
    effort: medium
    from:   terminusdb-store (two-level rank/select BitIndex)

* Implement an append-only delta-layer section (reserved id 0x09+) for real-time edit: each edit
  is an immutable additive section of added/removed chunk_ids + embeddings, resolved newest-wins
  at query time, with periodic rollup that rewrites a fresh base .nest. Map the content-addressed
  layer naming onto nest's existing content_hash + nest://content_hash/chunk_id citation model so
  citations stay byte-exact, and budget read-amplification growth (mitigated by rollup)
  explicitly.
    where:  new optional section family in crates/nest-format/src/layout + reader resolution order in
            crates/nest-format/src/reader; runtime newest-wins overlay in
            crates/nest-runtime/src/mmap_file.rs
    impact: medium
    effort: high
    from:   terminusdb-store (content-addressed immutable delta layers + rollup)

* Adopt the xloader external-merge-sort pattern for the deterministic builder so corpora exceeding
  RAM still build reproducibly: stream chunk/term tuples to spill files, external-merge-sort with
  stable alphabetical order (matches the bm25 deterministic-term-order constraint), zstd the
  intermediate spill runs to cut peak temp disk, then build hnsw_index(0x07)/bm25_index(0x08) and
  any graph section as separate streamed passes. Removes the in-RAM ceiling without touching the
  frozen format or determinism guarantee.
    where:  crates/nest-python/src/build_fn.rs (builder driver) with sort/spill helpers; no format change
    impact: medium
    effort: medium
    from:   apache/jena (xloader: external sort, compressed spill runs, per-index streamed phases)

* Add a golden-file search-conformance harness that machine-checks the search_contract: assert
  ann/hybrid results rerank to identical real-cosine top-k across exact/tiny/hybrid presets,
  proving query-layer determinism the way content_hash proves storage-layer determinism. Pure test
  scaffolding; the additive v1 format is untouched.
    where:  crates/nest-cli/tests (alongside cli_e2e.rs) and crates/nest-runtime/tests
    impact: low
    effort: low
    from:   opencypher (TCK canonical result serialization + ordered-vs-set comparison)

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - LogArray on HNSW neighbor IDs: the tiny preset's index section currently stores every edge as
    a fixed u32; packing at ceil(log2(N)) bits removes 10-20 wasted high bits per neighbor across
    the densest part of the smallest preset, directly lowering the 0.283 size ratio with zero
    query-time decode.
  - LogArray on offset/length arrays (chunks_original_spans, per-section length tables, chunk
    index): ~64k chunks need ~17 bits not 32/64, roughly halving those sections while keeping O(1)
    mmap random access for cite/materialize.
  - Plain Front Coding on sorted string sections (BM25 vocabulary, chunk_ids, provenance, future
    json/yaml/dataset keys): prefix sharing beats per-field zstd on sorted short
    strings/URIs/paths and stays binary-searchable, a domain-specific entropy win orthogonal to
    and stackable with zstd.
  - Delta+varint BM25 posting lists: monotone doc IDs become small gaps, and order-preserving
    varint shrinks both doc gaps and tf to 1-2 bytes vs fixed 8 bytes/posting on the largest part
    of the bm25 section.
  - Dictionary-encode repeated provenance/metadata into one per-file string/namespace table
    storing only small IDs: redundancy becomes integer locality, beating zstd-on-raw because dedup
    happens at the semantic-value level before the entropy coder runs.
  - Native typed-column encoding for structured payloads (json/yaml/parquet/datasets):
    integers/dates/bools/decimals stored as i56/i64/i128-fixed-point/ms-epoch instead of
    text+span, eliminating JSON text bloat and per-value span offsets that zstd still pays for,
    then optionally zstd-wrapped on top.
  - Graph-edge permutations compress near-free: each SPO/OSP permutation is a sorted integer
    sequence, so delta-coding + zstd makes the multi-permutation redundancy cheap while unlocking
    direction-agnostic queries; cross-context edge sharing dedups when swallowing whole
    databases/repos.
  - Succinct query-in-place is the strategic win: LogArray/PFC/BitIndex give near-entropy size
    WITH random access, the missing middle between raw-f32 and zstd-block, letting nest compress
    every non-embedding integer/string/offset/index section without the full-block decode zstd
    forces, while leaving the embedding hot path untouched.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Big-endian assumption: RDF stores use big-endian keys so lexicographic byte order equals
    numeric order, enabling comparator-free range scans. nest mandates little-endian, so naive LE
    packing breaks byte-order==value-order. Either store keys pre-sorted and binary-search by
    decoded value, or use an explicit order-preserving codec; do not silently inherit the
    big-endian range-scan trick.
  - Permutation index storage multiplies: 6 SPOG permutations cost ~6x index storage (RDF4J
    ~120-130 bytes/quad at 3 permutations). Graph/triple indexing MUST be an optional section
    (0x09+) gated by a capability flag, and nest should ship the minimum permutation set (e.g. SPO
    + one inverse), or default builds stop being tiny.
  - Do NOT vendor RocksDB (Oxigraph), LMDB (RDF4J), tokio (terminusdb-store), or any Java
    (Jena/RDF4J/RDFLib): these bring C++ LSM, server/async, or JVM dependencies that violate
    nest's small single-file offline-by-construction core. Clean-room the encoding ideas in Rust;
    reimplement layouts as flat mmap'd sorted sections, which is simpler and faster to read than
    an LSM and matches nest's zero-server model.
  - Keep the never-compress-embeddings rule intact: every steal here targets non-embedding
    sections (IDs, strings, offsets, indexes, metadata, graph edges). The dense embedding matrix
    stays raw f32/f16/int8 scored from mmap on the hot path; succinct structures do NOT shrink it
    and must not be placed in its path.
  - Substitute nest's SHA-256 for Oxigraph's SipHash and terminusdb's MD5/20-byte content hashes
    to preserve the single four-integrity-surface hash family; SHA-256 is slower but is paid at
    build time, not on the search hot path, so there is no runtime cost.
  - Content-addressed delta layers add read amplification that grows with stack depth; adopting
    append-only edit layers requires periodic rollup and an explicit depth budget, and must
    preserve content_hash/citation byte-exactness so nest://content_hash/chunk_id never breaks.
  - Determinism: HNSW must stay seeded and BM25 alphabetical; PFC bucketing, LogArray width
    selection, varint encoding, and any graph term-id densification must be a pure deterministic
    function of sorted input so same chunks + same model fingerprint still yields a byte-identical
    file. Most-bound-first BGP reordering needs an explicit id-order tiebreak to stay
    reproducible.
  - Additive-only discipline: use reserved encoding ids 4-255 and section ids 0x09+; bump only the
    in-section payload version (HNSW_PAYLOAD_VERSION, BM25_PAYLOAD_VERSION) for codec changes,
    never NEST_FORMAT_VERSION, and never let new optional sections enter content_hash (which is
    the canonical six only) or citations across corpora break.
  - Library code must never panic: all new codecs (LogArray width overflow, PFC bucket bounds,
    varint EOF, graph term-id range) must return typed thiserror NestError/RuntimeError, matching
    the existing ByteCursor EOF-as-error pattern in ann/codec.rs and bm25/codec.rs, not
    unwrap/index-panic.
  - File hygiene: every new module must stay <=300 lines (333 hard cap) and single-responsibility.
    Split graph into term_dict.rs/index.rs/join.rs and succinct into
    logarray.rs/bitindex.rs/pfc.rs rather than one large file.
  - openCypher is near-zero compression value: do not invest in Cypher grammar/TCK as a
    compression source. Borrow only the conformance-test methodology for proving search
    determinism, and only if cheap.


##################################################################################################
# embedded-db  -  embedded / datalog / temporal / kv / column storage engines (edit + real-time)
##################################################################################################

summary:     This cohort (datahike, datascript, xtdb, buntdb, tigerbeetle, rocksdb, duckdb, upscaledb)
             converges on a handful of patterns that map almost 1:1 onto nest's three biggest open
             gaps: real-time edit on a frozen single-file container, deeper compression of
             non-embedding sections, and large-corpus serving without paging cold data. The two
             highest-leverage steals are RocksDB's cross-block trained ZSTD dictionary (the single
             biggest size lever for nest's many-small-similar-chunk PT-BR corpus, where
             per-section zstd today cannot see cross-chunk redundancy) and the upscaledb/Lemire
             delta + SIMD bit-packing family (StreamVByte / SIMD-BP128) for the integer sections
             chunk_ids and chunks_original_spans, which are exactly the small/monotone arrays zstd
             handles poorly and which nest can search/materialize directly off mmap. DuckDB
             contributes the architectural pattern that should govern all of this: a deterministic
             Analyze-and-pick codec selection per section instead of fixed per-preset encodings,
             plus FSST for random-access text compression that fits the cite/materialize path
             better than zstd frames. For the edit pillar, BuntDB's AOF+Shrink and
             datahike/datascript's content-addressed structural-sharing tree both show the same
             answer: never mutate frozen v1 bytes; append a typed journal (new reserved section
             0x09+) overlaid in memory at open, with a deterministic `compact` that re-derives a
             minimal maximally-compressed container (the exact moment to re-run the trained
             dictionary and re-quantize). TigerBeetle's EWAH bitset and out-of-band
             content-addressed checksums, plus XTDB's per-file zone-maps + recency scalar, give
             the supporting machinery: compact scannable tombstone/liveness sets, a Merkle-root
             content_hash enabling single-chunk citation verification, and block-level
             skip-indexes so a large .nest stays fast. Every recommendation below respects nest's
             hard constraints: additive-only within v1 (reserved encoding ids 4-255, section ids
             0x09+), embeddings stay raw/f16/int8 scored straight from mmap (never zstd),
             deterministic/seeded, offline by construction, typed thiserror (never panic), and
             <=300 lines per source file.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The state of the art for embedded edit+realtime engines is a layered stack: (1) an
append-only/immutable substrate where edits never rewrite existing bytes -- either an AOF command
log replayed on open (buntdb), an LSM of immutable files (rocksdb, xtdb), or a copy-on-write
content-addressed tree where an edit touches ~log_b(N) nodes and unchanged nodes are shared by
hash (datahike/datascript). (2) Content-addressing as integrity AND dedup: node/block id =
hash(contents), so identical sub-structures across versions/corpora collapse to one physical block
(datahike SHA-512, tigerbeetle 128-bit AEGIS with the checksum stored out-of-band in the parent
reference, defeating torn/misdirected writes). (3) Lightweight, scannable, per-segment-adaptive
compression that decodes faster than memcpy and is operated on without full decompression:
DuckDB's cascade (RLE/FOR/delta/dictionary/FSST/ALP) chosen by a sampling Analyze phase; RocksDB's
cross-block trained ZSTD dictionary + Ribbon filters (~7 vs ~10 bits/key) + delta/restart key
encoding; upscaledb's SIMD-BP128/StreamVByte delta-bitpacking at 4-8 billion ints/sec;
tigerbeetle's EWAH run-length bitmaps operated on in compressed form. (4) Cheap per-block summary
statistics for skip/prune: zone-maps (min/max), Bloom/Ribbon membership filters, and a collapsed
monotone "recency" scalar so temporal/current-state queries elide whole cold files before touching
payload (xtdb). (5) Temporal model as append-only facts with an assertion/retraction flag and tx
ids, so time-travel (as-of/history) is a filter, never a mutation (datahike datoms in
EAVT/AEVT/AVET covering indexes; xtdb bitemporal recency). The unifying lesson for nest:
compression, integrity, dedup, and edit are not separate subsystems -- content-addressed immutable
blocks + adaptive lightweight codecs + per-block skip metadata deliver all four at once, and a
periodic deterministic compaction is where extreme compression re-enters after cheap appends.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Cross-block trained ZSTD dictionary (rocksdb): train ONE ZDICT dictionary by sampling across
   all text chunks, compress each section against it. Captures cross-chunk redundancy (repeated
   names/boilerplate in PT-BR fake-news articles) that nest's current per-section zstd::encode_all
   at level 19 cannot see. Deterministic given pinned zstd + sorted input. Highest single ROI on
   'compress more than everyone', zero recall cost (embeddings excluded).
2. Delta + SIMD bit-packing for integer sections (upscaledb / lemire simdcomp+streamvbyte,
   Apache-2.0/patent-free): SIMD-BP128 or StreamVByte over chunk_ids and chunks_original_spans
   reaches 1-4 bits/int on monotone/near-monotone arrays vs zstd's byte-granular framing overhead,
   AND decodes faster than memcpy with operate-in-register random access for citation/materialize
