   -- a rare codec that improves size and speed simultaneously.
3. DuckDB per-section Analyze-and-pick codec selection: a deterministic sampling estimate chooses
   the smallest codec per section instead of fixed per-preset encodings. Turns nest's presets from
   hardcoded choices into an automatic 'pick smallest' machine while preserving byte-identical
   determinism (seeded/ordered sampling).
4. FSST random-access text compression (duckdb, MIT): 255-entry static symbol table, ~2-3x on text
   like zstd but decodes a single string without inflating a whole frame -- a strictly better fit
   than zstd for nest's nest://content_hash/chunk_id cite/materialize path.
5. AOF + Shrink edit model (buntdb, MIT): append typed edit records to a journal overlaid in
   memory at open; never touch frozen v1 bytes; a deterministic compact() re-derives a minimal
   container. The cleanest way to add the real-time-edit pillar while preserving
   single-file/deterministic guarantees, and compact is exactly where re-compression (trained
   dict, re-quantize, drop dead chunks, rebuild to tiny preset) happens.
6. Per-block content-addressed structural sharing (datahike/datascript, EPL -- learn not vendor):
   block id = hash(contents) so duplicate chunks/embeddings/index-nodes across versions or similar
   corpora dedup to one physical block; an edit rewrites only ~3 dirty nodes. Dedup-as-compression
   for multi-version/multi-corpus .nest files.
7. EWAH run-length bitmap codec (tigerbeetle, Apache-2.0): in-place-scannable compression of
   sparse boolean id-sets with a hard 'never >0.1% larger than raw' guarantee -- ideal for nest's
   coming tombstone/liveness/doc-presence sets, scannable off mmap without decompression.
8. Zone-map + Bloom/Ribbon skip metadata per block (xtdb, rocksdb): cheap per-block min/max +
   membership filter lets search.rs and bm25 skip blocks that cannot beat the current top-k cosine
   bound or contain a query term, cutting SIMD dot products and mmap page faults so a large single
   .nest stays fast. Exact rerank keeps correctness regardless of false positives.
9. Out-of-band content-addressed checksums + Merkle-root content_hash (tigerbeetle, datahike):
   store a block's checksum in its parent reference; make content_hash a Merkle root over
   per-section/per-block hashes so a citation can verify a single chunk against the root without
   rehashing the whole file (partial verification).
10. Recency-scalar / append-only fact log for temporal (xtdb, datahike): collapse (valid_from,
   ingested_at) into one monotone u64 'recency' kept sorted, and model edits/retractions as
   append-only [chunk_id, op, tx] facts so 'serve current state' and 'as-of T' become a
   deterministic filter over tx ids -- the temporal pillar with no mutation of frozen sections.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Trained ZSTD dictionary codec for text sections. Add a new RESERVED encoding id (e.g.
  SECTION_ENCODING_ZSTD_DICT=4) in layout/mod.rs; in writer, train ONE ZDICT dictionary over
  chunks_canonical + chunks_original_spans + provenance via the zstd crate's ZDICT bindings,
  compress each against it, and persist the digested dictionary either as a new optional section
  (0x09) or a manifest field. Reader gains a decode arm in encoding/mod.rs decode_payload that
  loads the dict then decompresses. Embeddings untouched.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ (new zstd_dict_codec.rs), layout/mod.rs (new encoding id +
            reserved section), writer/encoding_choice.rs + writer/build.rs (train+emit),
            reader/decode.rs (decode arm)
    impact: Highest single compression lever. Cross-chunk redundancy in the PT-BR corpus (repeated
            names/boilerplate) becomes bytes saved on every text section in compressed/tiny/hybrid
            presets at zero recall cost. Old readers fail closed on the unknown encoding id
            (additive-safe).
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/facebook/rocksdb

* Delta + SIMD bit-packing codec for integer sections. Add encoding id 5 (e.g.
  SECTION_ENCODING_DELTA_BP) and a delta_bitpack.rs codec (blocks of 128, per-block bit-width
  header) for chunk_ids and chunks_original_spans. chunk_ids are sha256: strings today -- pair
  this with storing ids as raw u64 hashes (or delta-coded sorted ints) so they delta-code to ~1-4
  bits. Span (byte_start,byte_end) pairs are near-monotone and delta-code well. Decode kernel
  slots into the existing SIMD dispatcher (avx2 pshufb / neon tbl / scalar) for
  operate-in-register reconstruction during materialize().
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ (new delta_bitpack.rs), sections/chunk_ids.rs + sections/spans.rs
            (encode/decode integration), crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/ (vectorized unpack path)
    impact: Shrinks structural sections below zstd while decoding faster than memcpy; directly tightens the
            tiny preset (today 0.283) and speeds citation/materialize. Improves size AND speed.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/cruppstahl/upscaledb, https://github.com/facebook/rocksdb

* Per-section Analyze-and-pick codec selection. Replace the fixed per-preset SectionEncoding
  choice with a deterministic sampling Analyze pass: for each non-embedding section, cheaply
  estimate compressed size under each candidate codec (raw / zstd / zstd+dict / delta-bitpack /
  fsst) and emit the smallest. Seed/order the sampling so output stays byte-identical across
  builds.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/writer/encoding_choice.rs (turn enums into an estimator), writer/build.rs
            (selection loop)
    impact: Converts presets from hardcoded codecs into an automatic 'pick smallest per section' machine --
            the core 'compress more than everyone' machinery. Preserves determinism via ordered
            sampling.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/duckdb/duckdb

* FSST codec for random-access text. Add encoding id 6 (SECTION_ENCODING_FSST) with a 255-entry
  symbol table for chunks_canonical, giving ~2-3x like zstd but per-string decode straight off
  mmap so materialize/cite resolves one chunk without inflating a whole zstd frame.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ (new fsst.rs), sections/canonical.rs (decode integration),
            crates/nest-runtime materialize path
    impact: Better fit than zstd for the nest://content_hash/chunk_id byte-span reopen path; competitive ratio
            with random access. Complements (not replaces) the trained dict where whole-section
            scan dominates.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/duckdb/duckdb

* Append-only edit journal + deterministic compact. New nest-runtime journal.rs (<300 loc,
  little-endian repr(C), typed thiserror) holding
  add_chunk{content_hash,chunk_id,canonical_bytes,embedding} / del_chunk{chunk_id} records
  appended after the frozen v1 body or as a sidecar; reader overlays journal deltas in memory at
  open (base served from mmap). Per-Tx rollbackItems map for transactional abort. New nest-cli
  `compact` subcommand rewrites base+journal into a fresh deterministic .nest holding only live
  chunks, recomputing all four hashes and re-running the trained dict + re-quantization + index
  rebuild to the tiny preset.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/journal.rs (new), reader overlay in nest-format/src/reader/,
            crates/nest-cli/src/cmd/ (new compact.rs)
    impact: Unlocks the real-time-edit north-star pillar without bumping v2 or mutating frozen sections;
            compact is where extreme re-compression re-enters after cheap appends. Same live set
            => byte-identical output (determinism law holds).
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/tidwall/buntdb, https://github.com/replikativ/datahike,
            https://github.com/tonsky/datascript

* EWAH liveness/tombstone bitset codec. Add an EWAH word-aligned-hybrid bitset as encoding id 7
  (or an internal representation) for a deletion/tombstone bitmap, an hnsw live-node mask, and
  bm25 doc-presence sets. Operated on in compressed form off mmap; deterministic by ascending id
  order; <=300-line single-responsibility module.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ (new ewah.rs), used by the edit-log section and
            crates/nest-runtime/src/ann + bm25 liveness masks
    impact: Near-free, in-place-scannable compression of the sparse boolean id-sets edit/tombstones need, with
            a hard 'never materially larger than raw' guarantee. Enables soft-delete before the
            next compaction.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/tigerbeetle/tigerbeetle

* Per-block zone-map + Ribbon/Bloom skip section. New optional section_zonemaps (0x09): per-block
  embedding coarse centroid or per-dim min/max, plus a deterministic Ribbon filter (~30% smaller
  than Bloom at equal FP, clean-room from the Dietzfelbinger-Walzer paper) over bm25 terms /
  provenance source-ids. search.rs and bm25 skip blocks whose summary cannot beat the current
  top-k cosine bound or whose filter cannot contain a query term, before issuing SIMD dot
  products. Exact cosine rerank stays ground truth so false positives never cause wrong answers.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/manifest + sections (new section_zonemaps),
            crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs (block-skip in score_all),
            crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/
    impact: Lets one .nest hold far more data while staying fast -- cold blocks never paged or scored. The
            scaling axis of 'compress AND serve whole databases'. Ribbon also shrinks the
            membership filter itself.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/xtdb/xtdb, https://github.com/facebook/rocksdb

* Merkle-root content_hash for partial verification. Restructure hashing so content_hash is a
  Merkle root over per-section (and eventually per-block) content_hashes, and store each section's
  checksum in the manifest/parent reference (tigerbeetle out-of-band rule). A citation
  nest://content_hash/chunk_id can then verify a single chunk against the root without rehashing
  the whole file.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/writer/build.rs (footer_hash/content_hash computation),
            layout/section_entry.rs + manifest (checksum placement), reader/validate.rs
    impact: Enables single-chunk integrity verification (cheap citation trust) and is the precondition for
            per-block content-addressed structural sharing/dedup in a future edit region.
            Reinforces reproducible-build story.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/tigerbeetle/tigerbeetle, https://github.com/replikativ/datahike

* Recency-scalar temporal index for as-of queries. Store each chunk version's (valid_from,
  ingested_at) collapsed into one monotone u64 'recency' in the edit-log/journal section, kept
  sorted; model retractions as append-only [chunk_id, op, tx] facts. 'Serve current state' and
  'as-of T' become a deterministic filter over tx ids; a survivor/historical split keeps hot
  current-state scans off cold history.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/journal.rs (recency + fact tuples), search.rs (as-of filter before
            scoring)
    impact: Adds the temporal/time-travel pillar deterministically without mutating frozen sections;
            historical versions compact into denser blocks that the trained dict compresses well.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/xtdb/xtdb, https://github.com/replikativ/datahike

* ALP + FastLanes-style transposed bitpacking as the principled embedding quantization successor
  and SIMD-path unifier. Evaluate ALP (lossless float) and the 04261537 transposed bitpacking
  layout so one scalar decode kernel auto-vectorizes across avx2/neon/scalar -- potentially
  collapsing nest's three SIMD dot paths into one portable kernel while shrinking embeddings
  better than naive f16/int8 without recall loss. Gate strictly on keeping cheap random decode for
  the mmap hot path.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ (new alp.rs, embedding-only), crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/
            (unified kernel evaluation)
    impact: Could improve embedding size at zero recall cost and reduce SIMD-path maintenance surface. Higher
            risk: must not break direct-from-mmap f32 scoring.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/duckdb/duckdb

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Trained cross-chunk ZSTD dictionary on chunks_canonical/spans/provenance: captures repeated
    names/boilerplate across PT-BR articles that per-section level-19 zstd cannot see today.
    Biggest single lever; improves every zstd-using preset (compressed/tiny/hybrid) at zero recall
    cost since embeddings are excluded.
  - Delta + SIMD bit-packing (SIMD-BP128/StreamVByte) on chunk_ids and chunks_original_spans: 1-4
    bits/integer vs zstd's byte-granular framing overhead on short monotone int arrays; directly
    shrinks the tiny preset's structural overhead while decoding faster than memcpy.
  - FSST on canonical text: ~2-3x ratio with per-string random-access decode -- compresses the
    cite/materialize path's working bytes without forcing whole-frame inflation, a better
    space/latency tradeoff than zstd frames for citation reopen.
  - EWAH run-length bitmaps for tombstone/liveness/doc-presence sets: near-free compression of
    sparse boolean id-sets with a hard never->0.1%-larger guarantee, scannable in compressed form
    off mmap.
  - Ribbon filters instead of Bloom in the bm25/zonemap region: ~7 vs ~10 bits/key at equal
    false-positive rate -- ~30% smaller membership index, compact probabilistic substitute for an
    exact term dictionary.
  - Per-block content-addressed structural sharing / dedup: duplicate chunks, embeddings, and
    index nodes across versions or similar corpora collapse to one physical block, so a
    multi-version or multi-corpus .nest pays once -- dedup-as-compression for whole evolving
    repos/databases.
  - Per-section Analyze-and-pick codec selection: always emits the smallest available codec per
    section (raw/zstd/zstd+dict/delta-bitpack/fsst), squeezing every section to its best
    lightweight scheme instead of a fixed per-preset choice.
  - Compaction-time re-compression cycle: cheap appends absorb edits, then a deterministic compact
    re-runs the trained dict, re-quantizes embeddings (int8/f16), drops dead chunks, and rebuilds
    indexes to the smallest preset -- so edited corpora periodically re-derive a minimal
    maximally-compressed container instead of bloating.
  - ALP lossless float compression for embeddings (evaluate carefully): potential shrink below
    naive f16/int8 with no recall loss, only if random decode stays cheap enough for the mmap SIMD
    hot path.
  - LSM-concat recency compaction for history: store full edit history as one append-only sorted
    run that compacts/dedups instead of N snapshots, so old versions migrate to denser deeper
    blocks that the trained dict compresses well -- compact way to store full temporal history.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Never zstd/dictionary-compress or heavyweight-encode the embeddings section: it is scored
    f32/f16/i8 directly from mmap on the hot path. Any embedding codec MUST keep cheap random
    decode (ALP/FastLanes may qualify; zstd and FSST do not). nest already correctly bans zstd on
    embeddings -- preserve that invariant in every new codec.
  - Licensing traps: datahike/datascript are EPL-1.0 (weak-copyleft) -- learn the
    content-addressed tree and IStorage ideas, do NOT vendor Clojure source. upscaledb is GPLv3 --
    reimplement from papers; use the Apache-2.0/patent-free lemire/simdcomp and streamvbyte (and
    the permissive Rust ports) instead. rocksdb is Apache/GPLv2 dual -- clean-room Ribbon from the
    public paper, use the zstd crate's native ZDICT bindings, never vendor rocksdb. xtdb is
    MPL-2.0 (file-level copyleft) -- reimplement trie/zone-map/recency. buntdb, duckdb,
    tigerbeetle are MIT/MIT/Apache-2.0 (safe to adapt). The small permissive Rust core must stay
    uncontaminated.
  - Determinism is a hard law: ZDICT must be pinned to a fixed zstd version and fed
    sorted/identical sampled input; the Analyze-and-pick sampler must be seeded/ordered;
    EWAH/Ribbon/delta-bitpack must use fixed block sizes and ascending id order. Any
    nondeterminism breaks 'same chunks + same model fingerprint => byte-identical file' and
    reproducible builds.
  - Additive-only within v1: only consume reserved encoding ids 4-255 and section ids 0x09+; old
    readers must fail closed (UnsupportedSectionEncoding / MissingRequiredSection) on unknown ids.
    Do NOT bump NEST_FORMAT_VERSION unless an existing field changes meaning (a true v2). The edit
    journal and zone-maps must be optional sections, not changes to the frozen six.
  - No panics in library code: all new codecs and the journal must return typed thiserror
    NestError/RuntimeError, including on malformed/truncated/adversarial inputs (mirror the
    existing negative_zstd/negative_int8/negative_fp16 test discipline). Ribbon/Bloom false
    positives must never produce wrong answers -- the mandatory exact cosine rerank is the safety
    net; keep it unconditional.
  - Offline + single-file by construction: konserve-style pluggable KV backends, xtdb
    object-storage/distribution, rocksdb remote caching, and buntdb's whole-dataset-in-RAM model
    all violate nest's model. Adapt only the on-disk/in-process algorithm; drop distribution; keep
    base sections mmap-resident and hold only journal deltas in RAM (do not load the whole corpus
    into memory).
  - File-hygiene cap: each new codec (zstd_dict, delta_bitpack, fsst, ewah, alp) and journal.rs
    must stay <=300 lines (333 hard cap), single-responsibility. The Analyze-and-pick estimator
    and Ribbon construction are the most likely to bloat -- split estimator/codec cleanly.
  - Build-CPU cost asymmetry is acceptable but bounded: Ribbon construction is ~4-5x Bloom, ZDICT
    training and FSST table-building add a build pass, ALP/transposed-bitpacking add kernel
    complexity. nest builds in Python offline so one-time build cost is fine, but it must not
    regress the runtime hot path or the deterministic-build reproducibility guarantee.
  - Quantization/lossy interactions with content_hash: content_hash is over decoded canonical
    sections and must stay STABLE across encodings for non-quantized sections. Lossy embedding
    codecs (int8 today, ALP-exception paths tomorrow) already make content_hash encoding-dependent
    for embeddings -- keep the documented rule that content_hash equality holds only for
    non-quantized sections, and do not let a new text codec accidentally change decoded bytes.
  - CJK/Thai tokenizer gap compounds with bm25 skip-filters: a Ribbon/Bloom term filter built on
    the current word-segmented tokenizer inherits its degradation on CJK/Thai. Do not let the
    skip-filter silently drop candidates for those languages -- ensure the filter is advisory
    (skip only) with exact rerank as backstop, and flag the tokenizer as the real fix.


##################################################################################################
# fulltext-search  -  full-text and lexical search engines (inverted index, BM25, edge cases)
##################################################################################################

summary:     Across 16 lexical engines (Lucene, Tantivy/Quickwit/Toshi/Summa, Meilisearch, Typesense,
             Manticore/MCL, RediSearch, Xapian, SeekStorm, Sonic, tinysearch, Stork, FlexSearch,
             Michael-JB/bm25) the field has converged on one canonical, near-entropy posting codec
             and one canonical term-dictionary structure, both of which nest is currently NOT
             using. nest's bm25 section (0x08) today (verified in
             crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/codec.rs:14-39) writes every posting as a raw fixed 8
             bytes (u32 doc + u32 tf), raw u32 doc_lengths (4 bytes/doc), and length-prefixed
             UTF-8 terms with a 4-byte length prefix each; index.rs::search (lines 101-115) does
             exhaustive term-at-a-time OR touching every posting. This is the single
             least-compressed, least-optimized section in the whole container and is squarely on
             the 'compress more than everyone' critical path for the hybrid and tiny presets. The
             state of the art replaces all three layers losslessly and deterministically: (1)
             postings = delta-gap doc ids + per-128-block minimal-bit-width bitpacking
             (FOR/PForDelta/BP128) with a varint tail, typically collapsing 8 bytes/posting to 1-2
             bytes; (2) term dictionary = an FST (shared prefix AND suffix minimization,
             BurntSushi `fst` crate, MIT/Unlicense) that also unlocks prefix/range/typo lookup for
             free; (3) doc lengths = 1-byte log-quantized fieldnorm or a min+bitpacked column.
             Query-side, Block-Max WAND / MaxScore turns the O(all postings) OR into
             early-terminating top-k by storing per-block max-impact bounds. Every one of these is
             a pure internal payload change inside the already-reserved 0x08 section gated by
             BM25_PAYLOAD_VERSION (currently 1 in index.rs:8), needs NO NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump,
             stays deterministic (terms already sorted alphabetically, bit-widths are pure
             functions of the data), decodes branch-free off mmap (matching nest's embedding
             hot-path discipline), and is recall-safe because BM25 is only a candidate generator
             before nest's mandatory exact-cosine rerank.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The settled lexical-index stack is: FST term dictionary (term -> ordinal, mmap zero-copy,
O(term-len) lookup, free prefix/range/Levenshtein scan) over 128-doc posting BLOCKS where doc ids
are delta-encoded then bitpacked at each block's local minimal bit-width b=ceil(log2(max_gap+1)),
term-freqs bitpacked in a parallel block stream, with a VInt/varint remainder for the trailing
partial block and rare terms (df<128). A per-block skip list (last_doc_id, byte offset,
max_term_freq, fieldnorm bound) drives Block-Max WAND / MaxScore dynamic top-k pruning so query
cost scales with surviving blocks, not corpus size (production reports ~10-20% of exhaustive-OR
time). Doc lengths compress to 1 byte via BM25 length-saturation (log-quantized fieldnorm).
Roaring bitmaps (array/bitmap/run containers per 64k chunk, ~1.25 bits/doc dense) are the
alternative posting encoding when SIMD set-intersection on the compressed form matters (SeekStorm,
Meilisearch). The whole index is immutable, self-describing, byte-range addressable and served
straight off mmap with no decompression of the whole section (Quickwit/Summa prove
block-addressable lexical search; Sonic proves mmap'd FST vocab at a few MB RAM). bitpacking
decode hits 5-7 billion int/s SIMD with a clean scalar fallback (the `bitpacking` crate, Tantivy's
subcrate, MIT). Manticore/MCL adds per-block ADAPTIVE codec selection (const /
dictionary-when-<256-uniques / delta+bitpack / raw, chosen by cheapest cost per block) plus
min-max zone maps for skip-without-decode. The frontier extremes: tinysearch's per-document
Xor8/BinaryFuse8 filters (~9 bits/unique-token, no postings at all) for an ultra-tiny lexical
channel, and FlexSearch's build-time ordinal-bucket pre-scoring (score becomes a 4-bit ordinal,
not an f32). All the reusable building blocks are permissively licensed
(Tantivy/fst/bitpacking/roaring/xorf all MIT or Apache); the GPL/AGPL/SSPL daemons (Typesense,
Xapian, Manticore daemon, Quickwit, Summa, RediSearch) are reference-only but their algorithms are
public-domain IR and safe to clean-room in Rust.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Delta-gap + per-128-block minimal-bit-width bitpacking of doc ids with varint tail
   (FOR/PForDelta/BP128) - the universal posting codec, 4-8x lossless shrink of nest's raw 8-byte
   postings, deterministic, branch-free mmap decode
2. FST term dictionary (BurntSushi fst, MIT/Unlicense) - shared prefix+suffix minimization
   compresses vocab and unlocks prefix/range/Levenshtein lookup, closing the typo and CJK/Thai
   gaps
3. Block-Max WAND / MaxScore top-k pruning - turns the exhaustive term-at-a-time OR into
   early-terminating DAAT, recall-safe before the mandatory exact-cosine rerank
4. 1-byte log-quantized fieldnorm - 4x shrink of the raw u32 doc_lengths array, lossless-enough
   under BM25 saturation
5. Separated doc-length stream + sorted-term front-coding - near-free dictionary shrink and a
   simpler stepping stone before a full FST
6. Per-block adaptive codec selection (const/dict/delta-bitpack/raw) + min-max zone maps (MCL) -
   exploits local uniformity zstd misses, reusable across all integer-id sections
7. Per-document Xor8/BinaryFuse8 membership filter (~9 bits/unique-token) - a kilobyte-scale
   lexical channel for a tiny+lexical preset, false positives killed by rerank
8. Build-time ordinal-bucket pre-scoring (FlexSearch) - tf becomes a 4-bit ordinal, doc ids group
   into delta-friendly runs, replaces per-posting float math
9. Roaring-bitmap posting lists with SIMD set-ops on the compressed form - near-optimal for
   sparse-to-dense doc-id sets, reuses the AVX2/NEON dispatcher
10. BM25-as-sparse-embedding (Michael-JB/bm25) - scoring becomes a sparse dot product reusing
   nest's SIMD dispatcher exactly like the dense cosine path

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Replace raw (u32 doc, u32 tf) postings with delta-gap doc ids + per-128-doc-block
  minimal-bit-width bitpacking + a varint tail; tf as a parallel bitpacked block. Postings are
  already doc-sorted (index.rs:69), so gaps are positive and small. Bump BM25_PAYLOAD_VERSION 1->2
  ONLY (index.rs:8); no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION change. Keep the decode kernel scalar (word-at-a-time
  shifts) to honor portability/never-panic, with an optional AVX2/NEON unpack later via the
  existing simd dispatcher.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/codec.rs (rewrite to_bytes/from_bytes postings loop, lines 33-37 and
            72-77); put the bitpack/unpack primitive in a new
            crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/bitpack.rs (~80-120 LOC, under the 300-line cap); update
            the layout doc-comment in bm25/mod.rs:10-28.
    impact: Highest single win. Collapses the dominant posting array from 8 bytes/posting to ~1-2 bytes (4-8x)
            on the PT-BR corpus, shrinking section 0x08 and the hybrid/tiny presets several-fold
            with zero recall loss. Bitpacked ints are near entropy-optimal so it composes with
            (does not fight) the existing zstd-on-non-embedding pass.
    effort: medium
    from:   apache/lucene, quickwit-oss/tantivy, quickwit-oss/quickwit, toshi-search/Toshi,
            typesense/typesense

* Quantize doc_lengths from raw u32 to a 1-byte log-quantized fieldnorm (BM25 length-saturation
  makes the loss negligible and the exact-cosine rerank absorbs it), stored as a flat
  little-endian byte column. Optionally generalize to a min+num_bits bitpacked column for an
  exact-but-compact alternative.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/codec.rs (doc_lengths write at lines 22-24 and read at 55-58); decode
            the quantized length back in index.rs::search where dl is read (line 109). Document
            the quantization table in bm25/mod.rs.
    impact: 4x shrink of the per-doc length array (4 bytes -> 1 byte) with no measurable ranking change after
            rerank. Small but free and clean.
    effort: low
    from:   quickwit-oss/quickwit, toshi-search/Toshi, apache/lucene

* Add Block-Max WAND / MaxScore top-k pruning: store per-128-block max_term_freq (and a
  doc-length-derived BM25 upper bound) in the posting skip header, then make search iterate
  Document-at-a-Time skipping blocks that cannot enter the top-k heap, instead of the current
  exhaustive term-at-a-time OR over every posting.
    where:  new crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/wand.rs (keep WAND out of index.rs to stay under 300 lines, as
            the extracts suggest); rewrite the scoring loop in
            crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/index.rs::search (lines 101-115). Block metadata is
            written in codec.rs alongside the bitpacked blocks.
    impact: Cuts search-text query work to a fraction of corpus size, directly attacking the known latency
            gap. Recall-safe: BM25 only generates candidates for rrf_union (fusion.rs) before the
            mandatory exact-cosine rerank, so skipping/reordering candidates never changes
            returned scores.
    effort: medium
    from:   apache/lucene, quickwit-oss/tantivy, SeekStorm/SeekStorm, izihawa/summa

* Replace the length-prefixed alphabetical term blob with an FST (BurntSushi `fst` crate,
  MIT/Unlicense, pure-Rust, no_std-friendly) built from the already-sorted terms, mapping term ->
  u32 term-ordinal. Deterministic by construction over sorted input. Stage it AFTER the postings
  codec so the two land independently.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/codec.rs term loop (write lines 28-32, read 60-70) and the terms
            HashMap in index.rs (lines 32, 59, 78); add `fst` to crates/nest-runtime/Cargo.toml.
            Consider a separate bm25/dict.rs module to respect the file-size cap and
            single-responsibility rule.
    impact: Compresses the vocabulary via shared prefix+suffix (beats front-coding) and unlocks
            prefix/range/typo lookup for free, closing nest's typo-tolerance and CJK/Thai gaps.
            Only new dependency in the set, but it is the canonical, vetted, permissive choice
            used by 5 of the 16 engines.
    effort: medium
    from:   meilisearch/meilisearch, quickwit-oss/tantivy, izihawa/summa, valeriansaliou/sonic,
            toshi-search/Toshi

* Before (or instead of) the FST dependency, front-code the alphabetical term dictionary: store
  shared-prefix-length-with-previous-term + suffix bytes instead of a full 4-byte length prefix +
  full UTF-8 per term. Nearly free since terms are already sorted, zero new dependency.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/codec.rs term write (lines 28-32) and read (lines 60-70).
    impact: Meaningful dictionary shrink on natural-language PT-BR vocab with no new crate; a clean stepping
            stone that de-risks the FST decision and is reversible.
    effort: low
    from:   apache/lucene, github.com/xapian/xapian-docsprint

* Generalize the bitpack primitive into a reusable per-block adaptive integer codec (const /
  dictionary-when-<256-uniques / delta+bitpack / raw, chosen per block by cheapest cost, with a
  tiny repr(C) LE min-max header) as a NEW additive encoding id in the reserved 4-255 range,
  applied to non-embedding integer sections only (postings, chunk_ids, spans), NEVER embeddings.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ (new module, mirror the int8.rs/float16.rs/zstd_codec.rs pattern;
            register an id beside SECTION_ENCODING_* in layout/mod.rs:59-62) and wire into
            writer/encoding_choice.rs SectionEncoding enum; decode in
            encoding/mod.rs::decode_payload (lines 44-55). Min-max zone maps enable
            skip-without-decode in the bm25 search path.
    impact: Exploits local uniformity that a single global zstd pass misses (const/dict blocks collapse to a
            few bytes), and becomes the shared primitive for every integer-id section in the
            container, advancing compress-more-than-everyone beyond just bm25.
    effort: high
    from:   manticoresoftware/manticoresearch (+ manticoresoftware/columnar, the MCL library),
            RediSearch/RediSearch, quickwit-oss/tantivy

* Add an optional ultra-compact 'membership-only' bm25 codec variant: a deterministic per-chunk
  Xor8/BinaryFuse8 filter (xorf crate, MIT/Apache) over the chunk token set, stored as flat
  little-endian arrays one filter per chunk_id, probed in place off mmap with zero
  deserialization. Used as a cheap candidate generator (AND query terms, score by hit count)
  feeding RRF; the exact-cosine rerank removes the ~0.39% false positives.
    where:  a new codec variant selected inside section 0x08 (gated by BM25_PAYLOAD_VERSION) in
            crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/; build-side filter construction in nest-python (O(n)
            peeling is build-time only). Feeds the existing fusion.rs rrf_union path.
    impact: Enables a 'tiny+lexical' preset where the keyword channel costs only ~9 bits/unique-token/chunk
            (kilobytes total) instead of full postings, keeping lexical search alive in the
            smallest preset. Lossy candidate-gen only, never leaks into returned scores.
    effort: medium
    from:   tinysearch/tinysearch

* Upgrade the tokenizer into a swappable pipeline with deterministic normalization presets (raw ->
  diacritic-fold -> Snowball stem -> optional CJK/Thai n-gram/bigram segmentation), keeping the
  current behavior as the default preset so builds stay byte-reproducible. Pair with FST
  Levenshtein lookup for typo tolerance. The exact-cosine rerank means tokenizer changes only
  affect candidate recall, not final scores.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/tokenize.rs (currently a single fixed function, lines 8-28);
            introduce a Tokenizer trait shape and presets while preserving the default. Both build
            (index.rs:42) and query (index.rs:97) must use the identical preset for determinism.
    impact: Closes the documented CJK/Thai word-segmentation gap and adds typo/prefix search; FST-driven
            normalization also collapses surface forms, shrinking the dictionary. Must be opt-in
            per preset to preserve byte-identical reproducible builds.
    effort: medium
    from:   meilisearch/meilisearch, nextapps-de/flexsearch, Michael-JB/bm25, typesense/typesense

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Postings: delta-gap doc ids + per-128-block minimal-bit-width bitpacking + varint tail
    replaces raw 8 bytes/posting (u32 doc + u32 tf) -> ~1-2 bytes/posting, a 4-8x structural
    shrink of the dominant array in section 0x08. Near entropy-optimal so it stacks with the
    existing zstd-on-non-embedding pass for residual gains.
  - tf stream: most term frequencies are 1-2, so a parallel bitpacked tf block needs ~2-4 bits
    each vs the current full 4-byte u32 (8-16x on the tf column alone).
  - Doc lengths: 1-byte log-quantized fieldnorm replaces raw u32 doc_lengths -> 4x shrink (4 bytes
    -> 1 byte per doc), lossless-enough under BM25 length saturation and fully absorbed by the
    exact-cosine rerank.
  - Term dictionary: FST (shared prefix+suffix minimization) or front-coding (shared-prefix-len +
    suffix) replaces full 4-byte-length-prefixed UTF-8 per term -> large vocabulary shrink on
    natural-language PT-BR terms, with FST also delivering prefix/typo lookup for free.
  - Per-block adaptive codec (const / dict<256 / delta+bitpack / raw) collapses locally-uniform
    integer regions to a handful of bytes that a single global zstd pass misses, applicable to
    postings, chunk_ids, spans, and future HNSW edge lists alike.
  - Ordinal-bucket pre-scoring turns the per-posting tf into a 4-bit ordinal and groups doc ids
    into monotonic delta-friendly runs, which then zstd-compress far better than interleaved
    (doc,tf) pairs.
  - Xor8/BinaryFuse8 per-chunk filters give a whole lexical channel at a fixed ~9
    bits/unique-token with no postings/varints, an order of magnitude under full BM25, enabling a
    tiny+lexical preset measured in kilobytes.
  - Roaring bitmap posting lists (array/bitmap/run containers, ~1.25 bits/doc dense) are
    near-optimal for sparse-to-dense doc-id sets and still SIMD-intersectable on the compressed
    form, an alternative size win for dense terms. Net effect: section 0x08, today the
    least-compressed section in the container, becomes one of the most-compressed, advancing the
    compress-more-than-everyone north star specifically on the hybrid and tiny presets.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - SeekStorm's sharding makes BM25 scores non-deterministic and non-comparable across shards
    (per-shard avgdl, df, posting lengths, load-balanced non-deterministically). This would break
    nest's byte-identical reproducible builds and global-stats ranking. Steal only the
    encoding/pruning/SIMD-set-ops; keep a single deterministic non-sharded index with global stats
    and alphabetical term order.
  - License hygiene: Typesense (GPL-3.0), Xapian (GPL-2.0+), Manticore daemon (GPL-3.0), Quickwit
    (AGPL-3.0), Summa (AGPL-3.0), RediSearch (RSALv2/SSPLv1, not OSI-open) must NOT be vendored or
    copied. Their algorithms (FOR, delta+varint, BMW, range trees, trie-Levenshtein) are public IR
    and safe to clean-room in Rust. Only depend on the permissive building blocks: fst
    (MIT/Unlicense), bitpacking (MIT), roaring-rs (Apache/MIT), xorf (MIT/Apache), the Michael-JB
    bm25 ideas (MIT).
  - Any FST or quantization change to section 0x08 must stay an INTERNAL payload change gated by
    BM25_PAYLOAD_VERSION (index.rs:8, currently 1), NOT a NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump: 0x08 is
    optional/additive and its section meaning is unchanged. Bumping the frozen format version for
    an internal codec swap would violate the additive-only v1 rule.
  - Determinism must be preserved end to end: bit-widths must be a pure function of the sorted
    data, FST built over already-alphabetical terms (canonical), bucket boundaries fixed, hashing
    seeded. Do NOT adopt hash-based token IDs (Michael-JB/bm25, Sonic) for the canonical
    dictionary: collisions plus non-stable ordering break byte-reproducibility and reopenable
    nest:// citations. Keep an explicit sorted term dictionary.
  - Lossy tricks (fieldnorm quantization, ordinal-bucket scores, Xor-filter false positives,
    tokenizer normalization changes) are only safe because BM25 is a candidate generator before
    the mandatory exact-cosine rerank. They must NEVER leak into the returned score and must NOT
    touch the embedding hot path (embeddings stay raw f32/f16/int8 for direct SIMD scoring per
    nest's codec rules). Do not apply stream/columnar compression to embeddings.
  - SIMD bitpacking is optional, not required: ship a scalar word-at-a-time unpack first (matches
    the existing avx2/neon/scalar dispatcher and the never-panic + portability constraints), add
    vectorized unpack later. Do not introduce a hard dependency on a crate whose scalar fallback
    is unacceptable or which can panic in library code.
  - Skip the remote/IPFS/network Directory ideas (Summa, Quickwit object-store, Sonic RocksDB,
    SeekStorm RAM-vs-mmap server) and the 32KB-chunk HTTP caching: nest is offline-by-construction
    and never opens a socket; the OS page cache already demand-pages the mmapped file. Borrow only
    the block-addressable on-disk layout philosophy.
  - Respect file hygiene: keep the block codec in codec.rs/bitpack.rs, WAND in wand.rs, FST dict
    in dict.rs, each single-responsibility and under the 300-line cap (333 hard). Do NOT adopt
    rmp-serde/MessagePack (Stork) or Toshi/Summa server frontends: nest's existing repr(C) LE +
    zstd-on-non-embedding already beats raw MessagePack and the server layers are out of scope.
  - FlexSearch/Stork/tinysearch are in-memory or WASM-heap-resident designs that get slow above
    ~1MB corpora or on short queries; borrow their build-time-precompute and ordinal/filter size
    tricks, not their resident-memory query model. nest's mmap-and-skip discipline is already
    superior.


##################################################################################################
# embeddings-models  -  embedding and representation models (text + image, including tiny/static)
##################################################################################################

summary:     The embeddings-models corpus splits cleanly into two layers for nest, which only consumes float
             outputs (never runs inference): (1) a build-side production layer (CLIP,
             BERT/WordPiece, sentence-transformers, BGE-M3, Qwen3-Embedding, Model2Vec, SetFit,
             rust-sbert, rust-bert, word2vec, GloVe) that pins the post-encoder convention and
             ships cheap deterministic encoders; and (2) a storage/scoring layer that is the real
             prize for the 'compress more than everyone' north star. The collective state of the
             art is converging on one contract that nest already half-implements: produce
             L2-normalized unit vectors so cosine == dot product (CLIP, word2vec, all ST models),
             then aggressively compress the embeddings section along FOUR orthogonal,
             multiplicative axes -- value precision (f16/int8, already in nest), dimensionality
             (Matryoshka truncate-then-renormalize: Qwen3/BGE/ST), sub-byte vector quantization
             (fastText product quantization, ~8x), and representation sparsity (SPLADE/BGE-M3
             learned-sparse, ~99% zeros, zstd-friendly) -- with quality guarded by exactly nest's
             existing pattern: a lossy form for candidate generation plus a mandatory exact-cosine
             rerank that makes the returned score honest. nest's int8 'tiny' preset (0.283 ratio,
             recall@10 0.992) is mid-pack; PQ + MRL + sparse stack multiplicatively below it
             toward ~0.10-0.14. The biggest correctness gap (word-segmented BM25 that collapses on
             CJK/Thai) is fixed for free at the tokenizer layer (BERT CJK basic-tokenizer) and
             superseded at the index layer (SPLADE/BGE learned-sparse on subword ids). The
             model-production gap (300-500ms python boot, torch dependency) is closed by Model2Vec
             static distillation, a sub-30MB lookup-table encoder with an official MIT Rust port,
             giving sovereign offline re-embedding. Everything maps to additive-only changes: new
             encoding IDs in the free 4-255 range, new optional sections at 0x09+, and new
             manifest/search_contract fields, with zero NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Convergent contract across the field: encoders emit L2-normalized vectors on the unit hypersphere
so cosine(a,b) == dot(a,b) (CLIP's logit_scale temperature, word2vec normalize-at-load, every
sentence-transformers Normalize module, Qwen3/BGE truncate-then-renormalize). This single
convention -- which nest must PIN in search_contract as 'normalize=l2' -- is the precondition that
makes everything downstream cheap: pure SIMD dot product with no per-query sqrt/div, and
near-lossless int8 because every component lies in a bounded [-1,1] with one symmetric per-vector
scale. On top of that base, four independent compression axes have matured: (A) VALUE PRECISION --
f16 (2x) and int8 (4x), both validated as ~lossless on normalized+PCA-whitened vectors (Model2Vec,
sentence-transformers); nest already ships these. (B) DIMENSIONALITY -- Matryoshka Representation
Learning front-loads information so vec[:d] is independently valid after renormalize; Qwen reports
4096->1024 keeps ~95% recall at 75% storage cut, ST reports 1024->256 at 0.6% drop; a pure
deterministic prefix slice, multiplicative with (A). (C) SUB-BYTE QUANTIZATION -- fastText product
quantization (k=256 subvector k-means -> 1-byte codes, dsub=2 -> 1 byte per 2 dims = 8x base, norm
decoupled, asymmetric LUT scoring with no decode on the hot path) plus binary 1-bit quantization
(sign-threshold + popcount-Hamming, 32x, ST asymmetric rescore recovers ~96% recall). (D) SPARSITY
-- SPLADE (TEI) and BGE-M3 learned-sparse emit vocab-sized ~99%-zero vectors stored as CSR (sorted
term-id deltas + int8/f16 weights), an order of magnitude smaller than dense f32, scored by
integer merge-join, and zstd-compressible because they are off the float hot path. The unifying
retrieval discipline is compress-then-rescore: the lossy/sparse/ANN form does candidate generation
only, then a higher-fidelity rerank produces the final score (CLIP /rank, ST asymmetric rescore,
BGE weighted fusion, TEI cross-encoder) -- structurally identical to nest's mandatory exact-cosine
rerank after hnsw/bm25 RRF, which validates nest's design and means nest can adopt ALL of these as
candidate generators without weakening its 'returned score is real cosine' guarantee. On the
production side, the trend is away from heavy torch towards portable artifacts: a model is 'an
ONNX graph + a vocab' (clip-as-service) or, more radically, 'a [vocab x dim] static lookup table'
(Model2Vec: tokenize + gather + mean-pool, no forward pass, ~500x faster CPU, 8-30MB, PCA-whitened
+ SIF/Zipf-weighted, int8-quantizable, with an official Rust port needing only safetensors +
tokenizers). Multilingual quality (the PT-BR/CJK/Thai concern) is solved at two layers: BERT's
CJK-aware basic tokenizer (insert boundaries around Han codepoints, NFD accent strip, punctuation
isolation) fixes lexical tokenization, and M-CLIP/XLM-R/LaBSE text towers plus SPLADE/BGE subword
sparsity fix it semantically.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Product Quantization with asymmetric LUT scoring (fastText.zip): subvector k-means (k=256 ->
   1-byte codes, dsub=2) stores 1 byte per 2 dims (~8x base, ~16x vs f32) with norm decoupled;
   query builds a per-subvector lookup table once, then each candidate is dim/dsub byte-indexed
   loads + f32 adds with NO float decode on the hot path -- a perfect fit for nest's
   score-off-mmap SIMD model and the single highest-leverage embedding-compression steal.
   Demonstrated 250x model shrink (427MB->1.7MB) with negligible loss.
2. Matryoshka Representation Learning truncate-then-renormalize (Qwen3-Embedding, BGE-M3,
   sentence-transformers): embedding dimensionality becomes a near-lossless deterministic
   compression knob (slice prefix, L2-renormalize) that is multiplicative with int8/f16/PQ and
   also shrinks the HNSW index. 256-of-1024 at int8 = ~16x vs f32-1024. A pure deterministic
   slice, so byte-identical builds hold.
3. Compress-then-rescore as the universal search contract (sentence-transformers binary/int8, CLIP
   /rank, BGE fusion, TEI cross-encoder): store a lossy/sparse form for cheap candidate
   generation, rerank survivors at higher fidelity so the FINAL score is honest. This is nest's
   existing mandatory exact-cosine rerank generalized to the CODEC layer -- it is the ONLY safe
   way to compress embeddings while keeping 'returned score is real cosine', and it licenses
   adopting PQ, binary, and sparse all as candidate generators.
4. Learned sparse vectors (SPLADE in TEI, lexical_weights in BGE-M3): a vocab-sized ~99%-zero
   vector of (token_id, weight) pairs that is a semantic, language-agnostic, drop-in upgrade over
   BM25 tf-idf, stored as CSR (delta-coded ascending term ids + int8/f16 weights), zstd-compressed
   (off the float hot path), and scored by integer merge-join. Simultaneously shrinks the lexical
   index AND fixes nest's CJK/Thai BM25 gap with subword ids.
5. Model2Vec static distillation (MinishLab): collapse a sentence-transformer into a [vocab x dim]
   lookup table so encoding = tokenize + gather + mean-pool (no forward pass), PCA-whitened +
   SIF/Zipf-weighted offline, int8-quantizable, 8-30MB, ~500x faster CPU, dataset-free,
   deterministic, with an official MIT Rust port (safetensors + tokenizers). Gives nest a
   sovereign offline single-file embedder that kills the 300-500ms python boot and the torch
   dependency.
6. L2-normalize as a pinned build-time invariant (CLIP, word2vec, all ST Normalize heads): store
   embeddings pre-normalized so the SIMD dot equals true cosine with zero per-query sqrt/div, AND
   normalize-before-quantize tightens the int8 scale range to lift recall at the same byte cost.
   The precondition that makes every other compression axis safe to validate against a stable
   ground truth.
7. Binary quantization with popcount-Hamming first stage (sentence-transformers): sign-threshold
   normalized f32 to 1 bit/dim (32x), packbits, retrieve by XOR + popcount (~2 CPU cycles, a tiny
   AVX2 popcnt / NEON vcnt kernel), then asymmetric rescore against int8/f32 recovers ~96% recall.
   The cheapest possible first-stage candidate gate, fully inside nest's SIMD dispatcher.
8. BERT CJK-aware basic tokenizer + WordPiece greedy-longest-match: a data-only, model-free,
   deterministic pure function (insert boundaries around Han/CJK codepoint ranges, NFD accent
   strip, punctuation isolation, then greedy ## subword match over an ordered vocab) that fixes
   nest's named CJK/Thai BM25 degradation with ZERO bm25 codec change -- terms stay UTF-8
   length-prefixed alphabetical, section 0x08 byte-identical.
9. PCA-whitening before quantization (Model2Vec, sentence-transformers): decorrelate/normalize the
   embedding space offline (a build-time matrix multiply) so int8/binary/PQ lose ~nothing; ~2.8%
   gain even at full dim. A pure build-side conditioning pass that strengthens every nest codec at
   the same byte cost.
10. Contrastive distillation to a smaller-dimension student (SetFit, BGE self-distillation):
   re-embed a corpus into a lower-dim space (768->256) whose neighbor ordering is preserved,
   attacking the float COUNT (not just width) -- the one axis zstd/int8 cannot touch --
   multiplicative with int8, guarded by exact rerank. A 'nano' preset below tiny.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add Matryoshka dimension-truncation as a build-time codec: nest-python slices each f32 vector to
  a chosen mrl_dim prefix and re-L2-normalizes before writing; store full_dim + mrl_dim in the
  embeddings section header and mirror into search_contract. Runtime needs only honor the smaller
  per-vector stride from the manifest -- existing AVX2/NEON/scalar kernels and the int8 view run
  unchanged on the shorter row. Exact rerank stays true cosine because vectors are renormalized on
  the prefix.
    where:  build: nest-python embedding path (truncate+renormalize); format: new manifest field
            full_dim/mrl_dim + crates/nest-format/src/sections/contract.rs SearchContract (add
            mrl_dim, full_dim as optional fields); runtime: crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/mod.rs
            already strides by dim, no kernel change; nest-cli stats surfaces the size/recall
            tradeoff.
    impact: high
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3-Embedding, https://github.com/FlagOpen/FlagEmbedding,
            https://github.com/UKPLab/sentence-transformers

* Add encoding=4 'pq' (product quantization) as a new embeddings codec in the free 4-255 additive
  range, sibling to int8.rs. On-disk layout mirrors int8.rs prefix conventions: u32
  payload_version, u32 dsub, seeded-k-means codebook (m_subspaces*256*dsub f32 centroids),
  per-vector scalar norm (reuse int8 norm machinery), then n*(dim/dsub) u8 codes. Runtime SIMD
  dispatcher builds the asymmetric query LUT (query dotted vs mmapped codebook via existing dot
  kernels) once per query, scores each candidate as dim/dsub byte-indexed adds off mmap, then
  feeds the mandatory exact-cosine rerank -- PQ is only the ANN coarse filter, never the final
  score. Enables a 'nano' preset (~0.14 ratio) below tiny. k-means MUST be seeded in the python
  builder for byte-identical builds.
    where:  format: crates/nest-format/src/encoding/pq.rs (new) registered in encoding/mod.rs and
            writer/encoding_choice.rs, encoding id 4 in layout/mod.rs; runtime:
            crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/ new PQ scoring path + crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs
            candidate feed into existing rerank; build: seeded k-means + optional greedy coverage
            pruning in nest-python.
    impact: high
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/facebookresearch/fastText

* Add an optional learned-sparse index section at 0x09 (reserved range, additive) storing
  per-chunk ascending (token_id:u32 delta-varint, weight:int8/f16) CSR arrays; zstd the section
  (it is scored by integer merge-join, NOT a SIMD dot off mmap, so zstd is safe here unlike
  embeddings). Generalize the existing Bm25Index into a SparseIndex whose postings hold learned
  weights; s_lex = sum w_q*w_d via merge-join replaces tf-idf BM25 while reusing deterministic
  alphabetical/ascending ordering and the existing reciprocal-rank fusion. nest-python computes
  SPLADE/BGE-M3 lexical_weights at build time; rust does only integer merge-join at serve time
  (stays offline). Candidate generation only; exact-cosine rerank stays ground truth. Replaces the
  word-segmented BM25 (fixes CJK/Thai) AND shrinks the lexical index.
    where:  format: new section 0x09 in layout/mod.rs + crates/nest-format/src/sections/ (CSR codec,
            zstd-eligible); runtime: crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/ generalized to SparseIndex
            (index.rs, codec.rs) reusing fusion.rs RRF; build: nest-python SPLADE/lexical_weights
            step; search_contract gains a fusion descriptor.
    impact: high
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/huggingface/text-embeddings-inference,
            https://github.com/FlagOpen/FlagEmbedding

* Pin 'normalize=l2' as a checked invariant in search_contract and document/enforce L2-normalized
  embeddings: nest-python L2-normalizes (and for int8, normalizes BEFORE quantizing to tighten the
  per-vector scale range). Runtime reads normalize=l2 and runs a pure dot product with zero
  per-query sqrt/div; the exact rerank becomes a bare FMA scan. This is the precondition that
  makes int8/PQ/binary/MRL all safe and validates the 0.992 recall claim against a stable ground
  truth.
    where:  format: crates/nest-format/src/sections/contract.rs (normalize already a field --
            enforce/cross-check at open in reader); build: nest-python normalize-then-quantize
            ordering; runtime: crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/mod.rs and search.rs drop per-query
            norm when normalize=l2.
    impact: high
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/openai/CLIP, https://github.com/tmikolov/word2vec,
            https://github.com/UKPLab/sentence-transformers, https://github.com/cpcdoy/rust-sbert

* Replace the word-split tokenizer with a BERT-style two-stage pipeline: a basic-tokenizer pass
  (lowercase, NFD accent strip already partial for PT-BR, punctuation isolation, insert boundaries
  around CJK/Han codepoint ranges U+4E00-9FFF etc and Thai so ideographic/spaceless scripts
  segment per-char) plus an optional greedy-longest-match WordPiece over a small frozen vocab. The
  on-disk BM25 codec needs ZERO change -- terms stay UTF-8 length-prefixed sorted alphabetical,
  section 0x08 byte-identical, determinism preserved. Clean-room reimplement the algorithm + CJK
  ranges (not vocab.txt).
    where:  runtime: crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/tokenize.rs (rewrite, currently 28 lines
            word-split-on-non-alphanumeric); optional frozen vocab as a new additive section in
            0x09+ range if WordPiece is shipped.
    impact: medium
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/google-research/bert

* Ship a Model2Vec static-embed build mode in nest-python: distill/load a [vocab x dim] lookup
  table (PCA-whitened + SIF/Zipf-weighted baked offline, int8-quantizable, 8-30MB), encode via
  tokenize + gather + mean-pool with no forward pass. Dataset-free, ~500x cheaper CPU, fully
  deterministic (no GPU/transformer nondeterminism) -- directly serving the reproducible-build
  guarantee. Optionally vendor model2vec-rs (MIT, safetensors + tokenizers only) into a runtime
  feature so nest can re-embed query text and edited chunks natively offline, closing the
  300-500ms search-text python boot and giving sovereign single-file embedding with no transformer
  download.
    where:  build: nest-python static-embed mode + model fingerprint in provenance; optional runtime:
            query/edit re-embed path (model2vec-rs deps) gated behind a feature so the small core
            stays lean; static matrix could ship as an optional dictionary section for on-device
            re-embed.
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/MinishLab/model2vec, https://github.com/huggingface/setfit

* Add a build-time PCA-whitening pass before int8/PQ/binary packing in nest-python, storing the
  (deterministic) PCA rotation in provenance so dequant is reproducible. PCA
  decorrelates/normalizes the space (~2.8% quality gain even at full dim per Model2Vec) so
  scalar/sub-byte quantization loses ~nothing -- lifting the int8 'tiny' codec quality at the SAME
  byte cost and de-risking the push to PQ/binary.
    where:  build: nest-python whitening pass (numpy, build-side only) before crates/nest-format/src/encoding
            int8/pq packing; provenance records the rotation + int8 per-vector scale machinery for
            byte-identical dequant.
    impact: medium
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/MinishLab/model2vec, https://github.com/UKPLab/sentence-transformers

* Add a binary (1-bit) shadow codec as a new reserved encoding id for first-stage candidate
  generation: sign-threshold normalized f32 -> 1 bit/dim, packbits (1024d -> 128 bytes, 32x),
  retrieve by XOR + popcount-Hamming (a tiny AVX2 popcnt / NEON vcnt / u64::count_ones scalar
  kernel in the SIMD dispatcher), then rerank survivors with the already-mmapped int8/f32 vectors.
  Recovers ~96% recall via asymmetric rescore; never touches Python; the binary form is
  candidate-gen only so 'score is real cosine' holds.
    where:  format: new encoding id in 4-255 range, packbits shadow alongside embeddings; runtime:
            crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/ popcount-Hamming kernel +
            crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs two-fidelity candidate gather then rerank.
    impact: medium
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/UKPLab/sentence-transformers

* Steal GloVe's external-memory sorted-run + min-heap k-way-merge-with-accumulation for building
  large index sections (bm25/sparse postings, future graph edges) on a minimal-RAM budget: repr(C)
  little-endian fixed-width POD posting records {u32 term_id, u32 doc_id, f32 weight}, sorted into
  runs flushed to temp files, combined by a min-heap merge that accumulates duplicate-key weights
  in one streaming pass. Alphabetical/sorted term order maps directly onto the sorted-merge
  ordering so nest determinism falls out for free; any RNG (e.g. external Fisher-Yates if ever
  needed) must be seeded. Frequency-rank id remapping clusters dense postings at small ids ->
  better zstd ratio on index sections.
    where:  format/build: crates/nest-format/src/writer/ build path, e.g. external_merge.rs
            (single-responsibility, <300 lines); benefits bm25/sparse postings and any future
            graph-search edge section.
    impact: medium
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/stanfordnlp/GloVe

* Add CLIP/M-CLIP image+text shared-space support as the route to the 'fast image similarity'
  north star with ZERO new codec: export a ViT-B/32 (and M-CLIP XLM-R/LaBSE multilingual text
  tower for PT-BR) to ONNX, run it CPU-only in nest-python; L2-normalized 512-d image embeddings
  land in the SAME embeddings section and are scored by the existing SIMD dot dispatcher
  identically to text. Optionally store CLIP's logit_scale temperature scalar in provenance
  (additive, content_hash-safe) for a calibrated logit scoring mode in search_contract. Fully
  additive: no format bump, only embeddings + provenance model fingerprint.
    where:  build: nest-python ONNX CPU encode path (CLIP/M-CLIP) + temperature/model fingerprint in
            provenance; runtime: crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/ scores image vectors with no
            per-modality branch; search_contract optional logit_scale*cosine mode.
    impact: medium
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/openai/CLIP, https://github.com/jina-ai/clip-as-service

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Product quantization (encoding=4, dsub=2, k=256) stores 1 byte per 2 dims = ~8x over raw f32
    and ~2x over the current int8 codec, scored off mmap via asymmetric LUT with no float decode
    and guarded by exact rerank -- the single biggest lever to push embeddings (the largest,
    non-zstd-compressible section) toward a ~0.14 ratio and beat int8's 0.283 tiny preset.
    fastText.zip evidence: 427MB->1.7MB (250x) at negligible loss.
  - Matryoshka dimension truncation is multiplicative with value-precision: 256-of-1024 prefix (4x
    on dims) at int8 (4x on width) = ~16x smaller embeddings than f32-1024, a pure deterministic
    prefix slice with ~0.6-5% recall cost, also shrinking the HNSW index footprint since graph
    vectors are shorter. The one compression axis (float COUNT) that zstd/f16/int8 cannot touch.
  - Learned-sparse (SPLADE/BGE) CSR sections are ~99% zeros: stored as delta-varint ascending term
    ids + int8/f16 weights then zstd'd (safe because off the SIMD hot path), they carry semantic
    keyword search in an order of magnitude fewer bytes than dense f32 or a raw text index -- a
    text-dominant corpus can ship retrieval quality near dense at a fraction of the bytes,
    advancing compress-and-still-search.
  - Binary 1-bit quantization is 32x on the embeddings section (1024d -> 128 bytes) as a
    first-stage candidate gate; asymmetric rescore against the int8/f32 vectors recovers ~96%
    recall, so the 32x form is used only for candidate generation and the final score stays real
    cosine.
  - PCA-whitening + L2-normalization BEFORE quantization is a free quality multiplier: it
    conditions the space so int8/binary/PQ lose ~nothing (Model2Vec reaches 25% of original size
    with no measurable loss; ~2.8% gain even at full dim), letting nest push to lower-bit packing
    at the SAME byte cost and lifting recall at the 0.283 tiny ratio.
  - Contrastive distillation to a smaller-dimension student (768->256) attacks the float COUNT
    directly and composes multiplicatively with int8 (~3x on top of ~4x), plausibly pushing a
    'nano' preset below the current 0.283 tiny ratio, with the exact-cosine rerank guarding recall
    against the lossier embedder.
  - Codebook + residual via topic/IVF centroids (Top2Vec geometry): store K centroids + per-chunk
    topic_id (1-2 bytes) + small residual, exploiting redundancy ACROSS chunks; reordering chunks
    by topic_id also places similar embeddings adjacent, improving byte-locality and zstd ratio on
    the zstd-eligible sections (chunks_canonical, provenance). Auto-generated label terms double
    as a zstd custom dictionary seed for chunks_canonical.
  - Frequency-rank id remapping (GloVe/word2vec/Model2Vec Zipf) on bm25/sparse postings clusters
    dense entries at small ids, directly improving zstd entropy coding on the non-embedding index
    sections at zero quality cost.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - NEVER zstd the embeddings section: it is scored directly from mmap on the SIMD hot path, so
    any whole-section entropy coding breaks score-off-disk. PQ/binary/MRL/int8 are fine because
    they are FIXED-stride codecs read by stride, not stream-decompressed. Sparse/CSR sections
    (0x09) and centroid/topic sections ARE zstd-eligible precisely because they are scored by
    merge-join, not a float dot off mmap -- keep that boundary sharp.
  - Do NOT vendor torch/libtorch (rust-sbert tch-rs, rust-bert) or candle/ONNX-Runtime servers
    into the small rust core: these are hundreds-of-MB native deps that violate
    single-file/offline/small-core. Inference stays build-side in nest-python; the runtime only
    consumes floats. The ONLY runtime-embeddable encoder is Model2Vec (model2vec-rs: safetensors +
    tokenizers, MIT), and even that should be a gated feature, not core.
  - k-means (PQ codebook), PCA rotation, HDBSCAN/UMAP clustering, and any sampling shuffle are
    nondeterministic by default and will break byte-identical builds. PQ k-means and topic
    clustering MUST be seeded; UMAP/HDBSCAN (Numba/SciPy, fragile, non-deterministic) must stay
    python build-side and never enter the rust runtime -- use seeded k-means or fixed-threshold
    greedy cosine agglomeration for any in-runtime clustering. GloVe's default rand_long() shuffle
    must be reseeded if adapted.
  - Adopting a learned subword/WordPiece/SPLADE vocab changes the BM25/sparse term space, so
    determinism requires the vocab be FROZEN and its fingerprint recorded in
    provenance/search_contract; the greedy-match algorithm and CJK ranges are clean-room
    reimplementable, but a shipped vocab.txt artifact bloats the single file and is
    training-produced -- prefer the model-free CJK basic tokenizer as the must-have and treat
    WordPiece/SPLADE vocab as optional.
  - Honor the additive-only v1 constraint precisely: PQ/binary go in encoding ids 4-255 (4 is the
    next free), sparse/topic/splade go in section ids 0x09+; new manifest/search_contract fields
    are fine, but do NOT change the meaning of any existing field (mrl_dim/full_dim must be
    additive optional, not a redefinition of dim) or you force a true v2 NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump.
  - Keep every new rust module single-responsibility and <=300 lines (333 hard cap): PQ codec,
    popcount-Hamming kernel, SparseIndex, external_merge, and CJK tokenizer each need their own
    file; do not fold PQ scoring into the existing simd/mod.rs (already ~144 lines) past the cap.
  - ColBERT late-interaction (BGE-M3 multi-vector MaxSim) is O(N_q*N_d*dim) and storage-heavy
    (per-token vectors) -- use it only as an optional rerank-only signal if ever, NEVER as a
    candidate generator or a default, to keep the runtime light; the practical hybrid is dense +
    learned-sparse.
  - Never weaken the mandatory exact-cosine rerank: PQ, binary, sparse, and distilled-student
    vectors are ALL lossy and must remain candidate generators only. The returned score must
    always be real cosine recomputed against the highest-fidelity stored vectors, or nest loses
    its 'score is real cosine' guarantee.
  - logit_scale temperature scaling (CLIP) and weighted-sum fusion (BGE) introduce calibration
    scalars that, if folded into the stored/returned score, would make scores model-dependent and
    break cross-encoding score stability -- keep them as an OPTIONAL self-describing scoring mode
    in search_contract, never the default, so the default returned score stays plain cosine.
  - Image/CLIP support must not introduce a per-modality SIMD branch or a separate image codec:
    only L2-normalized 512-d vectors in the shared embeddings section qualify (they cosine-search
    identically to text). Anything requiring a distinct distance or layout would fork the hot path
    and violate the minimal-format goal.


##################################################################################################
# tokenizers  -  tokenization (the front of every embed + a compression primitive)
##################################################################################################

summary:     Across HF tokenizers, SentencePiece, tiktoken, and OpenNMT Tokenizer the field has converged on
             one shape that is almost custom-built for nest's north star: a tokenizer is a
             self-contained, frozen, deterministic, reversible compression codec. The shared
             primitives are (a) a byte-level alphabet (GPT-2/tiktoken bijective
             256-byte<->printable-Unicode map, or SentencePiece byte_fallback emitting <0xNN>)
             that makes ANY input -- CJK, Thai, emoji, JSON, YAML, PDF-text, binary -- losslessly
             tokenizable with zero UNK; (b) a learned variable-length dictionary code, either
             grow-by-merge BPE (HashMap<(u32,u32)->rank> integer-pair merges, greedy lowest-rank)
             or shrink-by-prune Unigram-LM (Viterbi best-path over a lattice scored by log-probs,
             O(n) memory); (c) reversible surface markup (U+2581 spacer / U+FFED joiner / case
             channel) so detokenization is a pure string op and the round-trip is byte-exact; (d)
             compact mmap-resident lookup structures (SentencePiece Darts double-array trie holds
             80k pieces in ~2MB vs ~83MB of pointer tries, just integer base[]/check[]/value[]
             arrays read straight off disk); (e) full (start,end) offset alignment back into the
             ORIGINAL string surviving destructive NFKC/lowercase/strip normalization; and (f) one
             version-tagged self-describing artifact (tokenizer.json / ModelProto) carrying the
             entire pipeline. The crucial reframing for nest: token-id streams ARE compression --
             they replace multi-byte UTF-8 runs with dense small integers BEFORE zstd, and because
             the dictionary is trained ON the corpus it captures domain morphemes (PT-BR news,
             JSON keys, code) that generic zstd cannot model. Stacking range/zstd on the id stream
             compounds the win. This is the single most mission-relevant external category for
             'compress more than everyone' on text-bearing sections, and it simultaneously closes
             the documented CJK/Thai bm25 gap with the same shipped model. Hard caveat repeated by
             every extract: NEVER apply to the embeddings section -- the hot-path SIMD cosine
             kernel scores raw f32/f16/int8 straight off mmap and tokenization would destroy that.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The state of the art is a unified pipeline normalizer -> pre-tokenizer -> model -> post-processor
-> decoder, where the 'model' is a learned variable-length dictionary over a CLOSED byte-level
alphabet, serialized as a single frozen self-describing artifact and decoded by a tiny
integer-indexed structure. Two training philosophies, one runtime shape. BPE (tiktoken, HF,
OpenNMT) grows a vocab by greedily merging the lowest-rank adjacent byte-pair; merges are stored
as integer (u32,u32)->rank HashMaps (no string compares), decode is HashMap<rank,Vec<u8>> concat,
fully deterministic and seedless. Unigram-LM (SentencePiece, HF) starts from a large
suffix-array-seeded vocab and prunes by EM (shrinking_factor 0.75) until target size; segmentation
is Viterbi over a lattice storing only best-path score+backpointer per char position (O(n) memory,
recognized-then-discarded lattice nodes), giving provably minimum-cost segmentation. Losslessness
on arbitrary bytes is universal via byte-level bijection (tiktoken/GPT-2) or byte_fallback to 256
raw-byte pieces (SentencePiece type=6) -- zero out-of-vocab loss, the property nest's
lossless/reversible/content_hash-stable guarantees demand. Reversibility is achieved with in-band
markup (U+2581 whitespace meta-symbol, U+FFED joiner, case channel) so the original reconstructs
byte-exactly. The runtime lookup is the Darts double-array trie (base/check/value/score parallel
arrays, transition next=base[node]+byte validated by check[next]==node, plus
first_byte_max_len[256] early termination) -- 80k pieces in ~2MB, pointer-light integer indexing
ideal for repr(C)+LE+mmap+never-panic, and it does NOT contend with the AVX2/NEON dot-product path
(trie traversal is branch-bound, not a SIMD kernel). Perf is excellent and build-time only:
tiktoken 3-6x faster than HF on 1GB GPT-2 text, HF ~1GB/CPU-min (<20s/GB), SentencePiece ~50k
sentences/sec with ~6MB footprint, all pure scalar Rust/C++ with no SIMD in the tokenizer.
Offset-alignment tracking (every token carries (start,end) byte offsets into the original even
through destructive normalization) is the proven design for span-exact citations. Determinism is
native: BPE merge-rank order and Unigram seeded-EM + stable tie-break (leftmost) make builds
reproducible, which maps cleanly onto nest's bm25 alphabetical-term-order and seeded-hnsw
discipline.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Byte-level closed alphabet (tiktoken/GPT-2 bijective 256-byte<->Unicode map; SentencePiece
   byte_fallback type=6 pieces): makes tokenization lossless and reversible on ANY input with zero
   UNK -- the non-negotiable property that lets a learned dictionary code satisfy nest's
   lossless/content_hash-stable/language-agnostic guarantees, and the direct fix for the CJK/Thai
   bm25 gap.
2. Token-id stream as dictionary compression front-end: a learned BPE/Unigram vocab turns
   multi-byte UTF-8 runs into dense small integers BEFORE entropy coding, and because it is
   trained ON the corpus it models domain morphemes (PT-BR news, JSON keys, code) that generic
   zstd cannot. The single most mission-relevant idea for compress-more-than-everyone on
   text-bearing sections.
3. SentencePiece Darts double-array trie: 80k pieces in ~2MB (vs ~83MB pointer tries) as plain
   integer base[]/check[]/value[]/score[] arrays read straight off mmap -- a perfect
   repr(C)+LE+never-panic decoder that keeps the tiny/portable mandate and never touches the SIMD
   cosine path.
4. Unigram-LM Viterbi best-path segmentation: O(n)-memory minimum-cost (most-probable)
   segmentation over a lattice, provably better symbol assignment than greedy BPE for compression,
   with seeded-EM determinism so byte-identical builds hold.
5. Reversible in-band markup (U+2581 spacer / U+FFED joiner / case channel from
   OpenNMT+SentencePiece): detokenization is a pure string replace, so token-id codec round-trips
   byte-exact and content_hash over decoded canonical text stays stable -- no separate span
   storage needed for reconstruction.
6. Integer (u32,u32)->rank merge tables (tiktoken/HF): pair-rank HashMap lookups, no string
   compares, seedless-deterministic greedy lowest-rank merge -- a ~150-200 loc pure-safe Rust
   merge loop that fits the 300-line file rule and the no-panic constraint.
7. Offset/alignment tracking through destructive normalization (HF (start,end) byte offsets
   surviving NFKC/lowercase/strip): the proven design to harden chunks_original_spans so
   nest://content_hash/chunk_id stays byte-exact after lossy normalization.
8. Single self-describing version-tagged artifact (tokenizer.json / proto2 ModelProto carrying
   normalizer+model+decoder): mirror inline in nest's manifest so the file stays self-describing,
   offline, and reconstructs the exact pipeline with no external dependency.
9. Word-result LRU + per-thread cache (HF cache.rs, +47-62% on parallel workloads; OpenNMT lacks
   it and pays per-request): memoize per pre-token at build time so the BPE/Viterbi hot loop is
   amortized over repetitive corpora.
10. precompiled_charsmap (SentencePiece NFKC compiled to a double-array trie + rewrite string,
   layout [u32 trie_len][trie][normalized]): normalization-as-data-table not code, a frozen
   deterministic LE blob that fits nest's data-over-code, reproducible-build philosophy.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Replace the naive is_alphanumeric word-splitter in bm25/tokenize.rs with byte-level subword
  segmentation (greedy BPE merge or Unigram Viterbi) driven by a frozen vocab, preserving
  lowercase + alphabetical term order for determinism. Closes the documented CJK/Thai degradation
  with zero format-breaking change.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/tokenize.rs (currently 28 loc, the char-class splitter); vocab loaded
            once by bm25::Bm25Index at index build and search time. No section change required if
            vocab ships inline in the manifest; or read it from the new tokenizer section in rec
            2.
    impact: high
    effort: med
    from:   https://github.com/openai/tiktoken, https://github.com/google/sentencepiece,
            https://github.com/huggingface/tokenizers

* Add a frozen tokenizer model as an optional additive v1 section (reserve next id 0x09) holding
  either a BPE merge table (integer (u32,u32)->rank arrays + reverse) or a Unigram
  vocab+log-scores, stored as a Darts double-array trie (repr(C), little-endian
  base[]/check[]/value[]/score[]) for mmap-resident O(n) decode. Section is per-section SHA-256
  checksummed and zstd'd via the existing non-embedding codec path. The same model feeds both rec
  1 (bm25) and rec 3 (text codec) -- one artifact, two uses.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/layout/mod.rs (add pub const SECTION_TOKENIZER_MODEL: u32 = 0x09 alongside
            0x07/0x08); crates/nest-format/src/sections/ new tokenizer.rs (<300 loc encode/decode
            mirroring bm25 codec.rs ByteCursor style); manifest gains a tokenizer descriptor so
            the file stays self-describing/offline.
    impact: high
    effort: med
    from:   https://github.com/google/sentencepiece, https://github.com/openai/tiktoken,
            https://github.com/OpenNMT/Tokenizer

* Add a token-id text codec as a new RESERVED wire encoding id (next free is 4; current ids are
  raw=0 zstd=1 f16=2 int8=3) for the chunks_canonical section ONLY: store text as
  varint/range-coded subword id streams instead of raw UTF-8, then optionally zstd on top.
  decode_payload reconstructs canonical UTF-8 via reversible markup so content_hash (which hashes
  DECODED bytes) is unchanged and citations stay byte-exact. Python (nest-python) trains the vocab
  offline with seeded EM / deterministic merges so builds remain byte-identical.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/mod.rs decode_payload match arm (add SECTION_ENCODING_TOKENIZED =
            4); crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ new tokenized_codec.rs (<300 loc, the read-only
            trie + Viterbi/merge decode); guard so encoding 4 is rejected for the embeddings
            section. Encode side trained in nest-python, emitted by writer/build.rs.
    impact: high
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/google/sentencepiece, https://github.com/openai/tiktoken,
            https://github.com/OpenNMT/Tokenizer, https://github.com/huggingface/tokenizers

* Adopt HF-style (start,end) byte-offset tracking THROUGH destructive normalization to harden
  chunks_original_spans, so canonical->original mapping survives NFKC/lowercase/strip and
