  nest://content_hash/chunk_id reopens the exact original byte span even when the canonical text
  was normalized.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/spans.rs (OriginalSpan encode/decode) plus the
            chunking/normalization step in nest-python that produces chunks_canonical +
            chunks_original_spans; the alignment is computed at build time, no runtime cost.
    impact: med
    effort: med
    from:   https://github.com/huggingface/tokenizers, https://github.com/OpenNMT/Tokenizer

* Add a tiny new 'micro' / 'archive' preset that pairs the token-id text codec (encoding 4) with
  zstd on the id stream and int8/hnsw embeddings, targeting an even smaller size ratio than
  today's tiny (0.283) on repetitive PT-BR news corpora, while keeping exact-cosine-rerank ground
  truth intact.
    where:  preset definitions in writer/build.rs + nest-python build surface; expose via nest-cli (no new
            subcommand needed -- it is a preset flag, keeping the thin 8-subcommand clap surface).
    impact: med
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/google/sentencepiece, https://github.com/openai/tiktoken

* Mirror the single self-describing artifact idea: keep the entire tokenizer config (normalizer
  rules, model params, version tag) inline in the manifest JSON rather than as opaque external
  state, so a .nest file remains fully self-describing and reconstructs the exact decode pipeline
  offline with no companion file.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/manifest/mod.rs (Manifest struct + to_canonical_json) and
            manifest/validate.rs (allow/validate the tokenizer descriptor, analogous to
            ALLOWED_DTYPES); the descriptor becomes part of content_hash inputs so it is
            deterministic.
    impact: med
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/huggingface/tokenizers, https://github.com/google/sentencepiece

* Add a build-time word-keyed LRU / per-pre-token cache around the merge/Viterbi loop so encoding
  repetitive corpora (news with repeated phrases, JSON with repeated keys) is amortized; OpenNMT's
  lack of this is a documented per-request cost, HF's presence of it is +47-62%.
    where:  inside the encode path of the new tokenized_codec.rs (build-time only, not on the mmap read/serve
            hot path); keep it allocation-light with a bounded LRU.
    impact: low
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/huggingface/tokenizers, https://github.com/OpenNMT/Tokenizer

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Token-id streams over a corpus-trained vocab replace multi-byte UTF-8 runs with dense small
    integers BEFORE zstd; on repetitive/structured corpora (PT-BR news, JSON keys, YAML, code)
    this lowers entropy-coder input vs raw bytes and the gain COMPOUNDS when zstd/range-codes the
    id stream -- the core lever for compress-more-than-everyone on text sections, and it composes
    with the existing zstd codec rather than replacing it.
  - Unigram-LM Viterbi gives provably minimum-cost (most-probable) segmentation, so the id
    assignment itself is information-theoretically tighter than greedy BPE -- fewer/denser symbols
    for the same lossless reconstruction.
  - Training the vocab ON the file's own corpus captures domain-specific morphemes (Portuguese
    inflections, dataset-specific tokens, repeated JSON schema keys) that a generic byte
    compressor like zstd cannot model, beating zstd-on-raw-bytes on exactly the
    structured/repetitive data nest targets.
  - The vocab cap (e.g. shrink-by-prune to a small target size) bounds dictionary size, so the
    per-file tokenizer section stays small (SentencePiece 80k pieces ~2MB trie) and the net size
    ratio improves for the tiny/micro presets rather than regressing.
  - Range/rANS coding the id stream (a natural next layer on top of dense ids) is the documented
    endgame BPE-ids -> entropy-coder; nest can stage this as encoding id 4 = tokenized, then later
    5 = tokenized+range, all additive within v1.
  - Same shipped model serves double duty: the bytes spent on the tokenizer section are amortized
    across BOTH the text-compression codec AND the bm25 subword terms, so the marginal cost of
    closing the CJK/Thai gap is near zero once the codec ships.
  - Byte-level closed alphabet guarantees losslessness on json/yaml/pdf-text/binary, extending the
    compress-everything mandate (text, json, yaml, parquet, pdf, audio/video metadata) to a single
    reversible codec path without per-format special-casing.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - NEVER tokenize the embeddings section. Every extract flags this: the hot path is SIMD cosine
    scoring raw f32/f16/int8 straight off mmap; token-id encoding would destroy random-access
    vector layout and the AVX2/NEON dot-product kernel. Enforce a hard guard that encoding id 4
    (tokenized) is rejected for the embeddings section in decode_payload / writer.
  - Determinism is the make-or-break constraint. EM training (Unigram) is iterative and tie-breaks
    matter; you MUST seed EM and use a stable leftmost tie-break (BPE is naturally
    seedless/deterministic, prefer it if EM determinism is hard to pin). Without bit-exact
    reproducibility the same chunks + same model fingerprint will NOT produce a byte-identical
    file, breaking nest's core guarantee.
  - content_hash hashes DECODED canonical bytes -- the token-id codec MUST round-trip byte-exact
    (reversible markup, byte_fallback for any OOV) or citations and the STABLE-across-encodings
    content_hash guarantee break. Add a roundtrip assertion in the codec tests mirroring
    zstd_roundtrip_preserves_bytes.
  - Reject the C++/ICU dependency stacks. SentencePiece (proto2) and OpenNMT (ICU) bring heavy
    deps that violate the small-core, sovereign, offline, no-external-runtime mandate. Reimplement
    the merge loop / Viterbi / double-array trie in pure safe Rust (no panic, typed thiserror),
    each module <=300 loc (333 hard cap), single-responsibility.
  - Format additivity: only use RESERVED encoding ids (next free is 4; raw/zstd/f16/int8 occupy
    0-3) and section ids 0x09+ (0x07/0x08 taken). Do NOT bump NEST_FORMAT_VERSION (still 1) unless
    an existing field changes meaning -- a tokenizer section/encoding is purely additive and stays
    v1.
  - Per-file vocab adds bytes; on small or non-repetitive corpora the tokenizer section can cost
    more than it saves. Gate the codec behind a preset/heuristic (only emit when corpus size and
    repetitiveness clear a threshold) so default builds never regress in size, and always keep
    raw/zstd as the fallback encoding.
  - Keep the tokenizer config inline in the manifest, not as a companion file, or the
    single-file/self-describing/offline guarantee breaks. The tokenizer descriptor must also be
    folded into content_hash inputs so it is deterministic and tamper-evident.
  - bm25 requires alphabetical term order for determinism; subword segmentation changes the term
    set, so verify the sort still yields a stable, reproducible posting layout (the codec.rs
    already sorts terms by string -- subword terms must remain valid UTF-8 strings to keep that
    path).
  - Tokenizer perf is build-time, not serve-time, so do NOT add SIMD to the tokenizer (trie
    traversal is branch-bound, no dot-product) -- it would waste effort and the cited ARM port
    only used CRC32 for model-load hashing, not Viterbi. Keep the serve hot path purely mmap +
    SIMD cosine.
  - The ~150-200 loc merge loop and the Viterbi+trie decoder must split across files to respect
    the <=300 line rule (e.g. trie.rs, decode.rs, codec.rs) rather than one monolith; mirror the
    existing bm25/{index,codec,tokenize,fusion} single-responsibility split.


##################################################################################################
# model-runtime  -  inference runtimes and tensor libraries for on-device embedding
##################################################################################################

summary:     Across onnxruntime/MLAS, onnx, and ggml the field has converged on one decisive technique for
             shrinking on-device embeddings: block-wise (group-wise) sub-byte weight quantization
             with a small per-block scale, packed in a self-contained repr(C) layout and
             dequantized fused-inline during the SIMD dot product so dequantized values never
             materialize in RAM. ggml's K-quants (Q4_K: 256-value super-blocks, fp16 d/dmin
             masters + 6-bit double-quantized sub-block scales + 4-bit quants = 4.5 bpw) and
             onnx's MX microscaling (32-elem blocks, shared FLOAT8E8M0 power-of-two exponent +
             int4 mantissa) are the two reference designs; MLAS SQNBitGemm is the reference fused
             dequant+dot kernel. This category is nest's single biggest unexploited lever, because
             embeddings are the one section banned from zstd (they are scored directly from mmap
             on the hot path), so the only way to shrink them is better quantization. nest is
             unusually well-positioned: its int8.rs payload ALREADY carries a scale_kind field
             (currently only 0 = per-vector), encoding ids 4-255 are reserved inside frozen v1,
             and search.rs already runs a mandatory exact-cosine rerank that absorbs quantization
             error so an int4 candidate stage keeps ground-truth scores. The one real blocker is
             an internal detail: mmap_file.rs materializes EVERY dtype to a full n*dim*4 f32
             buffer whenever an HNSW index is present (materialize.rs), so today's int8 already
             loses its in-place-scoring advantage for tiny/hybrid presets, and a 4-bit codec
             inherits and worsens that problem unless the ANN distance path is taught to read
             packed blocks directly.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The de-facto standard for compact on-device embeddings is fixed-size block (group) quantization
with a small per-block scale, three design points along a bpw/quality curve: (1) symmetric int8
with one scale per 32-element block (ggml block_q8_0, ~8.5 bpw) as the safe, recall-preserving
baseline; (2) int4 with a per-block scale, either an fp16 scale (MLAS MatMulNBits, ~4.25 bpw at
block 64) or a shared power-of-two E8M0 exponent (onnx MX, ~4.25 bpw at block 32), as the sweet
spot, where block scaling recovers the recall that flat per-vector int4 loses; (3) hierarchical
double-quantization (ggml Q4_K, 4.5 bpw) which adds a second quantization level (6-bit sub-block
scales relative to fp16 super-block masters) to track both global and local dynamic range across
256 values; and (4) codebook/i-quant schemes (IQ2_XXS, ~2 bpw) for the aggressive frontier. The
universal implementation pattern is: self-contained repr(C) little-endian block records,
SIMD-aligned, two int4 nibbles packed per byte, dequantized fused-inline inside the dot-product
SIMD pass (MLAS SQNBitGemm) across a runtime CPU-feature dispatch ladder (scalar -> NEON/dotprod
-> AVX2+FMA+F16C -> AVX512-VNNI, with VNNI's vpdpbusd doing 4 int8 MACs/lane). Block size trades
footprint vs accuracy: smaller block = more scales = bigger but more accurate, with 32-128 the
consensus sweet spot and fp16 scales keeping overhead near 0.25 bits/dim at block 64. A hard
structural constraint from ggml: K-quants require the quantized dimension be a multiple of 256
(768, 1024 fine; 384 not), so a block-32 fallback (q8_0/q4 block-32) is mandatory for off-size
models. Accuracy-recovery calibration (GPTQ/HQQ/imatrix) is the one part nest should NOT copy
wholesale: it depends on a calibration corpus and breaks determinism; plain RTN max-abs-per-block
is reproducible and, behind nest's mandatory exact-cosine rerank, sufficient.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. ggml Q4_K hierarchical double-quantization: 256-value super-blocks each carry two fp16 masters
   (d, dmin) and eight 32-value sub-blocks whose own scale/min are 6-bit ints relative to the
   masters, reaching 4.5 bpw vs nest's current 8 bpw int8 while a self-contained repr(C) record
   stays dequantizable in isolation off mmap. The key insight is spending metadata bits
   hierarchically so per-sub-block precision is nearly free amortized across 256 values.
2. MLAS SQNBitGemm fused dequant+dot: nibble-unpack + per-block-scale + multiply-accumulate happen
   in one SIMD pass so dequantized weights never hit memory, keeping the matmul memory-bandwidth
   bound. This is exactly the kernel shape nest's simd/mod.rs dispatcher needs for a 4-bit path,
   and it is what makes block-quant cheaper than it looks on the hot path.
3. onnx MX microscaling (E8M0 shared block exponent): a 32-element block shares one 8-bit
   power-of-two exponent, so dequant is a single exponent add/shift per block (no fp16 multiply),
   the cheapest possible block-scale to fuse into a NEON/AVX2 dot kernel before f32 accumulation.
   Near-int8 quality at int4 size.
4. Block-wise scaling as the fix for int8's per-vector-outlier loss: MLAS/ggml both show one scale
   per K-block (16/32/64) instead of per whole vector kills the single-outlier problem that
   crushes per-vector resolution. This retrofits onto nest's EXISTING int8 path as scale_kind=1 to
   recover accuracy at the same or near-same size, independent of any int4 work.
5. ggml block_q8_0 (one fp16 scale per 32 dims): the lowest-risk concrete artifact, a drop-in
   higher-quality, smaller-scale-overhead replacement for nest's per-vector-f32-scale int8 (4
   bytes/vector of scale -> 2 bytes/32 dims), shippable as scale_kind=1 with minimal new kernel
   surface.
6. ggml i-quants (IQ2_XXS, ~2.06 bpw): vector codewords indexed into a shared lookup grid so
   weights cost log2(grid) bits, not raw bits, the longer-term path to push well below 4 bpw, at
   the cost of a shipped codebook and a gather in the kernel.
7. onnx size_threshold gate: only spill tensors above a threshold to block-quant; small sections
   keep flat layout. Cheap idea to steal into writer/encoding_choice.rs so tiny corpora don't pay
   block-scale overhead.
8. Deterministic round-to-nearest-even max-abs-per-block quantization: a pure function of input
   with no seed, so block-quant preserves nest's byte-identical reproducibility guarantee for free
   (unlike GPTQ/imatrix calibration which depends on a calibration set).
9. GGUF/ONNX page-and-SIMD-aligned block layout: every block/tensor starts on a 32-byte (GGUF) /
   4KB (ONNX) boundary for zero-copy mmap. nest's 64-byte SECTION_ALIGNMENT already satisfies
   this; the lesson is to keep super-blocks an integer multiple of the SIMD load width and the
   section aligned so the first block does too.
10. ml_dtypes / hand-rolled nibble packing: int4/fp8 have no native numpy or Rust dtype, so all
   three projects hand-roll two's-complement nibble pack/unpack, confirming nest must own this in
   Rust and split pack/unpack out of the kernel file to stay under the 300-line cap.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add block-wise int8 as scale_kind=1 in the EXISTING int8 codec (one fp16 scale per 32-dim block,
  ggml block_q8_0 shape), reusing the float16 codec for the scale. Lowest-risk first step:
  recovers recall int8 loses to per-vector outliers and shrinks scale overhead from 4 bytes/vector
  to 2 bytes/32 dims, with NO new encoding id and NO format-version bump.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/int8.rs: branch on the already-present scale_kind field (add
            INT8_SCALE_KIND_BLOCK32=1 next to INT8_SCALE_KIND_PER_VECTOR=0); layout [u32 ver][u32
            scale_kind=1][f16 scales: n*(dim/32)][i8 bodies]. Int8EmbeddingsView gains
            block_scale(i, block); parse() currently rejects kind!=0 so old readers stay safe.
            Runtime: simd/mod.rs score_int8_section and search.rs score_all/score_subset Int8 arm
            apply per-block scale.
    impact: high
    effort: low
    from:   ggml block_q8_0, MLAS block-wise weight quantization, onnx MX block scaling

* Add an int4 block-scaled embeddings codec as RESERVED encoding id SECTION_ENCODING_INT4=4 + new
  dtype string 'int4_b64', modeled on int8.rs: split each dim-length row into 64-elem blocks, one
  f16 scale per block (reuse float16 codec), bit-pack two int4 codes per byte in block-contiguous
  [n, dim/64, 32] layout. ~4.25 bpw vs int8's 8, roughly halves the dominant section. Additive
  within frozen v1; the exact-cosine rerank keeps returned scores real cosine even though int4
  drives candidates.
    where:  New file crates/nest-format/src/encoding/int4.rs (split pack/unpack from kernel to stay under 300
            lines); wire into encoding/mod.rs (export + decode_payload passthrough since int4
            bytes ARE canonical) and expected_embeddings_size; add SECTION_ENCODING_INT4=4 to
            layout/mod.rs; add EmbeddingDType::Int4 in writer/encoding_choice.rs and DType::Int4
            in runtime mmap_file.rs.
    impact: high
    effort: high
    from:   MLAS MatMulNBits / SQNBitGemm, onnx int4 nibble packing, ggml Q4_0 4-bit nibbles

* Add a fused dequant+dot SIMD kernel (dot_f32_i4) beside avx2/neon/scalar that streams nibbles,
  applies the per-block f16 scale, accumulates cosine in f32 in one pass, never expanding the row
  to f32 in RAM (the SQNBitGemm pattern). Add score_int4_section and a DType::Int4 arm to
  score_all/score_subset.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/{avx2,neon,scalar}.rs (new dot_f32_i4 fn each, mirroring dot_f32_i8);
            simd/mod.rs new dispatch fn + score_int4_section; crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs
            add the Int4 match arms. Keep nibble unpack helpers in a small simd/pack.rs to respect
            the 300-line cap.
    impact: high
    effort: high
    from:   MLAS SQNBitGemm fused kernel, ggml AVX2/NEON dequant-and-dot, AVX512-VNNI vpdpbusd

* Fix the materialize-to-f32 leak so block-quant actually pays off for tiny/hybrid. Today
  mmap_file.rs::open materializes the WHOLE embeddings section to an n*dim*4 f32 buffer
  (materialize.rs) whenever an HNSW section is present, to feed the ANN graph. A 4-bit codec would
  be expanded to f32 in RAM, defeating compression on exactly the preset (tiny = int8+hnsw) that
  needs it most. Teach the ANN distance path to score against packed blocks via the new fused
  kernel (or at minimum keep f16, not f32, as the materialized form).
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/materialize.rs and crates/nest-runtime/src/ann/{search.rs,mod.rs}
            (HnswIndex::attach_vectors / distance fn); have the graph hold a borrowed packed view
            + scale table and call the simd fused dot instead of holding a dense f32 Vec. Also
            shrinks resident RAM for the existing int8 tiny preset.
    impact: high
    effort: high
    from:   ggml block-local self-contained random access off mmap, MLAS memory-bandwidth-bound fused matmul

* Ship a 'nano' preset (int4 block-64 embeddings + zstd text + hnsw) targeting a size ratio well
  below tiny's 0.283, since embeddings are the dominant incompressible bytes. Gate on dim being
  block-compatible (multiple of 64; a future Q4_K-256 variant needs multiple of 256) and fall back
  to block-32 int8 otherwise, matching ggml's K-quant dimension constraint.
    where:  Preset selection lives above the format crate (nest-python build path / CLI). Wire
            EmbeddingDType::Int4 through writer/encoding_choice.rs and the manifest dtype string;
            document the preset and recall@10 where tiny (0.283) is documented. Add a writer-side
            guard that rejects/falls-back when dim % block != 0.
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   nest tiny preset (0.283), ggml K-quant dim-multiple-of-256 constraint, ggml block-32 fallback

* Steal onnx's size_threshold gate: only apply block-quant overhead to embeddings sections above a
  byte threshold; tiny corpora keep flat float16/int8 so a handful of vectors don't pay per-block
  scale metadata. Keep per-section SHA-256 discipline (mirrors ONNX per-tensor checksum)
  untouched.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/writer/encoding_choice.rs (or writer/payload.rs where embedding dtype is
            chosen): add a min-bytes gate before selecting Int4/block-int8.
    impact: low
    effort: low
    from:   onnx external-data size_threshold gate

* Defer i-quant / codebook (IQ2_XXS ~2 bpw) and any GPTQ/imatrix calibration. They are the
  frontier but (a) imatrix needs a calibration corpus and breaks byte-identical determinism, and
  (b) i-quants ship a lookup grid and add a gather to the hot kernel. Record as a v2-era research
  line, NOT a near-term graft; plain RTN max-abs-per-block int4 already lands the biggest win
  deterministically.
    where:  Roadmap note only (kdb/research synthesis). If ever pursued, the codebook would be a new reserved
            encoding id with the grid embedded in the embeddings payload prefix; determinism would
            require shipping the exact grid, not deriving it from data.
    impact: medium
    effort: high
    from:   ggml i-quants IQ2_XXS, GPTQ/HQQ/imatrix calibration

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Block-scaled int4 embeddings at block 64 = ~4.25 bpw (4 bits + ~0.25 bits/dim for the f16
    scale) vs the current int8's 8 bpw, roughly HALVES nest's largest incompressible section and
    is the single biggest available lever on 'compress more than everyone', since embeddings are
    banned from zstd. On a 768-dim corpus that is ~3072 -> ~1632 bytes/vector for the embeddings
    body+scales.
  - A 'nano' preset (int4 block-64 + zstd text + hnsw) should push the total file size ratio
    meaningfully below tiny's 0.283, because the embeddings section dominates total bytes and is
    exactly what int4 targets; the mandatory exact-cosine rerank is expected to hold recall@10
    near tiny's 0.992.
  - Block-scaled int8 (scale_kind=1, block 32) shrinks scale overhead from 4 bytes per vector to 2
    bytes per 32 dims while ALSO recovering recall lost to per-vector outliers, a free
    accuracy+size win on the existing int8/tiny path with no new encoding id and no format bump.
  - ggml Q4_K (256-dim super-blocks, fp16 d/dmin + 6-bit sub-block scales + 4-bit quants = 4.5
    bpw) is the higher-quality variant for 768/1024-dim models (3x256, 4x256), trading ~0.25 bpw
    over flat int4 for noticeably better local-dynamic-range tracking, a strong second codec once
    the int4 path exists.
  - Longer term, i-quant codebooks (IQ2_XXS ~2.06 bpw) could quarter embeddings vs int8, but only
    behind a shipped grid and gather kernel; flagged as frontier, not near-term.
  - Reusing the existing float16 codec for the per-block scales means scale storage costs no new
    code and stays deterministic, so the compression win comes with near-zero new format surface.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - DETERMINISM: do NOT copy GPTQ/HQQ/imatrix calibration, they depend on a calibration corpus and
    would break nest's byte-identical 'same chunks + same model fingerprint => identical file'
    guarantee. Stick to pure round-to-nearest-even max-abs-per-block (a deterministic function of
    input, no seed), which preserves reproducibility for free.
  - IN-PLACE SCORING LEAK (already present): mmap_file.rs::open + materialize.rs expand EVERY
    dtype, including int8, to a full n*dim*4 f32 buffer whenever an HNSW section exists, to feed
    the ANN graph. Shipping int4 without fixing this means the codec halves DISK size but
    tiny/hybrid presets still hold a dense f32 copy in RAM, contradicting the 'scored directly
    from mmap, no decompress-to-heap' invariant. The ANN distance path must score packed blocks
    (or at least keep f16) before int4 delivers its promise.
  - DIMENSION CONSTRAINTS: ggml K-quants require dim be a multiple of 256 and even simple int4
    block-64 needs dim % 64 == 0. Demo/PT-BR models may use 384 or other off-sizes; the writer
    MUST guard and fall back to block-32 int8 (or flat f16) rather than silently producing a
    malformed/padded section.
  - 300/333-LINE CAP + SINGLE RESPONSIBILITY: a fused dequant kernel plus nibble pack/unpack plus
    a new codec will blow the line cap in one file. Split pack/unpack (encoding/int4.rs + a tiny
    simd/pack.rs) from the dot kernels (avx2/neon/scalar.rs), exactly as ggml separates
    quantize/dequantize/vec_dot.
  - NEVER PANIC: all new parse paths (nibble unpack, block-count math, scale-table bounds,
    dim%block checks) must return typed thiserror NestError/RuntimeError, never unwrap/panic,
    mirroring Int8EmbeddingsView::parse size checks. Unchecked nibble indexing into an mmap'd
    slice is a panic/UB risk on a malformed file.
  - ADDITIVE-ONLY FROZEN V1: int4 must take a RESERVED encoding id in 4-255 and a NEW dtype
    string; do NOT change the meaning of any existing field. scale_kind=1 on the int8 path is safe
    because the field already exists and old readers reject unknown kinds (parse() guards
    kind!=0). Bumping NEST_FORMAT_VERSION is reserved for true v2 only.
  - OFFLINE/SINGLE-FILE: reject ONNX's external-data/sidecar model outright, nest's whole point is
    one self-contained file; the only thing to steal there is the size_threshold gate idea, not
    the byte-range-into-another-file mechanism. No codec may pull a codebook or scale table from
    outside the .nest container.
  - LITTLE-ENDIAN repr(C): block records (scales then nibbles) must be little-endian and the
    section must stay 64-byte aligned so the first block lands on a SIMD boundary; keep
    super-block size an integer multiple of the SIMD load width or the fused kernel will
    mis-stride on the tail block.
  - RERANK FIDELITY NUANCE: int4's low precision is only acceptable BECAUSE search.rs runs a
    mandatory exact-cosine rerank over candidates, but that rerank reads the SAME int4 vectors, so
    the 'real cosine' is real-cosine-of-the-quantized-vector, not of the original f32. For exact
    ground truth a corpus needs an f32/f16 preset; document that nano trades a small
    absolute-score fidelity for size, and keep exact/compressed as the recall-max options.


##################################################################################################
# rag-orchestration  -  RAG, agent, LLM orchestration, and memory systems
##################################################################################################

summary:     Across 18 RAG/agent/memory repos, almost none ship a compression codec or on-disk vector format
             worth vendoring directly (most delegate storage to Postgres/Qdrant/LanceDB/Weaviate
             and are pure orchestration Python). The genuinely transferable value clusters in
             three layers. (1) Embedding-codec frontier (Weaviate recipes, RAGFlow/Infinity,
             semtools, supermemory): Rotational Quantization (seeded Fast Walsh-Hadamard rotate ->
             int8) makes a 4x int8 codec near-lossless at ~99% recall; LVQ (mean-center then
             per-vector residual quant) lets the HNSW graph traverse on compressed codes so the
             full-f32 RAM snapshot disappears; PQ/BQ chart the path to 8-32x; Matryoshka (MRL)
             prefix truncation turns embedding dimensionality into a model-backed lossy knob that
             stacks multiplicatively with quantization and is what the hot SIMD loop actually
             reads. All four fit nest's existing mandatory exact-cosine rerank, which IS the
             rescoring step every one of these codecs requires. (2) Structural dedup / versioning
             data-model (langchain record-manager, langgraph version-keyed blobs, FalkorDB CSC
             matrices, neo4j lexical graph): content-hash dedup of repeated chunks+embeddings,
             append-only version maps that reference unchanged content-hashed blobs instead of
             recopying, and CSR/CSC integer adjacency overlays that add graph search and let you
             drop chunk-overlap text redundancy. These advance the 'compress whole
             repos/databases' north star structurally. (3) Zero-byte retrieval-quality wins
             (llama_index auto-merge hierarchy, langroid rank_to_periphery + RRF generalization,
             Verba/langroid window expansion, dify/neo4j weighted fusion): coarse context
             reconstructed from existing spans at ~9 bytes/edge with no parent text stored. nest
             is already AHEAD of every repo here on storage (it is the only one with
             f16/int8/zstd/mmap-SIMD and a true content_hash). The single biggest near-term steal
             is Rotational Quantization, a self-contained additive upgrade to
             crates/nest-format/src/encoding/int8.rs that keeps encoding=3 and needs no
             format-version bump.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The category's collective state of the art for a sovereign embedded engine is: (a) embeddings
stored quantized but always paired with an exact rescoring pass over higher-precision vectors for
the final-k candidates -- nest already enforces this as a format invariant (rerank_policy=exact,
score_subset), which is stronger than most repos' ANN-proxy scores. (b) The best quantizers
gaussianize or decorrelate before scalar quantization: Weaviate's Rotational Quantization (3-round
blocked FWHT + sign flips, O(D log D), ~7us at 1536-dim) gives ~99% recall@10 at 4x where naive
max-abs int8 (exactly nest's current int8.rs) degrades on outlier-heavy dims; Infinity's LVQ
mean-centers then per-vector-scales the residual so NN ordering is preserved on the compressed
codes, letting HNSW build/traverse without any full-precision copy. (c) Matryoshka embeddings make
dimensionality a graceful lossy axis (3072->256 prefix + L2-renorm), enabling
coarse(truncated)-to-fine(full) two-pass search that multiplies with quantization. (d) Structural
dedup is treated as a first-class compressor: version-keyed content-addressed blobs (langgraph),
content-hash record managers (langchain), and CSC sparse graph matrices (FalkorDB) store edit
history and relationships by reference, not by copy. (e) Retrieval granularity is decoupled from
storage granularity: only leaf chunks are embedded, coarse context is reconstructed on read from a
tiny parent-child edge table or positional neighbor ids (llama_index AutoMerging, Verba/langroid
windowing, neo4j NEXT_CHUNK), spending ~9 bytes/edge and zero embedding rows. (f) Offline static
embedders (model2vec/POTION: per-token vector table + mean-pool, no matmul/GPU/network,
byte-deterministic) make the embedding step itself sovereign and the model a few-MB int8+zstd
table instead of multi-GB weights. Fusion has converged on RRF k=60 (which nest already has) with
weighted-sum/linear-alpha as deterministic tunable alternatives.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Rotational Quantization (Weaviate): seeded Fast Walsh-Hadamard rotation gaussianizes the vector
   so one 8-bit interval fits all dims -> ~99% recall@10 at 4x, near-drop-in upgrade to nest's
   naive max-abs int8 codec, recall recovered by the already-mandatory exact rerank.
2. LVQ two-stage residual quant (Infinity/RAGFlow): subtract global corpus mean, per-vector scale
   the residual; HNSW builds and traverses on the int8 codes alone, eliminating nest's wasteful
   full-f32 `vectors: Vec<f32>` RAM snapshot in ann/mod.rs while preserving NN order.
3. Matryoshka (MRL) prefix truncation (supermemory): importance-ordered dims mean a 256-d prefix
   is just the first K contiguous floats already on disk; coarse(prefix)-to-fine(full) two-pass
   search stacks multiplicatively with int8 (~48x fewer bytes touched in pass 1) and maps exactly
   onto nest's ANN-then-exact-rerank contract.
4. Version-keyed content-addressed blob dedup (langgraph): store edit history by referencing
   unchanged content-hashed chunk/embedding blobs via a small version map instead of recopying ->
   structural cross-snapshot dedup, the core primitive for real-time edit + whole-repo ingestion
   without breaking the frozen single-file guarantee.
5. Content-hash record manager (langchain): per-chunk content_hash + source_id manifest enables
   skip-if-present dedup and incremental re-embed of only changed chunks; nest already computes
   content_hash so this is near-zero marginal cost and collapses repeated boilerplate (and its
   fp32 embeddings) to one physical copy referenced by many spans.
6. PQ / Binary Quantization (Weaviate recipes, semtools IVF-PQ): 8-16x (PQ, codebook + 1
   byte/segment) and 32x (BQ, 1 bit/dim, Hamming MaxSim via popcnt/vcnt already in nest's SIMD
   dispatcher) compressors as new reserved encoding ids, both ranking-preserving under nest's
   exact rerank.
7. CSC/CSR typed sparse adjacency + lexical graph (FalkorDB, neo4j-graphrag): O(edges) integer
   overlay (src/dst u32 + edge_type u8, sorted, zstd-friendly) adds graph search and lets nest
   drop chunk-overlap text by reconstructing neighbor context from NEXT_CHUNK edges.
8. Auto-merge hierarchy (llama_index): parent-child edge table where parent text is reconstructed
   from children's existing chunks_original_spans -> multi-resolution retrieval at ~9 bytes/edge
   and zero extra text/embedding bytes.
9. model2vec/POTION static embedder (semtools): vocab x 256 token-vector table + mean-pool gives a
   byte-deterministic, GPU-free, network-free embedding backend; the table ships as int8+zstd,
   making nest's embedding step itself sovereign and reproducible.
10. rank_to_periphery + window expansion (langroid, Verba): pure-integer deterministic post-rerank
   reorder (anti lost-in-middle) and positional neighbor-chunk expansion from existing
   chunk_ids/spans -- zero-storage retrieval-quality wins on the returned chunk list.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Upgrade the int8 codec to Rotational Quantization: add a seeded Fast Walsh-Hadamard rotation
  helper, apply it at build time before quantization, store the rotation seed (and per-section
  min/max interval) in the int8 payload header via a new scale_kind, rotate the query with the
  same seed at search time, then let the existing exact rerank recover recall.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/int8.rs (new scale_kind=1 + rotation seed in the 8-byte prefix;
            bump INT8_PAYLOAD_VERSION, NOT NEST_FORMAT_VERSION, stays encoding=3); a new ~80-line
            fwht helper in nest-format/src/encoding/; query-side rotation in
            crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs validate_query/score paths; rerank already present
            via score_subset.
    impact: Pushes the tiny preset's recall from degrading toward ~0.99 at the SAME 4x footprint; makes int8
            the safe default and unlocks going below the 0.283 size ratio. Highest value-per-line
            in the whole category.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/weaviate/recipes

* Make the HNSW graph traverse on int8/LVQ codes and delete the full-f32 vectors snapshot. Store a
  single global f32 mean[dim] prefix + per-vector scale + i8 residual; build and search the graph
  using the existing dot_f32_i8 SIMD kernel instead of the f32 copy.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/ann/mod.rs (drop `vectors: Vec<f32>`), ann/build.rs + ann/search.rs
            (traverse on codes), and nest-runtime/src/materialize.rs (mean-centered decode); pairs
            with an int8_lvq variant in nest-format/src/encoding/int8.rs.
    impact: Removes the ~n*dim*4 byte RAM snapshot the runtime currently holds beyond mmap (called out as a
            cost in ann/mod.rs), shrinking memory for the tiny/hybrid presets while recall stays
            intact via exact rerank.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/infiniflow/ragflow

* Add an optional chunk_dedup manifest section: a sorted array of {content_hash -> source_id,
  original_span_ref} so a future `nest update`/`nest merge` re-embeds ONLY chunks whose hash is
  absent, copies embeddings/spans for unchanged hashes byte-for-byte from the old mmap, and
  collapses identical chunks across documents to one physical chunk+embedding referenced by many
  spans.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/ (new RESERVED section id 0x09, additive, own SHA-256 surface,
            sorted for byte-identical output); builder logic in
            crates/nest-python/src/build_fn.rs; new nest-cli subcommand.
    impact: Cross-file content-hash dedup collapses repeated boilerplate AND its fp32 embeddings (the largest
            section) when packing whole repos/datasets -- the biggest structural compression lever
            for the north star; also enables incremental edit.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/langchain-ai/langchain, https://github.com/Mintplex-Labs/anything-llm,
            https://github.com/FalkorDB/GraphRAG-SDK

* Add a Matryoshka-aware prefix layout to the embeddings section: record mrl_prefix_dims in the
  manifest extra map and, because importance-ordered dims are already contiguous on disk, run
  pass-1 SIMD scoring over the truncated K-dim prefix (L2-renormalized) to gather candidates, then
  reuse the mandatory exact-cosine rerank over full-dim vectors.
    where:  manifest `extra` field in crates/nest-format/src/manifest/mod.rs (additive, no version bump);
            two-pass path in crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs alongside score_subset; store a
            per-prefix L2-renorm scale or renormalize at score time for honest cosine.
    impact: Cuts hot-loop bytes-touched per candidate ~6-48x (e.g. 256-d int8 prefix vs 3072-d f32) improving
            BOTH size and throughput, stacking multiplicatively with int8/RQ; requires an
            MRL-trained model but is pure additive and deterministic.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/supermemoryai/supermemory

* Add a graph_edges section: a deterministic CSR/CSC adjacency over chunk_ids (src u32, dst u32,
  edge_type u8: 0=NEXT_CHUNK,1=FROM_DOCUMENT,2=ENTITY_LINK), sorted by (src,edge_type,dst), plus a
  bounded-BFS search-graph path that seeds from exact-cosine top-k, expands over edges, and
  re-runs exact cosine rerank on the expanded set.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/ (RESERVED section id 0x09+, own SHA-256); a ~150-line mmap
            graph::Adjacency reader + search-graph path in crates/nest-runtime/src/; 9th nest-cli
            subcommand; entity extraction stays python-side in nest-python.
    impact: Adds the north-star 'graph search' pillar with an O(edges) integer overlay that is highly
            zstd-compressible, and lets nest drop chunk_overlap redundancy from chunks_canonical
            by recovering neighbor context from NEXT_CHUNK edges.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/neo4j/neo4j-graphrag-python, https://github.com/FalkorDB/GraphRAG-SDK

* Add a chunk_hierarchy section (child_chunk_id u32 -> parent_chunk_id u32, level u8 + per-parent
  child-count) and a post-rerank auto-merge pass: count retrieved leaves per parent and, above a
  deterministic majority threshold, swap them for the parent before materialize resolves spans.
  Parent text is NOT stored -- it is reconstructed by concatenating children's
  chunks_original_spans.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/ (RESERVED 0x09+); auto-merge pass in
            crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs after rerank, before materialize_hits; hierarchical
            chunker in crates/nest-python.
    impact: Multi-resolution retrieval at ~9 bytes/edge and zero extra text/embedding bytes; only leaf chunks
            get embedding rows so the hot mmap-scored section stays minimal. Pure
            recall-context-per-byte win.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/run-llama/llama_index

* Generalize RRF to a rank-only sum over N>=2 lists with c exposed in search_contract, add a
  deterministic weighted-sum/linear-alpha fusion mode (final = w_vec*exact_cosine +
  w_kw*normalized_bm25, min-max normalized), and add a pure-integer rank_to_periphery final
  reorder (odds + reversed evens) plus positional window expansion from chunk_ids/spans.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/fusion.rs (generalize rrf_union, add weighted_sum_union);
            search_contract field in crates/nest-format/src/sections/contract.rs;
            rank_to_periphery + window expansion in crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs /
            materialize (reads existing FROZEN sections only).
    impact: Zero added bytes, all deterministic and panic-free, well under the 300-line cap; improves
            downstream LLM answer quality and lets a future third (fuzzy/CJK) signal drop into the
            hybrid path without touching the codec.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/langroid/langroid, https://github.com/langgenius/dify,
            https://github.com/weaviate/Verba, https://github.com/neo4j/neo4j-graphrag-python

* Vendor a model2vec/POTION-style static embedder as an offline, deterministic, zero-dependency
  embedding backend in nest-python: a potion token-vector table (vocab x 256, shipped int8+zstd)
  where embedding = tokenize + gather + mean-pool, and provenance.model_hash becomes the table's
  SHA-256.
    where:  crates/nest-python (or a tiny nest-runtime helper module under the 300-line cap); table can ship
            as a separate nest-encoded asset; wires into the existing model_hash/model_fingerprint
            provenance fields.
    impact: Makes the embedding step itself sovereign and byte-reproducible (no PyTorch/GPU/network),
            replacing a multi-GB neural model with a few-MB lookup table -- directly serves the
            offline/deterministic mission and shrinks the toolchain footprint. Caveat: CJK/Thai
            tokenization quality depends on the underlying sentencepiece.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/run-llama/semtools

* Add a deterministic on-disk embedding cache in the nest-python builder keyed by
  SHA-256(canonical_chunk_text) prefixed by model_fingerprint namespace, so rebuilds and
  cross-corpus dedup never re-embed identical text and aggressive re-encoding experiments
  (int8/RQ/PQ) become cheap to iterate.
    where:  crates/nest-python/src/build_fn.rs (a sidecar cache, NOT in the frozen container); reuses
            provenance model_hash as the namespace.
    impact: Build-time/operational only (saves recompute, not bytes), but it is what makes the whole
            quantization-codec roadmap above cheap to A/B; preserves byte-identical output since
            cache hits return the same f32 vectors.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/langchain-ai/langchain, https://github.com/Mintplex-Labs/anything-llm

* Roadmap PQ and Binary Quantization as new reserved embedding encoding ids (4-255 range): PQ =
  split D into segments, 256-centroid seeded k-means codebook per segment, 1 byte/segment with ADC
  lookup tables (~8-16x); BQ = 1 bit/dim sign, Hamming prefilter via popcnt(x86)/vcnt(NEON)
  already in the SIMD dispatcher, then exact rerank (~32x).
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ (new pq.rs / bq.rs, new SECTION_ENCODING_* ids 4 and 5, additive
            within v1); SIMD Hamming kernel in crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/; both gated behind
            nest's existing mandatory exact rerank.
    impact: Charts the path well past the tiny preset's 0.283 ratio toward 8-32x embedding compression while
            keeping real-cosine ground truth -- the deepest compression lever for 'compress more
            than everyone'.
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/weaviate/recipes, https://github.com/run-llama/semtools,
            https://github.com/infiniflow/ragflow

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Rotational Quantization makes the existing int8/4x path near-lossless (~99% recall@10) by
    adding only a deterministic O(D log D) FWHT rotation + a few header bytes -- same footprint,
    far better recall, so int8 becomes the default and the 0.283 tiny ratio can drop further.
  - LVQ lets the HNSW graph traverse on int8 codes, deleting the runtime's full-f32 `vectors:
    Vec<f32>` snapshot (n*dim*4 bytes of RAM beyond mmap) at equal recall.
  - PQ (~8-16x) and BQ (~32x, 1 bit/dim, ranking-preserving via Hamming MaxSim) as new reserved
    encoding ids push the embeddings section -- nest's largest and the only never-zstd'd section
    -- far below int8, all safe under the mandatory exact rerank.
  - Matryoshka prefix truncation (3072->256 ~12x) stacks multiplicatively with int8/RQ (4x) and
    BQ, and the smaller prefix is what the hot SIMD loop actually reads, improving size AND
    throughput simultaneously.
  - Content-hash dedup collapses repeated/boilerplate chunks AND their fp32 embeddings to one
    physical copy referenced by many spans -- the biggest structural lever when packing whole
    repos/datasets/databases into one container.
  - Version-keyed content-addressed blob dedup stores edit/version history by referencing
    unchanged hashed blobs instead of recopying, so a new corpus version is mostly free; composes
    with zstd/int8 by deduping before encoding the delta.
  - CSC/CSR sparse adjacency stores a whole relationship graph in O(edges) (monotone indices
    arrays, delta+varint+zstd friendly) instead of O(nodes^2) edge-list-of-strings, carrying
    graphs in kilobytes.
  - Hierarchy/lexical-graph edge tables (~9 bytes/edge) plus NEXT_CHUNK neighbor reconstruction
    let nest drop chunk_overlap text from chunks_canonical and rebuild context on read --
    multi-resolution retrieval for near-zero extra bytes.
  - Structure-aware and semantic chunkers (markdown/code/json/AST boundaries) produce more
    self-similar, boundary-aligned chunks_canonical that zstd compresses better and reduce span
    fragmentation.
  - MegaParse-style canonicalize-before-hash (normalize PDF/DOCX/parquet to deterministic markdown
    before chunking) exposes far more cross-document redundancy for zstd and strengthens the
    content_hash stability guarantee -- a precondition for compressing heterogeneous formats at
    all.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - REJECT the loopback-NFSv3 path in smfs and Weaviate/Verba's semantic answer cache -- both bind
    a socket / need online state and would violate nest's offline-by-construction, no-socket
    guarantee. A read-only FUSE mount in a SEPARATE nest-fuse crate (kept out of nest-runtime) is
    acceptable since FUSE opens no network socket.
  - REJECT every network/GPU dependency: MegaParse vision-LLM table reconstruction, Cohere/Jina
    rerankers, OpenAI/gpt-4o calls, hosted Supermemory, FalkorDB/Redis engine. Only the ALGORITHMS
    (budget trimming, chunk indexing, fusion math, CSC traversal) are stealable, never the
    runtimes.
  - Do NOT vendor SuiteSparse:GraphBLAS (heavy C dep, threading) or FalkorDB engine
    (SSPL-licensed, not vendorable) -- a minimal pure-Rust CSC matvec (tens of lines) covers k-hop
    traversal without the dependency. Skip its MVCC/mpsc machinery entirely; nest is frozen
    read-only single-process mmap.
  - Adopt langgraph's CWE-502 lesson as a hard rule: nest must NEVER deserialize arbitrary types
    from a container. Keep fixed section decoders + typed thiserror only; any new section
    (dedup/graph/hierarchy/tensor) must be a strict repr(C) LE decoder, never a constructor-style
    deserializer.
  - All new codecs (RQ rotation, LVQ mean, PQ k-means, BQ) MUST be seeded/deterministic so same
    input => byte-identical file. PQ/RQ k-means and any sampling must use a seeded RNG like the
    existing seeded HNSW; otherwise the reproducible-build contract breaks.
  - Matryoshka requires an MRL-trained model AND per-prefix L2-renormalization of the truncated
    slice -- skipping the renorm silently corrupts cosine. Bake renorm into the writer or
    materialize path; do not ship MRL search without it.
  - Keep additive-only discipline: new sections must use reserved ids 0x09+, new encodings ids
    4-255, and must NOT enter content_hash (optional sections are excluded by design so citations
    stay stable). Only bump NEST_FORMAT_VERSION for a true v2 where an existing field changes
    meaning; codec changes use payload-version bumps like INT8_PAYLOAD_VERSION.
  - Respect the file-hygiene cap: every new module (fwht, pq, bq, graph adjacency, hierarchy,
    fusion variants) must stay <=300 lines (333 hard cap), single-responsibility -- split
    codec/reader/search as the existing ann/ and bm25/ modules do.
  - model2vec/jieba/tree-sitter tokenizers carry large dictionaries or model files -- pick a
    small-footprint, offline, deterministic segmenter or the sovereign/reproducible guarantee and
    the tiny-footprint mission regress. The known CJK/Thai BM25 gap is real but must be fixed
    without a multi-MB dictionary download.
  - NEVER general-compress (zstd/lz4) the embeddings section -- it is scored directly from mmap on
    the hot path; this is the opposite of Jina/DocArray's outer-codec approach and is a core nest
    invariant. Embedding size reduction must come from quantization/dim-reduction (RQ/PQ/BQ/MRL),
    never entropy coding of the live dense bytes.
  - Build-time embedding cache and any incremental-edit staging must live in a sidecar /
    nest-python, NOT mutate the frozen container in place; flush edits via a deterministic rebuild
    so byte-identical reproducibility holds.


##################################################################################################
# data-pipeline  -  ML data lakes, dataset tooling, embedding benchmarks
##################################################################################################

summary:     Three reference systems define the state of the art for ML data-lake / dataset tooling, and their
             transferable value to nest is almost entirely architectural and metadata-layout, not
             codec: nest already wins on the embedding hot path (mmap SIMD dot, int8/f16
             quantization, mandatory exact rerank). Deep Lake (Apache-2.0) is the highest-value
             steal: its run-length + cumulative-prefix-index encoders
             (ChunkIdEncoder/BytePositionsEncoder/ShapeEncoder) collapse per-sample sidecar
             metadata to O(distinct-runs) with O(log n) random access and no payload decode. This
             maps directly onto nest's cold-path metadata problem:
             crates/nest-format/src/sections/spans.rs today writes a full source_uri string plus
             two raw little-endian u64 per chunk for every chunk, so a 5k-chunk single-document
             corpus stores ~5k repeated URIs and ~80KB of offsets that are almost always
             contiguous (end[i]==start[i+1]) - structural redundancy zstd only partially removes.
             img2dataset (MIT) contributes the "shadow columnar sidecar" idea (tiny filterable SoA
             scalar columns separated from heavy payload, scanned without decoding the bulk) and
             the explicit 1:1 positional-alignment invariant; both fit nest's single-file model as
             a new additive section (reserved id 0x09+) plus a hardened frozen guarantee. MTEB
             (Apache-2.0) contributes a rigorous retrieval-quality protocol (nDCG@k / MRR@k /
             Recall@k via a trec_eval-equivalent metric set, content-addressed result caching,
             chunked exact top-k) that turns nest's anecdotal "tiny preset recall@10 0.992" into a
             reproducible, seeded, per-preset quality report and lets nest push compression harder
             with measured non-regression. None of the three offers a new entropy coder or
             quantizer to beat nest's embedding stack; the wins are structural-redundancy
             elimination in metadata, candidate pre-filtering before SIMD scoring, and a quality
             yardstick. All three respect nest's offline/deterministic/single-file constraints if
             the network/distributed machinery is dropped (img2dataset's downloader, MTEB's HF Hub
             cache tier) and re-implemented in-crate from the published ideas rather than
             vendored.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The data-pipeline category converges on one dominant pattern: separate heavy opaque payload from
tiny structured metadata, and make per-sample metadata grow with information content (distinct
runs) rather than with sample count. Deep Lake formalizes this with run-length +
cumulative-last-index encoders: each sidecar row is (value, cumulative_last_sample_index_covered);
consecutive identical values (same chunk, same shape, same per-sample byte size) collapse to one
row, and lookup is a binary search over the cumulative column - so a fixed-shape 1B-sample tensor
needs ONE shape row and ~150MB of chunk-id encoder per 1PB, all addressable in O(log n) integer
ops with zero payload decompression. It splits sidecar metadata into a triplet (ChunkIdEncoder:
sample->chunk, BytePositionsEncoder: per-sample byte size with offsets recovered by prefix sum,
ShapeEncoder: sample shape), stores codec bytes verbatim (copy-without-recode when codecs match),
packs variable-length samples into ~8MB bounded chunks, tiles oversized samples, and serves
vectors with hnswlib HNSW that exact-linear-scans under ~100k rows - the same mandatory-exact
philosophy nest enforces. img2dataset operationalizes the split as a "shadow sidecar": a parquet
metadata file mirrors the heavy WebDataset tar 1:1 (~1.6MB vs ~270MB tar in LAION-400M, >150x
smaller), so all filtering/stats/validation run on the small columnar file without touching
payload, while a deterministic positional composite key (SSSSSIIII = shard-id prefix + in-shard
index) preserves exact order alignment across tar/parquet/npy and makes builds resumable;
multi-tier optional hashing (md5/sha256/sha512) verifies samples. MTEB defines the
quality-measurement state of the art: a fixed BEIR-derived task schema (corpus, queries,
relevant_docs as qid->cid->rel, top_ranked candidate lists), chunked exact top-k similarity
(corpus_chunk_size sub-batches keep the matmul working set bounded so corpora larger than RAM
still score), cosine implemented as inner product on L2-normalized vectors, scoring via
pytrec_eval (nDCG@k, MRR@k, Recall@k, MAP, capped recall), and content-addressed
per-(model,revision,task) JSON results in a tiered HF-Hub/disk/in-memory cache so identical inputs
never recompute. The unifying lesson for nest: the scored array stays contiguous, raw, and
order-aligned; everything else is small, columnar, structurally de-redundant, and
quality-certified.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Deep Lake run-length + cumulative-prefix-index encoder: rows of (value,
   cumulative_last_index_covered), identical consecutive samples collapse to one row, O(log n)
   binary-search lookup with zero payload decode - eliminates structural redundancy in per-chunk
   metadata that zstd alone leaves on the table.
2. Triplet split of sidecar metadata (chunk-id / byte-positions / shape) with offsets recovered by
   prefix sum rather than stored verbatim - directly maps to nest spans where contiguous chunks
   satisfy end[i]==start[i+1] and need only a per-chunk length plus run boundaries.
3. img2dataset shadow columnar sidecar: tiny filterable SoA scalar columns (source_id, lang,
   byte_len, token_count, tag) stored apart from heavy payload, scanned to pre-filter candidates
   WITHOUT decoding text or touching embeddings (>150x size gap demonstrated).
4. Deduplicated value dictionary + run-length id stream for repeated strings (source_uri) - one
   run per source document instead of one verbatim string per chunk; insertion-stable dictionary
   keeps it deterministic.
5. Explicit 1:1 positional-alignment invariant across all parallel artifacts addressable by index
   i - nest's on-disk analog hardens the chunk_ids[i]/canonical[i]/spans[i]/embeddings[i] contract
   that nest://content_hash/chunk_id already relies on.
6. MTEB reproducible retrieval-quality protocol: nDCG@k / MRR@k / Recall@k via
   trec_eval-equivalent scoring over a fixed task schema, turning anecdotal recall claims into
   seeded per-preset reports that justify pushing compression harder.
7. MTEB chunked exact top-k (corpus_chunk_size sub-batches, descending sort, bounded working set)
   - formalizes nest's exact rerank as a cache-friendly streaming pass over mmap'd embedding
   sub-blocks for corpora larger than cache.
8. Content-addressed 'same inputs never recompute' result caching - nest's content_hash already
   delivers this for free; can self-certify recall inside the container.
9. Deep Lake native copy-without-recode (store codec bytes verbatim when codecs match) - principle
   for future nest payload types (pdf/audio/video) to avoid lossy re-encode round-trips.
10. img2dataset composite positional key (sortable shard-prefix + monotone index) enabling
   append-only incremental/extend rebuilds as new logical shard ranges without rewriting prior
   sections - aligns with nest's additive-only rule.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Re-encode the chunks_original_spans (0x03) section with a deduplicated source_uri dictionary +
  run-length uri_id stream, plus delta/prefix-sum span encoding: for each contiguous run store
  (run_length, per-chunk length) and reconstruct absolute byte_start/byte_end by prefix sum
  exactly like Deep Lake's BytePositionsEncoder. Add a new section-encoding id (e.g.
  SECTION_ENCODING_SPANS_RLE = 4, within the reserved 4-255 range) so decode_payload dispatches to
  the new codec and decodes byte-identically to the current OriginalSpan vec.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/spans.rs (new encode/decode),
            crates/nest-format/src/encoding/mod.rs decode_payload dispatch + a new encoding
            module, crates/nest-format/src/layout/mod.rs encoding-id constant,
            crates/nest-format/src/writer/build.rs spans push site (replace maybe_zstd with the
            RLE encoder, still zstd-eligible on top).
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   activeloopai/deeplake

* Add an optional additive 'chunk_scalars' section (reserve id 0x09) holding a compact columnar
  SoA of per-chunk filterable scalars (source_id u32, lang tag, byte_len/token_count varint,
  dataset tag) declared via a manifest capability, so nest-runtime can pre-filter candidate
  indices before SIMD scoring and nest-cli stats/filtered-search run as a pure metadata scan that
  never decodes chunks_canonical or touches embeddings. Columns are homogeneous, delta-friendly,
  and zstd-eligible. Optional sections already do NOT participate in content_hash, so citations
  stay valid and no version bump is needed.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/layout/mod.rs (OPTIONAL_SECTIONS + id 0x09), new
            crates/nest-format/src/sections/chunk_scalars.rs (<=300 lines, single-responsibility),
            manifest capability flag in crates/nest-format/src/manifest, a candidate pre-filter
            hook in crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs score_all/score_subset, and a filter flag in
            crates/nest-cli/src/cmd/search.rs + stats.rs.
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   rom1504/img2dataset, activeloopai/deeplake

* Build a deterministic seeded retrieval-quality harness for the existing nest benchmark
  subcommand: adopt MTEB's task schema (corpus/queries/relevant_docs/top_ranked) and report
  nDCG@k, MRR@k, Recall@k vs the exact f32 baseline per preset, replacing today's single recall@k
  overlap number. One small metrics module per metric to honor the <=300-line
  single-responsibility rule; pure compute, no socket, fully offline and deterministic with a
  fixed seed.
    where:  crates/nest-cli/src/cmd/benchmark.rs (extend recall block into a metrics report), new
            crates/nest-runtime/src/metrics/ndcg.rs + mrr.rs + recall.rs modules, optionally
            exposed through nest-python.
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   embeddings-benchmark/mteb

* Formalize the 1:1 ordered-alignment invariant (chunk_ids[i], chunks_canonical[i],
  chunks_original_spans[i], embeddings[i], and any new scalar column[i] all share index i) as an
  explicit frozen guarantee and assert it in the reader/validate path, strengthening the
  nest://content_hash/chunk_id resolution contract that materialize_hits already assumes when it
  indexes self.spans[idx] and self.chunk_ids[idx] in lockstep.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/reader/validate.rs (cross-section length-equality assertions), documented
            in the layout module header and CONTRIBUTING conventions.
    impact: medium
    effort: low
    from:   rom1504/img2dataset

* Apply the same dictionary+RLE treatment to provenance and consider it for chunk_ids: provenance
  often repeats dataset-level fields across chunks; dedup them to one entry per distinct source.
  Leave chunk_ids' SHA-256 hex bodies raw (high-entropy, near-incompressible, correctly noted in
  build.rs) but RLE the structural framing if a sortable source-shard prefix is later adopted.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/provenance.rs, with the shared dictionary helper factored into
            crates/nest-format/src/sections/codec.rs to stay DRY and under the line cap.
    impact: medium
    effort: low
    from:   activeloopai/deeplake, rom1504/img2dataset

* Stream the exact-cosine rerank over mmap'd embedding sub-blocks using an MTEB-style
  corpus_chunk_size loop so the working set stays cache-resident on corpora larger than L2/L3,
  instead of one monolithic pass over all rows in score_all. Pure perf/locality refactor; results
  are byte-identical so determinism and the exact-rerank guarantee are untouched.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs score_all (and score_subset for large unions), keeping the SIMD
            dispatcher in crates/nest-runtime/src/simd unchanged.
    impact: medium
    effort: low
    from:   embeddings-benchmark/mteb

* Optionally embed an additive eval-results section (reserve a later 0x09+ id) recording
  per-preset nDCG@k/Recall@k vs the exact f32 baseline, so a .nest container self-certifies its
  retrieval quality without re-running the harness; nest's content_hash already gives MTEB-style
  'same inputs never recompute' so the section is a cache of a deterministic computation.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/layout/mod.rs (OPTIONAL_SECTIONS), new
            crates/nest-format/src/sections/eval_results.rs, populated by the nest-cli benchmark
            harness.
    impact: low
    effort: low
    from:   embeddings-benchmark/mteb

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - spans (0x03) dictionary+RLE: collapse ~5k verbatim source_uri strings to one dictionary entry
    per distinct document plus a run-length id stream - for single-document corpora this is
    near-constant instead of O(num_chunks), eliminating redundancy zstd only partially removes.
  - spans offset delta/prefix-sum: contiguous chunks satisfy end[i]==start[i+1], so store one
    run-length plus per-chunk length and recover absolute u64 offsets by prefix sum, replacing ~16
    bytes of raw little-endian u64 per chunk (~80KB at 5k chunks) with a handful of bytes per run;
    the delta stream is also far more zstd-compressible than raw 64-bit offsets.
  - Eliminate structural redundancy BEFORE the entropy coder: zstd shrinks bytes but cannot remove
    the inherent redundancy of contiguous 64-bit offsets and repeated strings; doing
    dictionary+RLE first then zstd compounds the two, which is the core
    compress-more-than-everyone lever on the cold path.
  - Columnar SoA chunk_scalars section: homogeneous single-type columns (lang, byte_len,
    token_count) compress dramatically better under zstd than today's row-interleaved metadata,
    and delta-coding monotone columns shrinks them further.
  - Provenance dedup: one entry per distinct source instead of repeated dataset-level fields
    across every chunk reduces provenance from O(num_chunks) toward O(num_sources).
  - MTEB-style measured non-regression unlocks more aggressive embedding compression: with
    reproducible nDCG@k/Recall@k vs f32, nest can justify pushing int8 / smaller dims / tighter
    hnsw in the tiny preset (already 0.283 size ratio, recall@10 0.992) with proof rather than
    guesswork - quality measurement is itself a compression enabler.
  - All of the above target ONLY non-embedding cold-path sections (zstd-eligible, never the SIMD
    hot path) and are read once during materialize/cite, so they add zero query-latency cost while
    shrinking the file.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Do NOT vendor code: Deep Lake and MTEB are Apache-2.0, img2dataset MIT - re-implement the
    run-length/cumulative-index and metric ideas from the published descriptions (Deep Lake CIDR
    2023 paper) in-crate; the encoder/metric concepts are freely transferable but copying source
    pulls in license/attribution and bloat that conflict with the small clean-core goal.
  - Frozen-format discipline: any spans re-encoding must decode byte-identically to the current
    OriginalSpan representation so content_hash (over DECODED canonical sections) stays stable and
    citations keep resolving; use a new encoding id in the reserved 4-255 range or a transparent
    in-section re-encode, and never bump NEST_FORMAT_VERSION (that is reserved for a true v2 where
    an existing field changes meaning).
  - Determinism: the source_uri dictionary must be insertion-stable (not hash-ordered) and RLE
    runs must form deterministically, or byte-identical reproducibility (same chunks + same model
    fingerprint => identical file) breaks; mirror the existing alphabetical-bm25 / seeded-hnsw
    discipline.
  - Drop all network/distributed machinery: img2dataset's downloader/fsspec/pyspark and MTEB's
    HuggingFace-Hub remote cache tier violate offline-by-construction (runtime never opens a
    socket) - only the on-disk layout, positional-key, and metric ideas transfer; keep MTEB's
    tiered cache to local-filesystem + in-memory only.
  - Line/scope cap: each new piece (spans codec, chunk_scalars section, each metric) must be its
    own <=300-line single-responsibility module (333 hard cap); resist a monolithic 'metadata-v2'
    module - factor the shared dictionary helper into sections/codec.rs.
  - Embeddings stay raw and mmap-scored: none of these ideas may touch the embeddings section
    encoding or the SIMD scoring path; Deep Lake's chunk-packing/tiling is for variable-shape
    tensors and must not fragment nest's contiguous, 64-byte-aligned, directly-scored embedding
    array.
  - Pre-filter must not weaken the exact-rerank guarantee: a chunk_scalars candidate filter
    changes WHICH chunks are scored, not HOW they are scored, and must be opt-in and documented so
    it never silently degrades the recall=1.0 exact ground truth the search_contract promises.
  - Additive-only for any new section: chunk_scalars / eval_results must be OPTIONAL sections (id
    0x09+) that do not participate in content_hash, so adding them to an existing corpus does not
    invalidate prior citations; gate them behind a manifest capability flag.
  - bm25 tokenizer is still word-segmented (degrades on CJK/Thai) - a known gap unrelated to these
    steals; do not let a metadata-quality harness mask that the lexical path needs separate
    attention for the PT-BR/CJK demo corpora.
  - MTEB metrics depend on relevance judgments (relevant_docs); nest's demo corpus has none yet,
    so the quality harness needs a seeded synthetic or hand-labeled qrels set to be meaningful -
    budget for building that, or the nDCG/MRR numbers are vacuous.


##################################################################################################
# content-addressed  -  content-addressed, reproducible, and distributed storage
##################################################################################################

summary:     The content-addressed cluster (IPFS/Kubo, Nix/NAR, Maven reproducible-central) collectively proves
             three things nest already half-implements and one thing it does not. First, nest's
             existing primitives are validated prior-art: content_hash over decoded canonical
             sections (crates/nest-format/src/reader/decode.rs:53-66) is already a NAR-grade
             construction - length-prefixed, domain-separated by section name, alphabetically
             ordered via CANONICAL_SECTIONS (layout/mod.rs:87). That is exactly Nix's
             NarHash-vs-FileHash split (logical identity stable across codec; physical checksum
             over stored bytes) and Kubo's "multihash indexes storage, encoding is a wrapper"
             model. The nest://content_hash/chunk_id citation mirrors CIDv1 + raw-leaves block
             addressing. So three extracts' headline recommendation ("adopt NAR framing for
             content_hash") is ALREADY DONE; nest should claim the validation, not redo the work.
             The genuinely new, high-value lever the whole cluster points at is what nest does NOT
             do: deduplication before entropy coding. nest today chunks upstream in Python
             (build_fn.rs receives pre-embedded chunks with explicit spans) and zstd-compresses
             each section independently (writer/payload.rs:47 maybe_zstd) with no shared
             dictionary and no cross-chunk or cross-file dedup. chunks_canonical
             (sections/canonical.rs) stores every chunk's text verbatim before zstd sees it. IPFS
             content-defined chunking (FastCDC/Gear rolling hash) + block-level dedup, and Nix
             store-optimise hard-link dedup (25-35% on redundant corpora purely from content-hash
             equality), are the single biggest untapped compression mechanism for nest's
             boilerplate-heavy targets (PT-BR news templates, repeated JSON keys, legal text,
             whole repos). The hard-won cross-cutting lesson is Nix's: dedup and CDC MUST run on
             decompressed bytes, with compression applied per-chunk/per-block AFTERWARD - getting
             this order wrong destroys the ratio. Maven contributes the
             reproducibility-as-verifiable-artifact discipline: a tiny declarative recipe
             (.buildspec) plus a per-output sorted SHA manifest (.buildinfo) whose equality IS the
             proof, with length recorded for free fast-reject, turning nest's "same chunks + same
             model => byte-identical" claim into something a third party re-derives offline
             without byte-diffing 100MB of embeddings.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
State of the art across the cluster: (1) Content identity is decoupled from physical encoding via
a canonical, metadata-stripped serialization hashed independently of its compressed form. Nix NAR
(magic 'nix-archive-1', u64-LE length-prefixed strings zero-padded to 8 bytes, dir entries sorted
by name, all metadata stripped except the executable bit) is the reference; narinfo carries both
NarHash/NarSize (logical, uncompressed) and FileHash/FileSize (physical, compressed) so signatures
over the uncompressed form survive recompression. (2) Content-defined chunking replaces
fixed-offset splitting: a rolling hash (Rabin polynomial; Buzhash cyclic-poly, ~3x faster;
Gear/FastCDC single shift+add, ~1350 MB/s) sets a boundary when the low/high N fingerprint bits
hit a target, so a local edit perturbs only one or two chunks and downstream boundaries (and their
hashes) stay stable - yielding high cross-version dedup. FastCDC adds zero-padded mask widening,
cut-point skipping below min-size, and normalized chunking for a tight size distribution. (3)
Block/chunk dedup keyed by content hash collapses identical content to one stored object
regardless of transport (Kubo blockstore raw-leaves where block multihash == plain content hash;
Nix store-optimise hard-links identical NAR files into .links/<checksum>; nix-casync/Tvix CDC
chunk-store + caibx index for sub-file dedup). (4) The order rule: compression sits OUTSIDE the
chunking/dedup layer (CDC on decompressed bytes, compress per chunk afterward) or dedup collapses
to near-zero. (5) Reproducibility is engineered and externally verifiable: pin the minimal input
set, normalize ordering (sort entries) and strip incidental metadata/timestamps before hashing
(killed ~92% of Maven nondeterminism), and prove it by equality of a per-output content manifest
rather than a byte diff. nest already satisfies (1) for its six canonical sections and the
citation layer, and partially (5) via reproducible(true) + REPRODUCIBLE_CREATED; it does NOT yet
do (2), (3), or expose (5) as a verifiable manifest.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Cross-chunk + cross-file content-hash dedup BEFORE zstd (Nix store-optimise + Kubo blockstore).
   Hash each canonical chunk (machinery already exists: chunk_id in chunk.rs is a SHA-256 over
   canonical_text), store each unique chunk's bytes once in chunks_canonical, and replace
   duplicates with a back-reference index. Free 25-35% on boilerplate-heavy corpora purely from
   content-hash equality, compounding multiplicatively with the existing per-section zstd. Highest
   value, lowest recall risk (text sections only, embeddings untouched).
2. FastCDC/Gear content-defined chunking for large blob inputs and edit-local rechunking. A small
   deterministic seeded rolling-hash kernel (left-shift+add, O(window) memory, ~1350 MB/s,
   SIMD-friendly) added to the Python build pipeline so editing one paragraph re-emits only the
   touched chunk(s); unchanged chunks keep byte-identical canonical_text + embeddings, making
   real-time-edit diffs tiny and enabling cheap incremental/append builds for the
   pdf/json/parquet/repo roadmap.
3. The 'compress outside the chunking layer, CDC on decompressed bytes' invariant (Nix
   nix-casync/Tvix lesson). A one-line design constraint that prevents the classic mistake of
   compressing then chunking, which would silently destroy nest's dedup ratio. Cheap to honor,
   expensive to discover late.
4. NarHash/FileHash dual-identity as the formal license to aggressively re-encode. nest's
   content_hash (decoded) vs per-section checksum (physical) IS this split and is already correct;
   the innovation to claim is the GUARANTEE it unlocks: re-encode a .nest from exact->tiny preset
   and prove identical content_hash, so the smallest preset provably carries the same content as
   the exact one. A killer sovereign re-packaging property worth a test + doc, not new code.
5. Reproducibility manifest a la Maven .buildspec/.buildinfo: a minimal declarative build recipe
   (model_hash, chunker_version, preset, section order) plus a flat sorted lowercase-hex
   per-section manifest (section_id, decoded_length, content_checksum). decoded_length gives free
   fast-reject before hashing; manifest equality proves two independent builds match without
   byte-diffing embeddings. Turns nest's determinism claim into a third-party-verifiable artifact,
   offline.
6. Canonical/sorted ordering as a dual-purpose primitive: it is simultaneously the reproducibility
   guarantee AND a zstd ratio win (sorting groups similar bytes). nest already sorts bm25 terms
   and section IDs; the innovation is extending the discipline consciously to every new section so
   ordering is never left to input order.
7. Shorter copy-paste-safe citation tokens via a confusable-free base32 (Nix nix-base32 omits
   e/o/u/t) over a truncated digest. Optional ergonomic upgrade to nest://content_hash/chunk_id so
   citations are shorter and dirty-word/confusable-free, with the full SHA-256 still available for
   verification.
8. Block-level deduped citation resolution (Kubo): once chunks_canonical is deduped, a citation
   can resolve to a single shared physical block, so identical boilerplate across many
   chunks/files cites one stored region - content-addressing all the way down to the byte span.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add a build-time content-hash dedup pass for chunks_canonical (and later
  chunks_original_spans/blob sections): SHA-256 each chunk's canonical_text, keep a first-seen
  table, store each unique text once, and emit a parallel u32 back-reference array (chunk index ->
  unique-block index). Expose as a new additive encoding id in the reserved 4-255 range (e.g.
  SECTION_ENCODING_DEDUP) or an internal payload variant of chunks_canonical, with zstd applied to
  the deduped unique-block pool. content_hash stays computed over the DECODED (re-expanded) bytes
  so it is unchanged and citations stay stable.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ (new dedup.rs module, <=300 loc, typed thiserror) wired into
            crates/nest-format/src/writer/payload.rs maybe_zstd path and
            crates/nest-format/src/sections/canonical.rs encode/decode; decode re-expands before
            crates/nest-format/src/reader/decode.rs content_hash sees it so the hash and
            nest://content_hash/chunk_id are invariant.
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/NixOS/nix, https://github.com/NixOS/nixpkgs (Nix store / NAR model, implemented
            in NixOS/nix), https://github.com/ipfs/kubo

* Enforce and document the 'dedup/CDC on decompressed bytes, compress afterward' invariant
  directly in code: the dedup pass operates on raw canonical bytes and ONLY then is the
  unique-block pool zstd-encoded; add a comment + a writer test asserting that
  compressing-then-deduping is never done. This is the Nix nix-casync/Tvix lesson encoded as a
  guardrail so a future contributor cannot accidentally invert the order and silently kill the
  ratio.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/writer/payload.rs (ordering in maybe_zstd / new dedup wrapper) and
            crates/nest-format/src/writer/tests.rs (a regression test that a corpus of repeated
            chunks shrinks under dedup+zstd more than zstd alone).
    impact: high
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/NixOS/nix
