* Add a FastCDC/Gear content-defined chunker as a deterministic seeded kernel for large blob
  inputs, used by the Python build pipeline as an alternative to upstream token/sentence
  segmentation. Single left-shift+add rolling hash, seeded mask (seed in manifest/chunker_version
  for deterministic-build compliance), min/avg/max size params, cut-point skipping. Edit-local
  boundaries mean a one-paragraph edit re-emits only touched chunks, so unchanged chunks keep
  byte-identical canonical_text + embeddings.
    where:  new encoding-neutral module crates/nest-format/src/chunking.rs (rolling hash + seeded mask, <=300
            loc, repr(C) little-endian if any state serialized, typed errors) exposed to
            crates/nest-python/src/ so the Python builder can call it before embedding;
            chunker_version string already threads through chunk.rs chunk_id() so determinism is
            captured.
    impact: medium
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/ipfs/kubo, https://github.com/NixOS/nix

* Emit a reproducibility manifest as an additive section (id 0x09+) or sidecar: a flat, sorted,
  lowercase-hex list of (section_id, decoded_length, content_checksum) plus a minimal recipe
  (model_hash, chunker_version, preset, section order). decoded_length enables O(1) fast-reject
  before any hash. Two independently-built .nest files compare by manifest equality without
  byte-diffing embeddings.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/manifest/ (recipe fields) + crates/nest-format/src/reader/decode.rs
            (already computes per-section decoded bytes; expose a manifest builder) consumed by a
            new crates/nest-cli/src/cmd/verify_reproducible.rs sibling to validate.rs (9th
            subcommand, offline, additive).
    impact: medium
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/jvm-repo-rebuild/reproducible-central

* Document and test the NarHash/FileHash re-encoding guarantee as a first-class sovereign feature:
  re-encode an existing .nest from exact (raw/f32) to compressed (zstd/f16 for text only) and
  assert content_hash_hex() is byte-identical for non-quantized sections. This is the Nix
  recompression-invariance property; nest's writer/tests.rs:105 already tests zstd vs raw
  equality, so extend it to a full preset re-pack round-trip and surface it in docs as 'ship the
  smallest preset, prove same content'.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/writer/tests.rs (extend zstd_encoding_preserves_content_hash to a preset
            re-pack) and a doc note in the frozen-format spec; no runtime code change, pure
            validation + claim.
    impact: medium
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/NixOS/nix, https://github.com/NixOS/nixpkgs (Nix store / NAR model, implemented
            in NixOS/nix)

* Adopt a confusable-free base32 (nix-base32 alphabet, omits e/o/u/t) as an OPTIONAL shorter
  citation token rendering of content_hash, keeping the full sha256:<hex> as the verifiable
  canonical form. Shorter, copy-paste-safe nest:// URIs for humans without changing the on-disk
  hash.
    where:  crates/nest-cli/src/cmd/cite.rs (render a short token alongside content_hash) + a small helper in
            crates/nest-format/src/ (encode only, decode still accepts full hex). Purely additive,
            no format change.
    impact: low
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/NixOS/nix, https://github.com/NixOS/nixpkgs (Nix store / NAR model, implemented
            in NixOS/nix)

* When dedup lands, make citation resolution block-aware: a nest:// citation whose chunk text is a
  duplicate resolves to the single shared physical block, and cite.rs reports the dedup-block
  index. This is Kubo raw-leaves block addressing applied to nest's chunk layer and proves
  content-addressing all the way to the byte span.
    where:  crates/nest-cli/src/cmd/cite.rs (after dedup decode, map chunk index -> unique-block index and
            surface it) building on the dedup back-reference array from recommendation 1.
    impact: low
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/ipfs/kubo

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Cross-chunk dedup of chunks_canonical before zstd: store each unique canonical_text once + a
    u32 back-reference array. On boilerplate-heavy corpora (PT-BR fake-news templates, repeated
    JSON keys/values, legal forms) this removes redundancy that per-section zstd cannot fully
    recover across distant chunks, compounding with the existing zstd/f16/int8 stack. Nix
    store-optimise shows 25-35% on redundant corpora from content-hash equality ALONE, before any
    entropy coder.
  - Cross-FILE dedup across a corpus of .nest files via content_hash equality: identical
    chunks/sections shared across many files store once and reference by content_hash (Maven 'same
    coordinates => same bytes' becomes 'same chunk => stored once'). Enables a future
    distributed/pack format that is far smaller than the sum of independent files.
  - FastCDC edit-local rechunking turns O(file) re-encodes into O(changed-chunks): a one-paragraph
    edit re-emits one or two chunks, so an incremental/append build writes a tiny diff instead of
    a whole new file - the on-disk delta between two corpus versions becomes proportional to the
    edit, not the corpus.
  - Honoring 'CDC/dedup on decompressed bytes, compress afterward' protects the entire dedup
    ratio: shared substrings are collapsed BEFORE zstd's window, so zstd then only has to compress
    the unique residue. Inverting the order (the classic mistake) would leave duplicates hidden
    inside compressed blocks and recover almost nothing.
  - Canonical/sorted entry ordering is a free zstd ratio win on every non-embedding section
    (grouping similar bytes shortens match distances), and it is the same operation that
    guarantees reproducibility - one discipline, two payoffs. nest already does this for bm25
    terms and section IDs; extending it to all sections is pure upside.
  - A zstd shared dictionary trained over the unique-block pool (natural follow-on once dedup
    exists) further shrinks many small similar chunks that individually defeat per-chunk zstd
    framing overhead - applies only to non-embedding sections, zero recall cost.
  - All wins are confined to non-embedding sections (canonical text, spans, future blob payloads);
    embeddings remain raw/quantized and scored straight off mmap, so every byte saved is
    recall-free and the SIMD hot path is untouched.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - DO NOT compress before dedup/CDC. The Nix nix-casync/Tvix lesson is load-bearing: CDC and
    content-hash dedup must run on decompressed bytes with compression applied per unique-block
    afterward, or dedup collapses to near-zero. Encode this as a code-level invariant + regression
    test, not just a comment.
  - NEVER dedup or CDC the embeddings section. Embeddings are scored directly from mmap on the
    SIMD hot path (AVX2/NEON dot products) and are intentionally never compressed; any indirection
    (back-reference table, variable-size blocks) would break the contiguous repr(C) little-endian
    layout and the alignment the SIMD dispatcher relies on. Dedup is text/span/blob-only.
  - Dedup must keep content_hash invariant. content_hash (decode.rs:53-66) is computed over
    DECODED canonical bytes; the dedup decoder MUST re-expand to the identical logical byte stream
    before the hash sees it, or every citation nest://content_hash/chunk_id breaks. This is the
    whole point of the NarHash/FileHash split - keep dedup strictly a physical-encoding concern.
  - Stay additive within v1. A dedup encoding id belongs in the reserved 4-255 range and a
    chunk-store/manifest section in 0x09+; only a change to the MEANING of an existing field (e.g.
    redefining content_hash preimage) justifies bumping NEST_FORMAT_VERSION to a true v2. Do not
    let dedup leak into a format break.
  - Determinism is non-negotiable. CDC rolling-hash seed/mask and dedup first-seen ordering must
    be fully seeded and deterministic (seed captured via chunker_version/manifest) so 'same chunks
    + same model fingerprint => byte-identical file' still holds. A HashMap iteration-order leak
    or unseeded mask would silently break reproducibility.
  - Single-file + offline must survive. Kubo's blockstore and Nix's binary cache are multi-file,
    networked stores; nest must keep dedup INSIDE one .nest container and as a one-shot build-time
    pass - no socket, no external block store, no separate index file at runtime. Cross-file
    dedup/pack is a future opt-in format, never the default sovereign single-file path.
  - Respect the small-core budget: <=300 lines/file (333 hard cap), single-responsibility, never
    panic in library code (typed thiserror). A CDC kernel + dedup module are each one file; resist
    pulling in heavy crates (avoid vendoring Kubo Go or Nix LGPL code - reimplement the algorithms
    clean-room from spec; standalone Rust gearhash/fastcdc are MIT/Apache if a dependency is
    justified, but a hand-rolled <=300-loc kernel fits nest's by-hand ethos better).
  - Do not over-build the reproducibility manifest. Maven's value is the SMALL recipe + sorted
    hash list, not a verification farm; nest's bounded single-file scope means a manifest section
    + one verify-reproducible subcommand suffices. Avoid Docker/container pinning and
    diffoscope-style deep diff machinery - out of scope for a solo, minimal-budget, offline tool.
  - Truncated/base32 citation tokens are ergonomic ONLY; never let a truncated digest become the
    verification surface. Keep full sha256:<hex> as the canonical, collision-safe identity and
    treat short base32 as a display convenience that still resolves to the full hash.


##################################################################################################
# factcheck-datasets  -  fact-checking / misinformation resources and datasets (nest demo domain + truth features)
##################################################################################################

summary:     This category contributes zero codec/SIMD/on-disk machinery to nest's "compress more than
             everyone" north star; every repo is either a dataset or a pure-Python LLM/sklearn
             framework. Its real value is domain modeling for the demo corpus and a credible
             "truth feature" narrative, plus three structural-compression levers
             (dedup-by-reference, near-duplicate clustering, low-cardinality enum
             dictionary-coding) that attack corpus cardinality before the codec runs. The
             collective state of the art is a converged "claim -> evidence -> stance/verdict ->
             justification" pipeline with a normalizable veracity axis (FEVER 3-way
             SUPPORTS/REFUTES/NEI; ClaimReview ratingValue/bestRating in [0,1]; dual
             original+normalized labels). Critically, nest's provenance section (0x05) is already
             a schema-free per-build JSON blob (crates/nest-format/src/sections/provenance.rs)
             threaded straight from Python (build_fn.rs), so nearly all "truth feature" work is
             additive metadata + a post-rerank facet in search.rs materialize_hits - NO new
             section id, NO NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump. Most extracts over-reach by proposing
             reserved sections 0x09+; for v1 that is unnecessary except for true graph topology.
             The one genuinely structured Rust opportunity is TabFact's typed-operator DSL
             (deterministic symbolic table verification), but it is a large new subsystem and
             should stay roadmap-only.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The field has converged on a four-stage pipeline (claim detection/check-worthiness -> evidence
retrieval -> verdict prediction -> justification) over a normalizable veracity axis. Three
labeling schemes dominate and interconvert: FEVER 3-way SUPPORTS/REFUTES/NOT-ENOUGH-INFO (NEI =
explicit abstention); Factcheck-GPT/OpenFactCheck stance vocabularies (CP/PS/RE/IR collapsible to
3-way, or True/False/Unknown ternary); and schema.org ClaimReview's numeric ratingValue/bestRating
giving a source-agnostic [0,1] truth score, with MisInfoText's dual-label idea (keep verbatim
original_label AND a normalized ordinal {false..true}) making the projection lossless. Evidence is
granularity-typed (sentence/document/table/multi-hop chain/conversation) and bound to spans
(Wikipedia sentence ids), directly analogous to nest://content_hash/chunk_id. Two divergent
verification paradigms: (a) retrieve-then-verify with neural similarity + LLM stance
(FactCheck-GPT, OpenFactCheck - but these are online/LLM-bound, violating nest's
offline-by-construction rule), and (b) TabFact's deterministic symbolic execution - compile NL
statements into a closed ~60-op typed DSL with trigger-word gating and memoized beam search, where
the program's boolean OUTPUT is the verdict (fully reproducible, explainable, no learned score).
On the data-engineering side: FakeNewsNet's dehydration (ship manifest + content-addressed object
pool, hydrate on demand) and GNN-FakeNews's two-plane split (integer graph topology via
COO+partition-index vs float feature matrices, different codecs each) are the transferable storage
patterns. PT-BR demo sources (Fake.br, FakeRecogna, FACTCK.BR, FakeTrue.Br) ship
aligned/length-debiased corpora with 8-25 field metadata schemas.

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. ClaimReview ratingValue/bestRating normalized [0,1] verdict (FACTCK.BR)
2. Dual original+normalized ordinal label, lossless projection (MisInfoText)
3. Dedup-by-reference + dehydrated manifest mode (FakeNewsNet)
4. Build-time near-duplicate clustering to cut embedding count (UglyFeed)
5. Two-plane integer-topology vs float-feature codec split (GNN-FakeNews)
6. TabFact typed-operator DSL, boolean output IS the verdict (TabFact)
7. Atomic-claim -> evidence -> stance -> verdict record schema (FactCheck-GPT)
8. Deterministic size-normalization pair truncation (Fake.br)
9. Register-by-name pipeline-as-data in search_contract (OpenFactCheck)
10. Aligned multi-source PT-BR corpora with enum-heavy metadata (FakeRecogna)

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Define a documented per-chunk truth record carried INSIDE the existing schema-free provenance
  JSON blob: {chunk_id, verdict_ordinal:u8 (0=false..4=true, with an explicit NEI/unverified
  sentinel), rating_value:u16, best_rating:u16, source_id:u16, label_dict_id:u8}, plus a
  once-stored source_label<->ordinal mapping table. No new section id, no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump
  - provenance (0x05) already decodes arbitrary JSON and is threaded from Python untouched.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections/provenance.rs (document the convention only; encoder already
            accepts any serde_json::Value) and crates/nest-python/src/build_fn.rs (provenance dict
            already passed through, lines 89-96)
    impact: high
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/jghm-f/FACTCK.BR, https://github.com/sfu-discourse-lab/MisInfoText,
            https://github.com/yuxiaw/Factcheck-GPT

* Add an optional post-rerank verdict facet/predicate to the search path: after the mandatory
  exact cosine rerank, filter or boost hits by verdict_ordinal (e.g. return only
  verdict<=mostly-false). Must run STRICTLY after rerank so the returned score stays real cosine
  and the search contract is untouched; verdict comes from the decoded provenance map, an O(1)
  enum compare per hit.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs materialize_hits (lines 229-256) - add an optional predicate
            over a per-index verdict slice loaded at open time; surface as a flag in
            crates/nest-cli/src/cmd/search.rs and search_ann.rs
    impact: medium
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/sfu-discourse-lab/MisInfoText, https://github.com/yuxiaw/OpenFactCheck

* Extend the cite subcommand to print the truth record + normalized verdict + evidence citations
  alongside the byte span, so nest://content_hash/chunk_id returns BOTH the exact span AND its
  agency verdict deterministically. Add a parallel `verify` alias if cite's output gets crowded.
    where:  crates/nest-cli/src/cmd/cite.rs (after line 60, decode provenance and print verdict fields;
            already decodes chunk_ids/canonical/spans)
    impact: medium
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/jghm-f/FACTCK.BR, https://github.com/yuxiaw/Factcheck-GPT

* Add a pure-Python ingester layer that maps fact-check sources into the truth-record provenance
  shape: ClaimReview JSON-LD (ratingValue/bestRating -> normalized [0,1]), FakeRecogna XLSX
  (8-col), Fake.br/FakeTrue.Br (25-field stylometry + length-normalized pairs), FACTCK.BR TSV.
  Computes verdict_ordinal + normalized score deterministically at build time; no model, no
  network - fully offline.
    where:  python/ demo-corpus builder (the PT-BR ingestion scripts) feeding the provenance kwarg of
            nest_python build()
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/jghm-f/FACTCK.BR, https://github.com/Gabriel-Lino-Garcia/FakeRecogna,
            https://github.com/roneysco/Fake.br-Corpus,
            https://github.com/viniciuszani/portuguese-fake-news-classifiers

* Build-time near-duplicate dedup pass: before embedding, cluster chunks by cosine + agglomerative
  threshold (reuse the HnswIndex neighbor graph to avoid UglyFeed's O(n^2) dense-matrix blowup),
  keep one canonical chunk per cluster, record collapsed members + cohesion score as provenance.
  Fewer chunks = fewer embeddings = the dominant byte cost removed at source. Deterministic if
  linkage + sorted tie-break are fixed (matches reproducible-build rule).
    where:  python/ build pipeline as an optional pre-pass before nest_python build(); cohesion metadata into
            provenance. Reuses crates/nest-runtime ann::HnswIndex for neighbor candidates instead
            of a dense matrix
    impact: high
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/fabriziosalmi/UglyFeed, https://github.com/KaiDMML/FakeNewsNet

* Deterministic size-normalization corpus transform for the demo: for each aligned true/fake pair,
  word-count-truncate the longer doc to the shorter's length before chunking. Legitimately shrinks
  demo bytes + embedding count and debiases length-as-label leakage; reproducible because nest
  builds are byte-deterministic given identical chunks.
    where:  python/ demo-corpus prep (before chunking, feeds build()); document as the canonical PT-BR demo
            transform
    impact: low
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/roneysco/Fake.br-Corpus,
            https://github.com/viniciuszani/portuguese-fake-news-classifiers

* ROADMAP ONLY (reserved section id 0x09+, no v1 bump): two-plane graph topology section for
  propagation/evidence graphs - integer COO edge list (delta+varint+zstd) + a node->graph
  partition index, stored separately from float features which stay on the existing embedding
  path. This is the concrete data layout for the mission's planned graph search and multi-hop
  evidence chains. Out of scope for v1; only the additive section-id reservation is in play now.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/layout/mod.rs (reserve a SECTION_GRAPH_EDGES = 0x09 constant +
            OPTIONAL_SECTIONS entry, additive, excluded from content_hash); future
            crates/nest-runtime graph module
    impact: medium
    effort: high
    from:   safe-graph/GNN-FakeNews, https://github.com/KaiDMML/FakeNewsNet,
            https://github.com/Cartus/Automated-Fact-Checking-Resources

* ROADMAP ONLY: a structured-query / symbolic verification path beside cosine/hnsw/bm25 - a small
  Rust enum of TabFact-style typed ops (filter_eq, greater, count, argmax, within, before/after)
  over a future tabular/parquet section, with a trigger-word->op-id dictionary and memoized seeded
  beam search. The boolean program output is a real, citable verdict, turning retrieval into
  explainable verification. Split ops/exec/search files to stay <=300 loc, typed thiserror, no
  panic. Large subsystem - defer past v1 core.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime new structured-query module (ops.rs/exec.rs/search.rs); op-code table +
            trigger-word map as an additive section 0x0A+ in nest-format
    impact: medium
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/wenhuchen/Table-Fact-Checking

* ROADMAP ONLY: a dehydrated/manifest mode where a .nest stores stable external source refs (id +
  url) instead of inlining heavy payloads, deterministically re-hydrating chunks on demand.
  Combined with content_hash dedup-by-reference, this is the lever for 'compress whole
  datasets/repos more than everyone' without bloating the container or touching the embedding hot
  path. Needs an additive RESERVED section for source refs.
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/layout/mod.rs (reserve a source-refs section id) + python builder hydration
            logic; sits beside the dedup pass
    impact: medium
    effort: high
    from:   https://github.com/KaiDMML/FakeNewsNet

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Low-cardinality verdict/category enums are textbook dictionary-coding wins: store the verbose
    source label string once in a manifest mapping table, then represent each chunk's truth value
    as a 3-bit/u8 normalized ordinal + small source_id, bit-packed and zstd'd on the existing
    non-embedding path. 'Pants on Fire!' -> ~3 bits, losslessly recoverable. Class (2 values),
    Category (6 values), ~10 agencies all collapse to near-zero bytes (MisInfoText, FACTCK.BR,
    FakeRecogna).
  - Build-time near-duplicate dedup (agglomerative/ANN-neighbor clustering) attacks corpus
    CARDINALITY - fewer chunks means fewer per-chunk f32/f16/int8 embedding vectors, which are the
    single largest byte cost. This is orthogonal to and multiplicative with the per-section codecs
    (zstd/f16/int8), and is the highest-leverage compression idea in the category (UglyFeed,
    FakeNewsNet).
  - Dedup-by-reference: deduplicate identical canonical spans before zstd and emit pointer indices
    in chunk_ids, storing each duplicate span once - exactly the same principle as nest's
    content_hash dedup, applied at chunk granularity (FakeNewsNet).
  - ClaimReview ratingValue/bestRating as two u16 LE + the derived [0,1] score stored implicitly
    via the mapping table: a compact domain-specific encoding that replaces free-text rating
    labels (FACTCK.BR).
  - Two-plane integer-vs-float split for graph topology: COO edge lists are sorted, low-entropy id
    streams that compress brutally with delta+varint+zstd, kept on the non-embedding path while
    float features stay raw-mmap on the hot path. Right codec for the right statistics
    (GNN-FakeNews).
  - Deterministic size-normalization (truncate longer pair member to shorter's word count)
    legitimately reduces demo-corpus bytes AND embedding count while debiasing length-as-label,
    without touching the FROZEN format (Fake.br).
  - Closed operator vocabulary + trigger-word dictionary (if the symbolic-query path is ever
    built) is a tiny fixed symbol table that zstd-compresses near-perfectly; command-string-hash
    memoization is structural subexpression dedup, the same dedup principle as content_hash
    (TabFact).

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Most extracts reflexively propose a NEW section id (0x09+) for truth metadata. This is
    unnecessary and risks scope creep: provenance (0x05) is already a schema-free per-build JSON
    blob (provenance.rs only enforces 'parses as JSON') threaded straight from Python (build_fn.rs
    lines 89-96). Carry verdict records THERE for v1 - no new section, no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION
    bump. Reserve 0x09+ only for genuine graph topology / structured-op tables, which are roadmap.
  - FactCheck-GPT, OpenFactCheck and FactCheck-GPT's retrieval are online/LLM-bound (GPT-3.5 per
    claim, Google/Serper search per claim). nest is offline-by-construction and NEVER opens a
    socket - adopt only the static data SCHEMA, computed once at build time; never replicate the
    live retrieval/LLM calls. The 'BM25-over-offline-Wikipedia' tier merely validates nest's
    existing bm25_index(0x08).
  - Per-chunk verdict provenance must NOT live in the canonical six sections that feed
    content_hash, or every label re-annotation would invalidate all citations. Carry it in the
    schema-free provenance blob (which IS one of the canonical six but whose meaning is unchanged)
    being careful that adding fields stays byte-stable and deterministic, OR in a future OPTIONAL
    section explicitly excluded from content_hash. Confirm whichever path keeps citations stable
    across re-labeling.
  - Any verdict facet/filter MUST run strictly AFTER the mandatory exact cosine rerank in
    materialize_hits - the returned score must remain real cosine, never a verdict-weighted proxy.
    Do not let a 'boost by verdict' knob leak into the score the search contract guarantees.
  - UglyFeed's clustering is AGPL-3.0 (copyleft) and materializes a dense O(n^2) cosine/distance
    matrix that walls at a few thousand docs - reimplement the algorithm cleanly (do not vendor)
    AND swap the dense matrix for nest's existing HnswIndex neighbor graph to avoid quadratic
    blowup on whole-database corpora.
  - Dataset LICENSING is the real ship-blocker: FEVER/FEVEROUS/TabFact are Wikipedia-derived CC
    BY-SA (share-alike); UPFD (GNN-FakeNews) and FakeNewsNet payloads are academic-only / not
    redistributable (Twitter ToS); FakeRecogna, Fake.br, MisInfoText have no clear OSS license.
    Vendor the loader/schema PATTERNS, not the raw corpora, and license-vet every dataset
    individually before shipping it in a .nest demo container. FACTCK.BR (MIT),
    Factcheck-GPT/OpenFactCheck (Apache-2.0), Automated-Fact-Checking-Resources (MIT) are the safe
    schema sources.
  - The bm25 tokenizer is word-segmented (confirmed in bm25/tokenize.rs: splits on
    non-alphanumerics) and degrades on CJK/Thai - fine for the PT-BR demo (it handles ã/ç/õ via
    char iteration) but do not over-invest a multilingual fact-check narrative on top of it
    without addressing the known tokenizer gap first.
  - The TabFact symbolic DSL and the graph-topology section are genuinely valuable but are LARGE
    new subsystems (multiple Rust files, new section ids, beam-search engine). They conflict with
    the small-core / <=300-loc-per-file / minimal-budget reality of a solo author - keep them
    firmly roadmap-only and ship the additive provenance truth-feature + dedup wins first.


##################################################################################################
# bindings-cli  -  language bindings, CLI ergonomics, distribution/client surfaces
##################################################################################################

summary:     This category is about nest's client/distribution surface (nest-python pyo3 bridge, nest-cli clap
             surface) and how vector data crosses language boundaries. None of these repos
             contains an entropy coder, quantizer, or on-disk codec, so their direct compression
             contribution is zero; their value is to the "portable, cheap, light,
             install-anywhere, allocation-free-at-the-boundary" pillars of the north star. The
             collective state of the art has four pillars. (1) PyO3 stable-ABI distribution: one
             cp312-abi3 wheel per OS/arch loads on every CPython >= 3.12 (and via abi3t/PEP-803 on
             free-threaded builds too), decoupling the binary from the interpreter patch version;
             paired with zero-copy buffer-protocol borrows (PyBuffer<f32>/PyReadonlyArray1) so a
             numpy query array is read in place as &[f32] instead of marshalled
             element-by-element. (2) Weaviate's tag-then-pack vector wire discipline: a vector is
             ONE contiguous little-endian float32 bytes blob plus a tiny side-enum
             (SINGLE/MULTI/quantized) - never `repeated float` proto framing (~5 bytes/elt) - with
             element count recovered from byte-length (len//4) rather than stored, exactly
             mirroring nest's mmap-contiguous, scored-directly embeddings layout and confirming
             the win for vectors is layout+quantization, not entropy coding. (3) clap's "the
             binary is the source of truth": Jaro did_you_mean typo correction on the error
             branch, and a re-exec dynamic-completion protocol (CompleteEnv) that can read the
             actual file for candidate completion, plus feature-gated crate split for minimal
             binary size. (4) Weaviate's search-semantics knobs (minimum_should_match / OR
             operator, relativeScoreFusion min-max normalized weighted sum) and Helm's
             uniform-config-record + deterministic-fold pattern for declarative, reproducible
             build profiles. Critically, I verified against nest's source:
             nest_file.rs:22-24/35-37/54-56 do query.extract::<Vec<f32>>() (a full heap
             alloc+memcpy per search), every nest-runtime search fn (search.rs:124/147/181)
             already takes &[f32], the search is read-only over an immutable mmap (so
             Python::detach is sound and deterministic), nest-python is on pyo3 0.28 + abi3-py312
             + cdylib (baseline correct), there is NO maturin/pyproject build path (pyproject.toml
             explicitly disclaims publishing), and nest-cli uses clap v4 with only the `derive`
             feature (no `suggestions`/`unicode`). So the steals are concrete upgrades, not
             green-field adoption.

state of the art
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Four converging pillars. ABI/DISTRIBUTION (PyO3): the stable ABI is a *build feature* not a source
macro - opting into abi3-pyXY makes the linker emit only Py_LIMITED_API symbols so one .so loads
on every CPython >= floor; PEP-803 abi3t extends this to free-threaded builds with an
opaque-PyObject variant forward-compatible to all future 3.X; maturin produces the single
cp312-abi3 manylinux/macos/windows wheel. ZERO-COPY FFI (PyO3): Bound<'py,T> = (Py<T> handle,
Python<'py> GIL token) drops the refcount on Drop; the buffer protocol via PyBuffer<f32> /
rust-numpy PyReadonlyArray1 borrows a contiguous backing store as &[f32] with no copy;
Python::detach releases the GIL around a pure-Rust compute so concurrent threads run. VECTOR WIRE
LAYOUT (Weaviate py/go clients): tag-then-pack - one struct.pack contiguous little-endian f32
bytes blob + a side VectorType enum carrying dtype/layout out-of-band, count derived from len//4,
additive room for int8/fp16 tags without touching the byte run; this is the wire twin of
mmap-and-score-directly and explicitly avoids protobuf's ~5 bytes/float repeated-field overhead.
CLI ERGONOMICS (clap): the binary is the runtime source of truth for valid inputs - strsim::jaro
did_you_mean at 0.7 cutoff on the error path, CompleteEnv re-exec completion that can interrogate
real data, CompletionCandidate decoupling generation from per-shell rendering, feature-gated crate
split for binary-size minimization. SEARCH SEMANTICS + CONFIG (Weaviate + Helm):
minimum_should_match/OR BM25 operator and relativeScoreFusion (per-result-set min-max normalize
then weighted sum) as deterministic alternatives/additions to reciprocal-rank fusion, both feeding
a single rerank; and the uniform-config-record schema folded deterministically into a feature
manifest (Helm's ENABLE_MODULES fold, version-pinning-by-tag for reproducibility, static pinned
distribution channel).

top transferable innovations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. PyO3 buffer-protocol zero-copy borrow (PyBuffer<f32>/PyReadonlyArray1): read the numpy query
   array's backing memory in place as &[f32], eliminating the per-search Vec<f32> alloc+memcpy
   that nest_file.rs currently pays before the SIMD kernel - highest value, smallest surface, hot
   path.
2. abi3 + abi3t single-wheel distribution via maturin: one cp312-abi3 wheel runs on
   3.12/3.13/3.14+ and (with abi3t) free-threaded builds, directly closing nest's listed
   PyPI/maturin gap and delivering the 'install-anywhere' leg of the north star for the
   python-builds half.
3. Weaviate tag-then-pack vector layout (contiguous LE-f32 bytes + 1-byte dtype side-enum, count =
   len//4): validates nest's mmap-contiguous embeddings choice and gives the discriminator-tag
   pattern that lets nest swap f32->f16->int8 per named-vector with zero per-element decode and no
   NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump.
4. Python::detach (release GIL) around the read-only mmap search: lets concurrent Python threads
   issue parallel deterministic searches against one open NestFile, since the file is immutable
   post-build - free throughput with no correctness/determinism cost.
5. clap Jaro did_you_mean (suggestions feature, deterministic 0.7 cutoff): typo correction over
   the 8 subcommand names AND extensible over real container chunk_ids so a missed `nest cite`
   suggests the nearest valid id, on the error branch only (zero hot-path cost).
6. clap CompleteEnv re-exec dynamic completion that reads the actual .nest file for
   content_hash/chunk_id/preset completion - offline, single-file, but MUST stay in pure-rust
   nest-cli/nest-runtime so the ~300-500ms python boot never lands on the completion path.
7. Weaviate search-semantics knobs: minimum_should_match/OR BM25 operator (alphabetical term order
   = reproducible) and relativeScoreFusion as a typed alternative to reciprocal-rank fusion, both
   feeding the SAME mandatory exact-cosine rerank so ground-truth scoring is unchanged.
8. Helm uniform-config-record + deterministic-fold: a declarative build profile (TOML) where each
   section/codec is a {enabled, encoding, params} record folded into the writer's section table,
   making 'add hnsw_index(0x07)/bm25_index(0x08)' one boolean and keeping reproducible builds
   honest via version-pinning-by-tag (model fingerprint + codec versions).
9. clap feature-gated crate split: make hnsw/bm25/pyo3 opt-in cargo features so the smallest
   sovereign binary ships only what a given deployment needs.
10. Count-from-byte-length trick (len//(dim*elt_size)): derive element/row counts from the section
   byte length instead of storing a redundant count, shaving header bytes - a micro-win consistent
   with nest's compress-more discipline.

nest recommendations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Replace query.extract::<Vec<f32>>() with a zero-copy PyBuffer<f32> borrow, passing the borrowed
  &[f32] straight to nest-runtime's search/search_ann/search_hybrid (all already take &[f32]). Use
  pure-pyo3 PyBuffer (abi3 buffer API available since 3.11, so the py312 floor is fine) - no numpy
  build dependency. Keep the Vec<f32> extract as a fallback for non-buffer inputs (plain Python
  lists) so the API stays ergonomic.
    where:  crates/nest-python/src/nest_file.rs lines 22-24, 35-37, 54-56 (the three .extract() sites); calls
            into nest-runtime/src/search.rs:124/147/181 unchanged.
    impact: Eliminates one heap alloc + element-wise memcpy per search call end-to-end; combined with the
            already zero-copy mmap read of stored embeddings, the hot path becomes allocation-free
            from numpy query in to SIMD dot-product out. Ensures int8/f16/zstd compression wins
            are not paid back as marshalling overhead at the python boundary.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/PyO3/pyo3

* Wire maturin + a real [build-system] pyproject.toml (or maturin's own config) to produce ONE
  cp312-abi3 wheel for manylinux/macos/windows that loads on 3.12/3.13/3.14+, published to PyPI.
  Add the abi3t (PEP-803) feature path so the same wheel also loads on free-threaded CPython. Pin
  the model fingerprint + codec versions in the wheel/profile metadata so same-input =>
  byte-identical-file is auditable post-install.
    where:  New maturin build-system block replacing the current pyproject.toml disclaimer (lines 1-10);
            crates/nest-python/Cargo.toml features (add abi3t alongside abi3-py312); CI matrix for
            the three OS wheels.
    impact: Closes the listed PyPI/maturin known gap; delivers the 'portable, single-file, install-anywhere'
            leg of the north star for the python-builds half with one tiny artifact per OS instead
            of per interpreter patch version. Keeps distribution surface minimal (cheap/light
            pillar).
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/PyO3/pyo3, weaviate/weaviate-helm

* Wrap the pure-Rust nest-runtime compute inside Python::detach (release the GIL) in
  NestFile::search/search_ann/search_hybrid, after the query buffer is bound. Safe because the
  file is an immutable mmap post-build (read-only), so concurrent Python threads can issue
  parallel searches against one open NestFile with identical deterministic results.
    where:  crates/nest-python/src/nest_file.rs search methods, around the self.rt.search* calls (after the
            PyBuffer borrow is materialized into an owned slice or held across detach via the
            buffer's lifetime discipline).
    impact: Free concurrency for multi-threaded Python callers with zero determinism cost; complements the
            abi3t free-threaded wheel. No new sockets, no panic surface.
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/PyO3/pyo3

* Enable clap's `suggestions` feature and add Jaro did_you_mean over the 8 subcommand names; then
  extend the same deterministic Jaro scorer over real chunk_ids (mmapped via nest-runtime) so a
  missed `nest cite nest://hash/<typo>` suggests the nearest valid chunk_id without altering the
  frozen format. Runs only on the error branch.
    where:  crates/nest-cli/Cargo.toml (add `suggestions` to clap features at workspace Cargo.toml:18);
            crates/nest-cli/src/cmd/cite.rs and main.rs error handling for the chunk_id
            suggestion.
    impact: Better CLI ergonomics (clean pillar) with zero hot-path cost; chunk_id suggestion improves
            citation UX against opaque hash-addressed ids. Deterministic (sorted by similarity).
    effort: low
    from:   https://github.com/clap-rs/clap

* Adopt clap CompleteEnv-style re-exec dynamic shell completion that reads the actual .nest file
  to complete content_hash, chunk_id, and preset names - but implement the candidate generation
  ENTIRELY in pure-rust nest-cli/nest-runtime (mmap read), never invoking the python embedder, so
  the ~300-500ms python boot overhead never lands on the completion path.
    where:  New crates/nest-cli/src/cmd/complete.rs (or clap_complete integration in main.rs); reads via
            nest-runtime MmapNestFile for chunk_ids/content_hash; must stay under the <=300-line
            file cap.
    impact: Offline, single-file-aware tab completion that improves the portable/clean pillars; binary re-exec
            is cheap for the Rust CLI. Strictly avoids the python-boot hot path.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/clap-rs/clap

* When nest adds named/multi-vector embeddings (text + image per chunk), copy Weaviate's
  tag-then-pack layout exactly: a per-section 1-byte dtype/encoding-id discriminator (reusing
  nest's existing SECTION_ENCODING ids raw=0/zstd=1/float16=2/int8=3) plus a uint16/uint32 LE
  row-dim prefix, then a contiguous blob the runtime mmaps straight into the SIMD kernel; recover
  the vector count from byte-length (len/(dim*elt_size)) instead of storing a redundant count.
  Keep it additive (new section id 0x09+ reserved space; no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump).
    where:  crates/nest-format/src/sections + encoding/mod.rs (new named-vector section, reuse
            SECTION_ENCODING_* tags); crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs scoring path slices the
            mmap per named vector.
    impact: Lets nest swap f32->f16->int8 per named-vector by changing a 1-byte tag, never the byte layout -
            additive, reproducible, zero per-element decode. Shaves redundant count bytes from
            headers. Directly serves the compress-more axis without format churn.
    effort: medium
    from:   weaviate/weaviate-python-client, weaviate/weaviate-go-client

* Add two deterministic search-semantics knobs feeding the EXISTING mandatory exact-cosine rerank:
  (a) minimum_should_match / OR BM25 operator (alphabetical term order preserved => reproducible)
  and (b) relativeScoreFusion (per-result-set min-max normalize then weighted sum) as a typed
  alternative to reciprocal-rank fusion in the hybrid preset. Surface both as clap flags on
  search-text/hybrid.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/index.rs (min_should_match), crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs:181
            search_hybrid (fusion selection); crates/nest-cli/src/cmd/search_text.rs flags.
    impact: More recall/precision control in the hybrid path with ground-truth scoring unchanged (both still
            feed exact cosine rerank). Fully deterministic. Improves search quality without
            touching the frozen container.
    effort: medium
    from:   weaviate/weaviate-go-client, weaviate/weaviate-python-client

* Replace ad-hoc preset/build flags with a single declarative build profile (TOML) where each
  section/codec is a uniform record {enabled, encoding, params} mirroring presets
  exact/compressed/tiny/hybrid; a deterministic fold collects enabled records into the writer's
  section table (Helm's ENABLE_MODULES-style reduce in declaration order). Record exact model
  fingerprint + codec versions in the profile (version-pinning-by-tag) so same-input =>
  byte-identical-file is guaranteed and auditable.
    where:  crates/nest-python/src/build_fn.rs (build entrypoint) consuming the profile; crates/nest-format
            manifest as single-source-of-truth (each section a self-describing record carrying
            encoding id + per-section checksum).
    impact: Makes adding hnsw_index(0x07)/bm25_index(0x08) one boolean instead of imperative wiring; an
            additive-format-friendly config surface nest lacks today. Reinforces reproducible
            byte-identical builds and accelerates codec/preset experimentation (meta-win toward
            compress-more).
    effort: medium
    from:   weaviate/weaviate-helm

* Mirror clap's feature-gated crate split: make hnsw (ann), bm25, and pyo3 opt-in cargo features
  so a minimal sovereign nest-cli binary can ship without ANN/BM25/python when not needed.
    where:  crates/nest-runtime/Cargo.toml and nest-cli/Cargo.toml feature flags gating ann::HnswIndex /
            bm25::Bm25Index modules; main.rs subcommand registration behind cfg.
    impact: Smallest possible sovereign binary (light pillar); deployments that only need exact cosine over
            text drop the HNSW/BM25 code entirely. No effect on the frozen format.
    effort: medium
    from:   https://github.com/clap-rs/clap

* Stream the bulk-build path with a producer/consumer + auto-flush + error-threshold batch
  lifecycle (Weaviate's context-manager batch ergonomics) so large-corpus builds (the PT-BR
  fake-news datasets and future whole-DB ingests) chunk and embed without holding the entire
  corpus in memory.
    where:  crates/nest-python/src/build_fn.rs build pipeline; bounded queue feeding the embedder +
            nest-format writer, flush on completion/error.
    impact: Bounded-memory builds for the 'compress whole databases/repos' north-star goal; decouples
            chunk-production rate from write rate. Determinism preserved (ordered emission).
    effort: medium
    from:   weaviate/weaviate-python-client

compression wins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Count-from-byte-length (Weaviate): derive vector/row counts as len/(dim*elt_size) instead of
    storing a redundant count field per section - shaves header bytes on every
    embeddings/named-vector section, consistent with nest's compress-more discipline.
  - 1-byte dtype side-enum tag (Weaviate tag-then-pack): makes per-(named-)vector dtype
    fp32/fp16/int8 a single discriminator byte rather than a layout change, so nest can shrink an
    embeddings section by swapping codecs WITHOUT touching the byte run or bumping
    NEST_FORMAT_VERSION - keeps the format additive while the codec gets smaller (enables future
    per-vector int8 on the image side while text stays f16, etc.).
  - Zero-copy buffer-protocol borrow (PyO3): keeps the search hot path allocation-free end-to-end
    (mmap'd quantized/compressed embeddings in, borrowed query in), so the int8/f16/zstd
    compression wins in nest-format are NOT given back as alloc+memcpy marshalling overhead at the
    python boundary - protecting realized compression gains at serve time.
  - abi3/abi3t single-tiny-wheel distribution (PyO3): not a byte-codec, but the
    minimal-distribution-surface leg of 'compress more / be lighter than everyone' - one small
    artifact per OS serving every Python on every machine instead of N interpreter-specific
    binaries.
  - Validation that for vectors the win is layout + quantization, NOT entropy coding (Weaviate
    confirms `bytes`-not-`repeated-float` is the right call): reinforces nest's correct decision
    to keep embeddings as a raw contiguous mmap run scored directly (never zstd on embeddings) and
    to invest compression effort in better quantization (int8 today, candidates: per-vector scale
    int8, product/scalar quantization later) rather than entropy-coding the hot-path floats.

watch-outs (conflict with nest constraints)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - NEVER adopt any transport from the Weaviate clients: both are network clients (REST + gRPC
    over sockets, default :50051) - nest is offline-by-construction and must NOT open a socket.
    Steal ONLY the byte-layout (tag-then-pack), the count-from-length trick, the fusion math, and
    the batch ergonomics; leave gRPC/HTTP entirely.
  - clap dynamic completion (CompleteEnv) re-execs the binary - the ~300-500ms python boot
    overhead from search-text MUST never land on the completion path. Keep completion candidate
    generation in pure-rust nest-cli/nest-runtime (mmap reads only), never invoking
    python/embed_query.py.
  - abi3 has a small documented runtime cost vs version-specific builds (cannot inline against a
    known CPython struct layout). Acceptable for nest because the hot loop is pure-Rust
    nest-runtime, NOT across the FFI boundary - but do not move numeric/scoring work across the
    pyo3 boundary to 'use' the binding; PyO3 must stay confined to nest-python, with
    nest-format/nest-runtime pure Rust.
  - Keep the typed-thiserror -> PyValueError mapping intact when refactoring the query path:
    library code must never panic (HARD CONSTRAINT). A PyBuffer borrow that fails (non-contiguous,
    wrong dtype, wrong shape) must map to a typed PyValueError, not unwrap/expect.
  - relativeScoreFusion (min-max normalize) and minimum_should_match must remain fully
    deterministic to respect nest's reproducibility guarantee: preserve alphabetical BM25 term
    order and seeded behavior; min-max normalization is order-independent but verify tie-breaking
    is deterministic. Both MUST still feed the mandatory exact-cosine rerank - never return an
    ANN/BM25 proxy score.
  - Helm's value is meta only (declarative config + version-pinning), and the extract itself rates
    it low/none for compression - do not let the build-profile refactor balloon nest-cli or the
    manifest. Respect the <=300-line-per-file (333 hard cap), single-responsibility, edition-2024
    conventions; a config compiler can easily violate the line cap.
  - The named/multi-vector layout steal is FUTURE work (nest stores a single embeddings matrix
    today). When added it MUST use a RESERVED section id 0x09+ and the additive-only rule - do NOT
    bump NEST_FORMAT_VERSION unless an existing field changes meaning. Reuse existing
    SECTION_ENCODING ids; do not invent a parallel dtype tag scheme that diverges from the
    encoding ids.
  - maturin/PyPI introduces a network-fetch install step and an external supply chain - this is
    for distribution convenience and does NOT compromise runtime sovereignty (the runtime still
    mmaps a local file, no socket). But pin everything (model fingerprint, codec versions, wheel
    hashes) so the 'same chunks + same model fingerprint => byte-identical file' guarantee
    survives the install boundary; an unpinned dependency would silently break reproducibility
    (Helm's explicit warning).
  - PyReadonlyArray1 (rust-numpy) would add a numpy build dependency and a heavier dep tree;
    prefer pure-pyo3 PyBuffer<f32> to keep the small-core/light pillar. Only add the rust-numpy
    feature path as an OPTIONAL feature if a numpy-native ergonomic API is demanded.
  - Python::detach requires the borrowed query data to be safely held across the GIL release;
    ensure the buffer's backing memory cannot be mutated/freed by Python during the detached
    compute (copy into an owned Vec before detach if the buffer's lifetime cannot be guaranteed) -
    getting this wrong is a use-after-free, the one place the zero-copy win must be traded for
    safety.
